## कथा उदयपुर (Jagta Hua Kasba) — Hindi original (JAGTA-HI, 63)
जैसे ही हम एक बुर्ज से बाईं तरफ मुड़े, हमें सीध में दूसरा बुर्ज नजर आया। इस बुर्ज के सामने उदयपुर की मौजूदा ठसाठस बस्ती शुरू होती है। लेकिन इन दो बुर्जों के बीच हमें कुछ ऐसा दिखाई दिया, जो बिल्कुल ही अलग और ध्यान खींचने वाला था। दो बुर्जों के बीच की पत्थर की पुरानी करीब बीस फुट ऊंची राजमहल की सुरक्षा दीवार बीच से टूटी हुई थी। सीमेंट का एक लंबा और ऊंचा फ्रेम बनाया गया था। इस पर लोहे का बड़ा सा फाटक लगा था। दीवार पर एक साइन बोर्ड इसके निजी संपत्ति होने की घोषणा कर रहा था, जिस पर खसरा और वार्ड नंबर के साथ कुछ फोन नंबर भी लिखे थे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* As soon as we turned left from one bastion, we saw another bastion straight ahead. In front of this bastion the present crowded settlement of Udaypur begins. But between these two bastions we saw something quite different and attention-grabbing. The old stone defence wall of the palace, about twenty feet high, between the two bastions was broken in the middle. A long, tall cement frame had been built, with a big iron gate on it. A signboard on the wall declared it private property, with khasra and ward numbers and some phone numbers.
C07-JAGTA-HI-001   p. 14   also Ch 11
वर्ना ऐसे मंदिर गांव-गांव में हैं, लेकिन चौकोर दुर्ग से सुरक्षित नगर के भीतर एक विशाल चारदीवारी और बुर्जों से घिरे राजमहल की रूपरेखा इस इलाके में सिर्फ वहीं हैं। यह अवश्य ही उदयादित्य के पहले से बसी एक बस्ती रही होगी, जहां परमारों की सैन्य टुकड़ी सीमाओं की सुरक्षा के लिए तैनात होगी। उदयादित्य की नियुक्ति ने इस बस्ती का भाग्य जगा दिया था, जहां अब कुछ ऐसा होने वाला था, जो सदियों बाद भी स्मृतियों में जीवित रहेगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Otherwise such temples are in village after village, but a palace surrounded by a vast enclosure wall and bastions inside a town protected by a square fort has its outline only here in this region. This must certainly have been a settlement established before Udayāditya, where a Paramāra military contingent would be posted to guard the borders. Udayāditya's appointment awakened the fortunes of this settlement.
C07-JAGTA-HI-002   p. 18   also Ch 4
क्या मजबूत पत्थरों से बनी ऊंची चारदीवारी और बुर्जों से घिरे किसी एक हजार साल पुराने राजमहल का कोई हिस्सा किसी की निजी संपत्ति हो सकता है, जिसकी एक दीवार को तोड़कर सीमेंट और लोहे का दरवाजा लगा दिया गया हो और कोई इस तरफ ऐसी-वैसी निगाह न देखे इसके लिए पुरानी दीवार पर एक साइन बोर्ड टांग दिया गया हो- 'निजी संपत्ति, उदयपुर पैलेस, खसरा नंबर-822, वार्ड नंबर-141'
*Gloss [my translation]:* Can any part of a thousand-year-old palace surrounded by a high enclosure wall of strong stones and bastions be someone's private property, one of whose walls has been broken to fit a cement and iron door, with a signboard hung on the old wall: 'Private property, Udaypur Palace, Khasra No. 822, Ward No. 141'.
C07-JAGTA-HI-003   p. 18
परमारों के समय उदयपुर की पहाड़ी पर भी किले की सुरक्षा दीवार बनाई गई थी, जो आज भी सामने और गूगल मैप पर दिखाई देती है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* In the time of the Paramāras a fort defence wall was also built on the hill of Udaypur, which is visible today in front of us and on Google Maps.
C07-JAGTA-HI-004   p. 23
चारों ओर दुर्ग की प्राचीरों से घिरे इस नगर की शोभा में चार चांद लगने वाले थे। दुर्ग की पश्चिम की दीवार के नीचे एक विशाल जलाशय बन रहा था। वह यहां से गुजरने वाले सैन्य और व्यवसायी कार्फलों के साथ कृषकों और श्रद्धालुओं के लिए जल की आपूर्ति करेगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The beauty of this town, surrounded by the fort's ramparts, was to be enhanced. Below the fort's west wall a vast reservoir was being built, supplying water to passing military and merchant caravans, farmers and devotees.
C07-JAGTA-HI-005   p. 28   also Ch 2
पहाड़ी रास्तों में किले की प्राचीरों के अवशेष आपसे गुस्सा नजर आएंगे, क्योंकि उन्हें देखने कोई नहीं आता। उनके पास सदियों की गवाही है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* On the hill paths, remains of the fort ramparts look angry because no one comes to see them. They hold centuries of testimony.
C07-JAGTA-HI-006   p. 39
जब आप यह सब देखते हुए गलियों में घूमेंगे तो कई जगह पुराने पत्थर के विशाल दरवाजों से होकर गुजरेंगे। परमारों के व्यवस्थित उदयपुर में यह दरवाजे रास्तों पर लगाए गए थे। इनके आसपास कतार से वो दुकानें नजर आएंगी, जहां सदियों का मलबा भरा हुआ है। ये रौनकदार बाजार का हिस्सा रहे होंगे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Walking the lanes you pass through large old stone gateways. In the orderly Paramara Udaipur these gates were set across roads. Beside them are rows of shops filled with centuries of debris; they must have been part of a lively market.
C07-JAGTA-HI-007   p. 39   also Ch 8
मुगलों के हाथों हुए उस बड़े हत्याकांड की बुरी खबरें उदयपुर में चंदेरी दरवाजे से ही आई होंगी, जहां मैं कुछ बाद में गया। वह घेरा मेनरोड के समानांतर रही उदयपुर के किले की दीवार का एक दरवाजा है। वह उदयपुर में उत्तरदिशा की तरफ है। उस जमाने में जिस बड़े शहर की तरफ किसी शहर का कोई दरवाजा होता, वह उसी शहर के नाम होता।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Bad news of that great massacre must have come to Udaipur through the Chanderi gate. It is a gate of Udaipur's fort wall running parallel to the main road, on the north side. In that era a city's gate was named for the big city it faced.
C07-JAGTA-HI-008   p. 49   also Ch 10
आपातकाल में सुरक्षा के उद्देश्य से बनाई गई पहाड़ी किले की प्राचीर के पीछे हर तरफ से आकर छिपे हिंदू अपने घर-बार छोड़कर यहां कुछ देर की राहत पा गए थे। नीचे मंदिर के प्रांगण से उठ रहा भारी शोर वे यहां से देख रहे होंगे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Hindus who had come from all sides and hidden behind the hill fort's rampart, built for safety in emergencies, got brief relief there. They must have been watching from here the great noise rising from the temple courtyard below.
C07-JAGTA-HI-009   p. 106
हम उस चंदरी गेट पर भी गए जिसके बारे में पुरातत्व विभाग के ही एक अधिकारी ने बताया था कि कभी वहां एक पुराना मंदिर रहा होगा। मूर्तियों और स्तंभों के टुकड़े अब तक वहां लावारिस पड़े हैं। पांच लोग मुश्किल से नमाज पढ़ सकें इतना छोटा सा एक मस्जिदनुमा ढांचा वहीं गेट के बाहर बाद के दौर का है, जिसमें मंदिर का मलबा ही इस्तेमाल किया गया है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* We went to the Chanderi gate, about which an archaeology department officer had said an old temple must once have stood there. Pieces of images and pillars still lie orphaned. Just outside the gate is a tiny mosque-like structure of a later period, big enough for five people to pray, in which the temple debris itself is used.
C07-JAGTA-HI-010   p. 109
हम दोनों मंदिर में जाने की बजाए सबसे पहले एक ऐसी इमारत में गए, जिसे वहां बीजा मंडल कहते हैं। बाहर से दिखाई देने वाला यह गाँव के आम घरों की तरह एक रिहाइशी परिसर है, लेकिन भीतर जाकर ही समझ में आता है कि मूल इमारत कभी बहुत ही भव्य रही होगी। इसके एक तलघर नुमा कमरे का दरवाजा किसी पुराने मंदिर जैसा ही है। लगभग उतना ही पुराना, जितना पुराना मंदिर।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Instead of going into the temple we first went into a building that is called Bija Mandal there. From outside it is a residential complex like ordinary village houses, but only inside does one understand that the original building must once have been very grand. The door of one of its basement-like rooms is just like that of an old temple, about as old as the temple.
C07-JAGTA-HI-011   p. 11
अगर वह मंदिर था तो भीतर गर्भगृह से देवता नदारद थे और इस खाली जगह में भूसा भरा हुआ था। गर्भगृह के दोनों तरफ दो कमरे भी थे, उतने ही पुराने और तराशे हुए शानदार स्तंभों पर उनकी छत का वजन टिका हुआ था। छत पर बहुत बाद में कुछ रहने लायक कमरे बना लिए गए होंगे। पुराना और नया निर्माण साफ दिखाई देता है। नया भी नया नहीं है। चार-छह सौ साल पुराना ही होगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* If it was a temple, the deities were absent from the sanctum, and this empty space was filled with husk. On both sides of the sanctum there were two rooms, equally old, their roof's weight resting on splendid carved pillars. Some habitable rooms must have been built on the roof much later. Old and new construction is clearly visible. The new is not new either; it must be four to six hundred years old.
C07-JAGTA-HI-012   p. 11   also Ch 5
मूल शिव मंदिर के अलावा भी और पुराने कई मंदिर और बावड़ियां हैं, जो किसी चमत्कार से ही बच रहे हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Besides the original Shiva temple there are several other old temples and stepwells that survive only by a miracle.
C07-JAGTA-HI-013   p. 19   also Ch 11
इस पहाड़ को काटकर एलोरा की तरज पर किसी रॉक टेंपल की परियोजना के अवशेष भी हैं, जो अधूरी रह गई। कटी हुई पहाड़ी पर आज भी मूर्तियां दिखाई देती हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* There are also remains of a project of a rock temple, cut out of this hill on the lines of Ellora, which remained unfinished. Sculptures can still be seen on the cut hill.
C07-JAGTA-HI-014   p. 23   also Ch 5
आप किसी से जैन मंदिर परिसर का पता पूछिए। वहां निहथ्ये तीर्थंकर
दशकों से आपकी ही प्रतीक्षा कर रहे हैं, जिनकी जीवंत प्रतिमाओं पर किसी कालखंड
में प्रहार किए गए थे। उनकी खंडित प्रतिमाएं भी उतनी ही पुरानी हैं, जितना उदयपुर
का इतिहास।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Ask someone for the Jain temple complex. There the weaponless Tirthankaras have waited for you for decades, their images having been struck at some period. Their broken images are as old as the history of Udaipur.
C07-JAGTA-HI-015   p. 38   also Ch 8
सबसे पहले किसी से बीजामंडल का पता पूछिए। एक आवासीय परिसर की मंदिर
के समय की ही बनी एक ऐसी इमारत, जिसमें एक अधूरा शिलालेख, मंदिर जैसे ही
प्रवेश द्वार, स्तंभ और गर्भगृह हैं। यहां आपको मवेशी बंध हुए मिलेंगे और भूसा भरा
हुआ मिलेगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* First ask someone for the Bijamandal: a building in a residential complex, made in the time of the temple, with an incomplete inscription, a temple-like entrance, pillars and a sanctum. Here you will find cattle tied and fodder stored.
C07-JAGTA-HI-016   p. 38
उनकी खंडित प्रतिमाएं भी उतनी ही पुरानी हैं, जितना उदयपुर
का इतिहास। यह शिव के उपासकों की ही नगरी नहीं थी, यहां तीर्थंकरों के समूद्ध
अनुयायी भी रहे होंगे वरना इतनी भव्य मूर्तियां कौन बनवाता।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Their broken images are as old as Udaipur's history. This was not only a city of Shiva worshippers; there must have been many followers of the Tirthankaras, otherwise who would have commissioned such grand images?
C07-JAGTA-HI-017   p. 38   also Ch 8
पिसनहारी का एक सादा मंदिर कोई भी बता देगा, जो
बहुत ही सादगी से बनाया गया था। लेकिन इसके प्रवेश द्वार की शानदार नक्काशी
बेजोड़ है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Anyone can tell you of Pisanhari's plain temple, built very simply. But the splendid carving of its entrance is unmatched.
C07-JAGTA-HI-018   p. 38
मूल पर्वत से कटा हुआ एक शिवलिंग के आकार का टुकड़ा मिलों दूर से दिखता है। वह आपको बुला रहा है। यह परमारों के समय रॉक टेम्पल बनाने का एक बहुत अधूरा काम है, जो पूरा नहीं हो पाया था। उस कटी हुई पहाड़ी पर मूर्तियां तराशी जा रही थीं। फिर किसी वजह से यह प्रोजेक्ट बंद हो गया होगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* A piece cut from the original mountain, shaped like a Shivalinga, is visible from miles away. It is a very unfinished Paramara-period rock temple that was never completed. Images were being carved on that cut hill; the project must have stopped for some reason.
C07-JAGTA-HI-019   p. 39   also Ch 2
आप पहाड़ पर जाकर देखिए, आप पाएंगे कि एलोर के रॉक टेम्पल की तरह उदयपुर में पहाड़ी को काटकर रॉक टेम्पल बनाया जाने वाला था। लेकिन यह काम शुरूआती स्तर पर ही बंद हो गया था। चंद सबूत आज भी हैं। वह जिस राजा ने भी शुरू कराया हो मुझे लगता है कि वह अपने पूर्ववर्ती राजा उद्यादित्य को पीछे छोड़ना चाहता था। ठीक राजा नरवर्मन की तरह, जिन्होंने विदिशा में विजय मंदिर बनवाकर उदयपुर में अपने पिता के महान निर्माण कार्य से कई कदम आगे जाने की कोशिश की थी।
हो सकता है कि वह नरवर्मन के विजय मंदिर से भी चार कदम आगे का कुछ रचने वाला हो।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Go up the hill and you will find that, like the Ellora rock temple, a rock temple was to be made by cutting the hill at Udaipur. But the work stopped at an early stage. A few proofs remain today. Whichever king began it, I think he wanted to outdo his predecessor Udayaditya, just like King Naravarman who built Vijay Mandir in Vidisha trying to go several steps beyond his father's great construction at Udaipur. Perhaps he was about to create something even beyond Naravarman's Vijay temple.
C07-JAGTA-HI-020   p. 78   also Ch 2
आने वाला यह रॉक टेम्पल परमार राजवंश का एक बहुत महत्वाकांक्षी प्रोजेक्ट रहा होगा। मुमकिन है कि उस समय एलोरा तक के शिल्प और कला विशेषज्ञ यहाँ बुलाए गए हों। तब तक एलोरा में रॉक टेम्पल बनकर तैयार हो चुका था और उसकी प्रसिद्धि देश के भर के शिल्प विशेषज्ञों और शिल्प प्रेमी राजवंशों तक चली गई होगी। कौन जाने परमारों के किस प्रतिनिधि ने एलोरा के दक्ष शिल्पियों और वास्तुविदों को उदयपुर बुलाया हो। कुछ न कुछ ऐसा अवश्य हुआ होगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* This rock temple visible from afar on a high hill must have been a very ambitious project of the Paramara dynasty. It is possible that craft and art experts as far as Ellora were summoned here. By then the rock temple at Ellora was complete and its fame had gone to craft experts. Who knows which Paramara representative called Ellora's skilled craftsmen and architects to Udaipur. Something like this must have happened.
C07-JAGTA-HI-021   p. 79   also Ch 5
वे खंडित टुकड़े भी अपनी धूल धूसरित हो चुकी मुद्रा में उदयपुर के बचे रहे गए वैभव को फीका साबित करते हैं।
उदयपुर का रॉक टेम्पल इन दोनों को ही पीछे छोड़ने वाला था लेकिन यह प्रोजेक्ट बहुत आगे नहीं चल पाया।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Even those broken pieces make the surviving glory of Udaipur look faded. Udaipur's rock temple was to outdo both of these, but this project could not go much further.
C07-JAGTA-HI-022   p. 79
वह इतनी खूबसूरती से तराशा गया था कि लग रहा था जैसे कम्प्यूटर से डिजाइन बनाकर किसी कारखाने से ढलकर निकली धातु का कोई कारनामा है। अगर कम्प्यूटर पर तकनीकी करामात से पत्थरों के रंग पर धातु का रंग कर दिया जाए तो वह हूबहू मिसाइल जैसा ही लगे। मंदिर के सामने की एक संकरी गली में ऊपर चढ़कर मलबे और झाड़ियों से घिरे विशाल परिसर के एक कोने में दूर एक सात फुट ऊंचे प्लेटफॉर्म पर वह मंदिर था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* It was carved so beautifully that it looked like metalwork cast in a factory from a computer design. Up a narrow lane in front of the temple, in a corner of a vast complex surrounded by debris and bushes, the temple stood on a seven-foot-high platform.
C07-JAGTA-HI-023   p. 94
जैसे कोई मिसाइल प्रक्षेपित होने के लिए तैयार हो। हूबहू अंतरिक्ष की यात्रा के लिए तैयार कोई याना। सात फुट ऊंचे प्लेटफॉर्म पर यह बहुत ही संकरे डिजाइन का मंदिर है, जिसके द्वार पर गणेशजी की एक घिसी हुई आकृति है। यह इतना छोटा और पतला है कि मंदिर के भीतर मंदिर जैसा कम और किसी तपस्वी की ध्यान स्थली ज्यादा लगती है। कोई ऐसी जगह जिसे किसी सिद्ध साधक की साधना के लिए तैयार की गई हो। बाहरी डिजाइन ऐसा है जैसे कम्प्यूटर पर तैयार किया गया हो।
*Gloss [my translation]:* As if a missile were ready to be launched. Exactly like a craft ready for space travel. On a seven-foot-high platform this is a temple of a very narrow design, at whose door is a worn figure of Ganesha. It is so small and thin that inside it feels less like a temple and more like a meditation place of an ascetic. A place that seems prepared for the practice of an accomplished sadhaka. The outer design is as though prepared on a computer.
C07-JAGTA-HI-024   p. 95   also Ch 5
हमने मिसाइल मंदिर वाली नई खोज अपने नए मेहमानों के साथ ही जाकर देखने का निर्णय लिया, जो प्रवीण शर्मा पहले ही तस्वीरों में हमें भेज चुके थे। कंट्रीलो झाड़ियों की सुरक्षा में कैद उस मंदिर के ऊंचे जरूर प्लेटफॉर्म पर प्रयंकाजी जा चलीं और गर्भगृह में झांककर बचते-बचाते लौटीं। दीपकजी ने पूरे परिसर में बिखरे पत्थरों को हर तरफ से देखा। ये एक रौनकदार विशाल परिसर रहा होगा। कोई मठ या मंदिर या कोई कारोबारी प्रतिष्ठान।
*Gloss [my translation]:* We decided to go see, with our new guests, the new discovery of the 'missile temple', which Praveen Sharma had already sent us in photos. Priyanka went on to the high platform of that temple, imprisoned by thorny bushes, peered into the sanctum and returned cautiously. Deepak looked at the stones scattered over the whole complex from all sides. This must have been a splendid vast complex. Some math or temple or some commercial establishment.
C07-JAGTA-HI-025   p. 95
बीच बस्ती में मिसाइल की आकृति का यह मंदिर किसी भव्य परिसर के खंडहरों के बीच जैसे प्रतिक्षारत मौन में स्थिर था। घनी झाड़ियों से बचकर निकलते हुए आप जब उसके सामने पहुंचते हैं तो हैरत के सिवा कुछ नहीं होता। यह एक ऐसी जगह है, जिसके पास सुनाने के लिए अपनी अलग ही कहानी होगी। उदयादित्य से दूर किसी सदी का एक सुनहरा पन्ना।
*Gloss [my translation]:* In the middle of the settlement this missile-shaped temple stood still in waiting silence amid the ruins of some grand complex. Making your way past dense bushes, when you reach in front of it there is nothing but amazement. This is a place that must have its own different story to tell. A golden page of some century far from Udayaditya.
C07-JAGTA-HI-026   p. 95
प्रसिद्ध पुरातत्ववेत्ता नारायण व्यास ने इसकी तस्वीरें देखकर आकलन किया है कि यह 16 वीं शताब्दी का है। यानी उदयादित्य के बाद सदियों तक मंदिरों के निर्माण जारी थे, जबकि उदयपुर इस दौरान इस्लामी हमलावरों का शिकार हो चुका था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The renowned archaeologist Narayan Vyas, seeing its photographs, has assessed that it is of the 16th century. That is, temple construction continued for centuries after Udayaditya, while Udaypur had in this period fallen victim to Islamic invaders.
C07-JAGTA-HI-027   p. 96   also Ch 10
उसके आसपास पांच-सात समाधियां घरों से घिरी और लगभग लुप्त होती हुई हमने देखीं। वहां कई सती स्तंभ लगे थे, जो गायब होने की हालत में आ चुके थे। आसपास ज्यादातर आबादी मुस्लिम समाज की है। हो सकता है कि आने वाले सालों में वे स्तंभ इनकी सघन बसाहटों के बीच घिरकर खत्म कर दिए जाएं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Around it we saw five-seven samadhis hemmed in by houses and almost vanishing. Several sati pillars stood there, close to disappearing. Most of the surrounding population is Muslim. In coming years those pillars may be enclosed in their dense settlements and ended.
C07-JAGTA-HI-028   p. 109
लेकिन जल्दी ही यह दिखाई देना बंद हो जाएगा क्योंकि मंदिर तक पहुंचने की एकमात्र संकरी गली की एक इमारत में जामा मस्जिद का बोर्ड टंग गया है। इसकी छोटी मीनारों पर लोहे और प्लाईवुड की बड़े आकार की मीनारें टोपी की तरह पहना दी गई हैं। अब मंदिर नहीं यह मस्जिद दूर से दिखाई देना शुरू हो जाएगी।
*Gloss [my translation]:* But soon this will stop being visible because a Jama Masjid board has been hung on a building in the only narrow lane leading to the temple. Large iron and plywood minarets have been put like caps on its small minarets. Now not the temple but this mosque will begin to be visible from afar.
C07-JAGTA-HI-032   p. 23   also Ch 11
परमारों के स्पष्ट स्थापत्य की बेशकीमती गवाही मंदिर के पीछे थोड़ी ही दूर पर मौजूद खंडहर राजमहल परिसर का हर खंभा, हर चौखट और हर दरवाजा देता है। इसके एक हिस्से में बनी मस्जिद के स्तंभों के पत्थरों को टटोलकर देखिए। वे भी परमारों का पता बताते हैं। मस्जिद भी राजमहल के विशाल चौकोर परिसर में बनी है। यह मूलरूप से महल का ही हिस्सा रही होगी, लेकिन मुसलमानों के कब्जे के बाद दरबार जैसी बनावट वाली इस रचना को इबादतगाह में बदल दिया गया।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Every pillar, lintel and door of the ruined palace complex a little behind the temple is precious evidence of Paramara architecture. Feel the stones of the pillars of the mosque built in one part: they too point to the Paramaras. The mosque is also built within the palace's vast square complex. It must originally have been part of the palace, but after Muslim occupation this court-like structure was turned into a place of worship.
C07-JAGTA-HI-033   p. 51
पास ही एक टीले में तरोश हुए पत्थर झड़ियों और मिट्टी-मलबे से झांक रहे थे। यह कोई इमारत रही होगी, जो गिरकर ढेर हो गई। उससे सटकर एक पुराने खंडहर को दरगाह के रूप में रंगा-पोता गया था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Nearby, carved stones peeped out of bushes and rubble in a mound. It must have been a building that fell in a heap. Adjacent to it an old ruin had been painted and plastered as a dargah.
C07-JAGTA-HI-034   p. 70
दो में से एक मस्जिद को उदयपुर वाले बचपन से कचहरी कहते आए हैं, उनके बाप-दादों ने भी यही बताया था कि यह कचहरी है। लोगों को याद है कि पहले कभी कचहरी के नाम का एक साइन बोर्ड भी लगा रहा था। एक कचहरी का रूपंतरण मस्जिद में कब हो गया, कोई नहीं जानता। मस्जिद ही नहीं जामा मस्जिद। इसे बादशाह जहांगीर के नाम से याद किया जाता है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* One of the two mosques Udaypur people have called the 'kachahri' since childhood; their forefathers too had told them it is the kachahri. People remember that a signboard named kachahri was once also put up. When a kachahri was turned into a mosque, no one knows. Not just a mosque, the Jama Masjid. It is remembered by the name of Emperor Jahangir.
C07-JAGTA-HI-035   p. 101   also Ch 11
खास बात यह है कि यह कचहरी या मुस्लिम भाइयों की जामा मस्जिद साफ़तौर पर परमारों के समय के राजमहल का ही एक हिस्सा रही है, जो चार बुर्जों और बीस फुट ऊंची दीवारों से घिरे चार बीघा के लंबे-चौड़े प्रांगण में कभी महल के दाहिनी तरफ था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The special point is that this kachahri, or Jama Masjid of the Muslim brothers, has clearly been a part of the palace of Paramara times, which was once to the right of the palace in a spacious four-bigha courtyard surrounded by four bastions and twenty-foot-high walls.
C07-JAGTA-HI-036   p. 102   also Ch 10
दिल्ली के सुलतानों और बादशाहों के वज्र में आने के बाद मुस्लिम कालखंड की मस्जिदें उदयपुर की कहानी में कुछ नए पन्ने भी जोड़ती हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* After coming under the sway of the sultans and emperors of Delhi, mosques of the Muslim period add some new pages to Udaypur's story.
C07-JAGTA-HI-037   p. 152
उन पर उर्दू में खुदा के संदेश लिख दिए। क्या वह खुदा ईश्वर का शत्रु था ?
मंदिर के अवशेषों से मस्जिद बनाने का कार्य आरंभ हुआ। मस्जिद के पत्थरों के पीछे झांकिए तो कोई न कोई मूर्ति चस्पा होगी। मंदिर को ढंकने का मानचित्र बना। मंदिर परिसर में खड़े होकर देखने पर मस्जिद की आधी-अधूरी बनी दीवारें इसका साक्ष्य देती हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* They wrote messages of God on them in Urdu. The work of building a mosque from the remains of the temple began. Look behind the mosque's stones and some image will be stuck there. A plan was made to cover the temple. Standing in the temple complex, the half-finished walls of the mosque give evidence of this.
C07-JAGTA-HI-038   p. 161   also Ch 5
घटेरा रोड पर एक देवी मंदिर है, जहां आपको विदिशा के बीजामंडल जैसी हूबहू एक बावड़ी दिख जाएगी और पूरे परिसर में कई खंडित मूर्तियां। यहां हाल ही में एक टीले की खुदाई में एक और उससे पुरानी बावड़ी मिली है, जिसमें जमीन की सतह से नीचे तक ले जाने वाला 45 डिग्री का एक उतर दिखाई देगा। कभी यहां से सीधे नीचे बावड़ी तक लोग जाते होंगे। घोड़े इससे उतरकर नीचे तक जाते थे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* On Ghatera Road is a devi temple where you will see a stepwell exactly like the Bijamandal of Vidisha, and several broken images. A recent mound excavation found an older stepwell with a 45-degree ramp going below ground level. People must once have gone straight down; horses descended by it.
C07-JAGTA-HI-039   p. 39
ईंट भट्टे के पास एक पुरानी बावड़ी तक ले गए, जिसका डिज़ाइन आश्चर्यचकित करने वाला है। घुमावदार मजबूत पत्थरों की सीढ़ियों से नीचे तक जाया जा सकता था। वहां भी हमने एक शिलालेख देखा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* They took us to an old stepwell near a brick kiln whose design is astonishing. One could go down by strong spiral stone steps. There too we saw an inscription.
C07-JAGTA-HI-040   p. 70   also Ch 6
हम तीन शिलालेखों का एक एक्सक्लूसिव सेक्शन लेकर आए, जिससे राइम लाइन में उदयपुर की सदियों पुरानी अनुभव संपन्न यात्रा को समझा जा सके। हमने जलक्षेत्रों को अलग से फोकस किया। शानदार बावड़ियों की तस्वीरें गुजरात में प्रसिद्ध पाटण की रानी की बाव याद दिला रही थीं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* We brought an exclusive section of three inscriptions to understand Udaipur's centuries-old journey on a timeline. We focused on the water bodies separately. Pictures of the splendid stepwells reminded one of Patan's Rani ki Vav in Gujarat.
C07-JAGTA-HI-041   p. 78   also Ch 6
उदयपुर से प्रस्थान करने के पहले हम उस उदय समुद्र की तरफ गए, जहां एक हफ्ता पहले ही ईटैक की टीम काम कर चुकी थी और उसकी लंबाई 980 मीटर नापी थी। तराशे हुए पत्थरों के शानदार घाटों की सीढ़ियां अब उदय समुद्र में नहीं उन खेतों में उतरती हैं, जहां बीतते गर्माते हुए मार्च के महीने में गेहूं की फसल कटने के लिए तैयार खड़ी थी। वे अब दूर तक फैले उपजाऊ काली मिट्टी के खेत हैं, जहां कभी उदयादित्य का बनवाया हुआ विशाल तालाब समुद्र की तरह किनारों से टकराता था। ईटैक की टीम के काम को उस दिन गंज बासौदा की हैरिटेज टीम ने अपने कैमरों में उतारा था और नीचे दो-एक सीढ़ियों तक खुदाई करने में मदद की थी। जमीन की ऊपरी सतह पर ही एक-आध फुट खुदाई में ढेर सारे शंख-सीपियां और तांबे का एक सिक्का मिला था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Before leaving Udaipur we went to Uday Samudra, where the INTACH team had worked a week earlier and measured its length as 980 metres. The steps of the splendid carved-stone ghats now descend not into Uday Samudra but into fields where wheat stood ready for harvest, a vast tank built by Udayaditya once lapped the shores like a sea. Digging a foot or so on the top surface gave many shells and a copper coin.
C07-JAGTA-HI-042   p. 110   also Ch 2
बांध और जलाशय निर्माण परमारों की विशेषज्ञता थी। जहां भी विशाल मंदिर बने वहां और विशाल जलाशय बनाए गए। सूखे मौसम के आठ महीनों में काफिलों में यात्रा करने वाले मालवाहकों, कारोबारियों, साधुओं, तीर्थयात्रियों, सैनिक समूहों और उनके रथों और बैलगाड़ियों के हजारों मवेशियों के लिए यही जलाशय पड़ाव के समय काम आते थे। नगर की पानी की आपूर्ति तो होती ही थी। उदयपुर में उदय समुद्र के अलावा भी अनगिनत बावड़ियां बनवाई गई थीं, जहां परमारों के शिल्पियों ने अपने हुनर की जोरदार नुमाइश की है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Dam and reservoir building was a Paramara speciality. Wherever huge temples were built, even larger reservoirs were made. In the eight dry months they served as halting places for caravans of freight carriers, traders, sadhus, pilgrims, military groups and thousands of cattle. The town's water supply too. In Udaipur apart from Uday Samudra countless stepwells were built where Paramara craftsmen displayed their skill.
C07-JAGTA-HI-043   p. 110   also Ch 8, Ch 2
करीब तीन सौ मीटर लंबी इस गली के दोनों तरफ पत्थर से बनी दशकों पुरानी दुकानें हैं। कपड़े की, किराने की, मिठाई की। ये दुकानें उदयपुर में ही पाए जाने वाले पत्थरों की बनी हैं, जिनमें सीमेंट और ईंटों का इस्तेमाल न के बराबर था। कई पुराने और बंद मकानों में पुराने दौर के पत्थर के ही दरवाजे साफ दिखाई देते हैं। यहां सब कुछ पत्थर का ही था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* On both sides of this lane, about three hundred metres long, are decades-old shops built of stone: cloth, grocery, sweets. These shops are made of stones found in Udaipur itself, with almost no cement or bricks. In many old, closed houses stone doors of the old period are clearly visible. Everything here was of stone.
C07-JAGTA-HI-045   p. 9   also Ch 2
मंदिर के सबसे शानदार परिसर के दोनों तरफ परमार राजाओं के समय बनवाए हैरतअंगेज निर्माण हैं। भारी-भरकम तराशे हुए पत्थरों की जमावट से बने पुराने घर। संकरी गलियां भी कभी अच्छी खासी चौड़ी सीधी साफसुथरी सड़कें रही होंगी, जो परमारों के बाद नाजायज कब्जों के चलते सिकुड़कर तंग हो गईं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* On both sides of the temple's finest precinct are astonishing constructions made in the time of the Paramara kings. Old houses built by arranging massive carved stones. The narrow lanes too must once have been fairly wide, straight, clean roads, which after the Paramaras shrank and became narrow through unlawful occupation.
C07-JAGTA-HI-046   p. 10
हम उन गलियों में गए, जहां परमार राजाओं की खास स्थापत्य शैली में बने विशाल दरवाजे अब तक कायम हैं। दोनों तरफ के घर धीरे-धीरे प्राचीन दरवाजों से बहुत सटते गए हैं। इसलिए वे पुराने चौड़े और व्यवस्थित रास्ते संकरी गलियों में बदल चुके हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* We went into those lanes where huge doorways built in the Paramara kings' special architectural style still survive. Houses on both sides have gradually pressed very close to the ancient doorways. Therefore those old, wide, orderly roads have turned into narrow lanes.
C07-JAGTA-HI-047   p. 12   also Ch 5
धूल भरे पथरीले रास्तों के दोनों तरफ के खस्ताहाल वैभव को टटोलते हुए हम दोनों ही अंदाजा लगा रहे थे कि इस खंडहर के उजाड़ दरवाजों से पता चलता है कि यहां कोई कारोबारी परिसर रहे होंगे या कोई अति महत्वपूर्ण व्यक्ति का आवास रहा होगा। मुमकिन है कि राजकाज से संबंधित कोई कार्यालय रहे हों, जहां किसी दौर में दूर-दूर के लोगों की चहल-पहल रहती होगी। किसी समय यहां रथों, बैलगाड़ियों और घुड़सवारों का आवागमन बड़ी शान से होता रहा होगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Groping through the decayed splendour on both sides of the dusty, stony paths, we both guessed that these desolate doorways of the ruin show there must have been some commercial complexes or the residence of some very important person. It is possible they were offices connected with administration, where people from far came and went. At some time chariots, bullock-carts and horsemen must have moved here in great style.
C07-JAGTA-HI-048   p. 12
मैं कूद-फांदकर कुछ अंदर तक गया और एक ऐसे कमरे में जा पहुंचा, जो निश्चित रूप से परमारों के समय किसी अति महत्वपूर्ण व्यक्ति का निवास रहा होगा। दीवारों पर तराशे हुए पत्थरों की शानदार अलमारियां थीं, जिनमें रोजमर्रा के उपयोग का सामान रखा जाता होगा। किसी समय वे पत्थर की दीवारें, दरवाजे और अलमारियां अंदर चटख रंगों से रंगे गए होंगे। लेकिन अब फर्श बुरी तरह उखड़े पड़े थे, जहां आवारा लोग गंदगी फैलाने के लिए आजाद थे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* I jumped and climbed some way inside and reached a room that must certainly have been the residence of some very important person in the time of the Paramāras. The walls had splendid cupboards of carved stone, in which everyday articles must have been kept. At some time those stone walls, doors and cupboards must have been painted in bright colours. But now the floors were badly torn up, where vagrants were free to spread filth.
C07-JAGTA-HI-049   p. 13   also Ch 11
दोनों तरफ पत्थर के बिना पलस्तर वाले मकानों की संकरी गलियों और प्राचीन दरवाजों से घूमते हुए हम वापस मंदिर आए। रास्ते में हमने राजा उदयदित्य के समय के असल मानचित्र का खाका भी खींचा। तब ये रास्ते कैसे 90 डिग्री पर एक दूसरे को काटते हुए बनाए गए होंगे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Wandering through narrow lanes of unplastered stone houses and ancient gateways on both sides, we returned to the temple. On the way we also sketched an outline of the original plan from the time of King Udayāditya. How these roads must have been made, crossing each other at 90 degrees.
C07-JAGTA-HI-050   p. 15   also Ch 4
पूरा नगर अपने पुराने डिजाइन पर ऊबड़-खाबड़ हालत में मौजूद है, जहां परमार राजवंश के वास्तुशिल्प के अनुसार बने दीवार-दरवाजे, मंदिर, महल, तालाब, बावड़ी, कमरे, दीवारें और गलियां हैं। इनसे गुजरते हुए लगता है कि आप अतीत में लौटकर सदियों पहले के खोए हुए वैभव की सैर कर रहे हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The whole town exists in a rough condition on its old design, where there are walls, gateways, temples, palace, tanks, stepwells, rooms, walls and lanes built according to Paramāra dynasty architecture. Passing through them it feels as though you have returned to the past and are touring the lost splendour of centuries ago.
C07-JAGTA-HI-051   p. 18   also Ch 12
वे कह रहे हैं कि कल किसी ने गेट पर निजी संपत्ति का बोर्ड लगा दिया था, उसे हटा दिया गया है। यह साढ़े तीन बीघा में फैला राजमहल राजस्व विभाग के रिकॉर्ड के अनुसार किसी की निजी संपत्ति नहीं है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* He says someone hung a private-property board on the gate yesterday and it has been removed. This palace spread over three and a half bighas is, according to revenue department records, not anyone's private property.
C07-JAGTA-HI-052   p. 31   also Ch 11
उनके दरवाजे और दीवारों में बचे रह गए
आले परमारों के डिजाइन की शानदार नुमाइश करते हैं। गलियारों के स्तंभों के बीच
उठा दी गई पत्थर की दीवारें आपको साफ बताएंगी कि ये बाद के दौर के कब्जे हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The niches surviving in their doors and walls are a splendid display of Paramara design. Stone walls raised between the pillars of the corridors will tell you plainly that these are encroachments of a later period.
C07-JAGTA-HI-053   p. 38
कई पुराने मकान मलबे में बदल रहे हैं। किसी मलबे के ढेर से चढ़कर जब आप झोंकियों के भीतर झांकेंगे तो चौक, कमरे और बरामदे नजर आएंगे। जरूर इन बड़े घरों में कोई खास लोग रहते होंगे। वे कौन थे, कब उनके यह शानदार घर वीरानी के हवाले हो गए और आज इनका वारिस कौन है?
*Gloss [my translation]:* Many old houses are turning into debris. Climbing a heap and peering in, you see courtyards, rooms and verandas. Special people must have lived in these big houses. Who were they, when were these splendid homes abandoned, and who is the heir today?
C07-JAGTA-HI-054   p. 39
मंदिर के टूटे-फूटे अवशेष उनके घर के बाहर-भीतर हर कहीं नजर आ रहे थे। घर के आगे के एक संकरे पुराने कमरे में उनकी किराने की छोटी सी दुकान थी। प्रवीणजी हमें लेकर भीतर गए। भीतर के कमरों का नजारा दस सदियों पुराना था। वह बिल्कुल किसी मंदिर के गर्भगृह जैसा था, जिस पर शानदार मूर्तियों के तराशे हुए पत्थर जड़े हुए थे। भीतर कोई मूर्ति नहीं थी लेकिन आसपास दो कमरे और थे। यह बहुत ही पुराना निर्माण कार्य था। बाईं तरफ के अंधेरे कमरे के फर्श के पत्थर ठोकने पर ऐसी आवाजें कर रहे थे जैसे नीचे एक और कमरा हो।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Broken remains of the temple were visible everywhere in and outside his house. The rooms inside looked ten centuries old, like a temple sanctum set with carved stones of splendid sculptures. There was no image inside but two more rooms nearby; very old construction. The stones of the floor of the dark left room sounded as if another room lay beneath.
C07-JAGTA-HI-055   p. 68
यह घर कभी एक टीले में दफन था। वे आगे के हिस्से में रहते थे। रहने के लिए और ज्यादा जगह के लिए टीले की सफाई शुरू हुई तो शानदार तराश गए पत्थरों में निर्मित यह कमरे बने हुए मिले। इनमें काफी सारा कबाड़ा भरा हुआ था, जिसे किसी किसी कबाड़ी को बेचा गया था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* This house was once buried in a mound. They lived in the front part. When clearing the mound began for more living space, rooms built of splendidly carved stones were found. They were filled with a lot of junk, which was sold to a scrap dealer.
C07-JAGTA-HI-056   p. 69
बुंदेलखंड की सीमा पर उदयपुर उनके राज्य के सबसे व्यस्त व्यावसायिक केंद्रों में से एक था, जहां चारों तरफ दुर्ग से घिरे नगर में एक राजमहल, कई बावड़ियां, दरवाजे,
मंदिर, चौक-चबूतरे, कचहरी सदियों तक बनाए गए थे। गूगल मैप से पूरी नगर रचना को आज भी देखा-समझा जा सकता है, जो ऊपर पहाड़ी तक की गई किलेबंदी तक विस्तृत है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* In the fort-ringed town a palace, many stepwells, gates, temples, chowks and platforms, and a kachahari were built over centuries. The whole town plan can still be seen and understood on Google Map, extending to the fortification up to the hill.
C07-JAGTA-HI-057   p. 70–71   also Ch 8
क्योंकि उदयपुर की बसाहट में पुराने दौर की ही बस्तियां, इमारतें और कारोबारी परिसर रहे होंगे। गूगल मैप की मदद से मौजूदा मकानों के बगल में या पीछे ऐसी कई जगहें साफ नजर आती थीं, जो बेपरवाह वक्त की बेतरतीब हरियाली में गुम हैं। उनके बीच से झांकती पत्थर की संरचनाएं यह इशारा करती हैं कि वहां जरूर कुछ ढांचे रहे होंगे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Udaipur's settlement must have had old-period colonies, buildings and trading complexes. With Google Map many places beside or behind present houses were clearly visible, lost in untended greenery. Stone structures peeping from them hint that some structures must have been there.
C07-JAGTA-HI-058   p. 93
हमने तत्काल कदम आगे बढ़ाए और बीजामंडल के सामने घनी झड़ियों में ही जा घुसे। निकलने लायक जगह बनाते हुए सामने पत्थर की इमारतों तक गए। वह इमारतों की पिछली दीवारें थीं। ये इमारतें दूसरी तरफ सामने कहीं खुली होंगी। लेकिन उनके पीछे पत्थरों की जमावट से कई अलग-अलग कालखंडों की कहानी साफ देखी जा सकती थी।
*Gloss [my translation]:* We went into the dense bushes in front of Bijamandal and made our way to stone buildings, which were the back walls of buildings that must open elsewhere in front. The arrangement of stones behind them clearly showed the story of several different periods.
C07-JAGTA-HI-059   p. 93
एक राम मंदिर के पिछवाड़े के पत्थर सबसे पुराने थे, जिनके पास बड़े बाजार की पुरानी दुकानों की इमारतें शायद बाद में खड़ी की जाती रही होंगी। लेकिन ये सब भी पांच सौ साल से ज्यादा ही पुराने रहे होंगे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The stones at the back of a Ram temple were the oldest, near which the old shop buildings of the big market were probably raised later. But all these too must be more than five hundred years old.
C07-JAGTA-HI-060   p. 94
यह एक दरबारे-आम या दरबारे-खास जैसी एक संरचना है, जहां राजा या बादशाह अपने मंत्री, मेहमान या शहर के रसूखदार लोगों के साथ बैठते रहे होंगे। लोग अपनी समस्याएं और सूचनाएं लेकर उनके पास आते होंगे। वे अपने अभियंतों और वास्तुविदों के साथ बैठकर मंदिर के निर्माण की हर बारीकी समझते होंगे और
वितीय मामलों पर चर्चा करते होंगे। वे अगली मंज़रणा में उदय समुद्र के निर्माण के बारे में मार्गदर्शन लेने आए शिल्पियों के समूह को संबोधित करते होंगे और भोपाल में सभा मंडल के निर्माण की जानकारी देते होंगे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* This is a structure like a Darbar-e-Aam or Darbar-e-Khas, where the king or emperor must have sat with his ministers, guests or notables of the city. People must have come to them with their problems and information. They must have sat with their engineers and architects to understand every detail of temple construction and discussed financial matters. In the next audience he must have addressed the group of craftsmen who came for guidance on the construction of Uday Samudra and given information on the construction of the Sabha Mandal in Bhopal.
C07-JAGTA-HI-061   p. 101–102   also Ch 9
आज संकरी गलियों में गुम बौनक और बदहाल उदयपुर ने तब क्या दिन देखे होंगे। राजमहल के ध्वस्त होते खंडहर उस वैभवशाली समय की सबसे शानदार गवाही हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* One can only guess what days today's Udaypur, lost in narrow lanes and in poor condition, saw then. The crumbling ruins of the royal palace are the most splendid witness of that glorious time.
C07-JAGTA-HI-062   p. 126   also Ch 4
समूचे उदयपुर नगर क्षेत्र और आसपास पाँच किमी की परिधि में महत्वपूर्ण पुरातात्विक सम्पदा बिखरी हुई है। मन्दिर की पाँच सौ मीटर परिधि में ही बने हुए मकान प्राचीन पुरासामग्री से निर्मित हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Important archaeological wealth lies scattered over the whole Udaypur town area and within a radius of five km around. Houses built just within a five-hundred-metre radius of the temple are built of ancient archaeological material.
C07-JAGTA-HI-063   p. 166
उनकी टीम ने हजार साल पुराने तालाबों, बावड़ियों, रावण टोल में 27 फुट की चट्टान पर मौजूद नटराज शिव, पिसनहारी मंदिर, पहाड़ी की किलेबंदी, बीजामंडल, मलूकदास जैन का आवास, पुराने दरवाजे, बड़ा बाजार और सबसे महत्वपूर्ण राजमहल के खंडहर को हर कोने से जाकर देखा। उदयपुर को उन्होंने एक ऐसी जगह के रूप में पाया, जहां वाकई तत्काल बड़े पैमाने पर संरक्षण की जरूरत थी।
*Gloss [my translation]:* His team viewed from every corner the thousand-year-old tanks and stepwells, Nataraj Shiva on a 27-foot rock at Ravan Tol, the Pisanhari temple, the hill's fortification, Bijamandal, Malukdas Jain's residence, old gates, the Bada Bazaar and most importantly the palace ruins. They found Udaipur a place that really needed immediate large-scale conservation.
C07-JAGTA-HI-064   p. 87   also Ch 5, Ch 11
चार संरक्षित स्मारकों में हाई-तीन सौ साल पुरानी दो मस्जिदें भी राज्य सरकार के जिम्मे में हैं। बाकी दो में एक पिसनहारी का मंदिर और रावणटोल की चट्टान पर बनी नटराज शिव की 27 फुट लंबी मूर्ति है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Among the four protected monuments, two mosques, about three hundred years old, are also in the state government's charge. Of the remaining two, one is Pisanhari's temple and the other is the 27-foot-long statue of Nataraja Shiva built on the rock of Ravanatol.
C07-JAGTA-HI-065   p. 101   also Ch 11
लेकिन इंटेक की टीम ने अपने ग्राउंड वर्क में 42 नए स्मारकों को चिन्हित किया है, जिनकी देखरेख बहुत जरूरी है और ये समय के साथ बरबाद हो रहे हैं। पांच-छह प्राचीन बावड़ियां बहुत बेहतर हालत में अब तक बची हुई हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* But the INTACH team in its groundwork has identified 42 new monuments whose care is very necessary and which are being ruined with time. Five or six ancient stepwells have survived in much better condition till now.
C07-JAGTA-HI-066   p. 152
यह इकलौती ऐसी जगह है, जहां पुरानी हवेलियां हैं, महल हैं, पुराने बाजार हैं, किले की दीवारे हैं, मंदिर हैं, प्राचीन तालाब और बावड़ियां हैं। एक विकसित नगर रचना का हर अवशेष यहां मौजूद है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* This is the only place where there are old havelis, palaces, old markets, fort walls, temples, ancient tanks and stepwells. Every remnant of a developed town layout is present here.
C07-JAGTA-HI-067   p. 152
## Jagta Hua Kasba / कथा उदयपुर — English translation (JAGTA-EN, 41)
In the Paramaras' time, a fort's protective wall had been built on Udaipur's hill too, which is visible even today, both in front and on Google Map.
C07-JAGTA-EN-001   ≈ p. 23 (Hindi original)
The bad news of that great massacre at Mughal hands must have come to Udaipur through the very Chanderi Darwaza, where I went a little later. That gate is a gate of Udaipur's fort wall running parallel to the main road. It is toward the north in Udaipur. In that era, whichever big city a city's gate faced, it was named after that city. In Khilji's time there is mention of the Badaun [*?*] gate in Delhi. As the Delhi Darwaza in Chanderi, so the Chanderi Darwaza in Udaipur.
C07-JAGTA-EN-002   ≈ p. 51 (Hindi original)
We also went to that Chanderi Gate, about which an officer of the archaeology department itself had told [us] that once there must have been an old temple there. The pieces of images and pillars still lie ownerless there. A small mosque-like structure, so small that five people could barely offer *namaz* in it, is there outside the gate, of a later period, in which only the temple's rubble has been used. Around it, we saw five to seven samadhis, surrounded by houses and almost vanishing. Several *sati* pillars were installed there, which had come to the condition of disappearing.
C07-JAGTA-EN-003   ≈ p. 109 (Hindi original)
On this hill — surrounded by dense, tall grass grown up in the rains, and by a thick forest of teak (*sagwan*) — there is some old little fort, which is not visible even from ten feet away. In the surrounding villages they call it "Garhi." This too is in the Ganj Basoda tehsil of Vidisha district.
C07-JAGTA-EN-004   ≈ p. 154 (Hindi original)
Passing through those very lanes, we went to an old temple named Pisanhari. In lane after lane, pieces of old carved stones have been brought into use anywhere at all.
C07-JAGTA-EN-005   ≈ p. 12 (Hindi original)
There are also the remains of some rock-temple project on the model of Ellora, [made] by cutting this hill, which remained unfinished. On the cut hill, images are visible even today.
C07-JAGTA-EN-006   ≈ p. 23 (Hindi original)
The temple of the Jain Tirthankaras they had smashed to smithereens. Amid the heap of stones, those broken Tirthankaras are telling their own tale even today.
C07-JAGTA-EN-007   ≈ p. 26 (Hindi original)
Ask someone the way to the Jain temple precinct. There, defenceless Tirthankaras have for decades been awaiting only you — on whose living images blows were dealt in some period. Their broken images too are as old as Udaipur's history. This was not the city of Shiva's worshippers alone; here there must have been prosperous followers of the Tirthankaras too — otherwise who would have had such magnificent images made? Amid the broken stones, those mutilated Tirthankaras, present for centuries, want to say something to you.
C07-JAGTA-EN-008   ≈ p. 39 (Hindi original)   also Ch 8
Anyone will tell you of a plain temple of Pisanhari, which was built very simply — but the splendid carving of its entrance is matchless.
C07-JAGTA-EN-009   ≈ p. 39 (Hindi original)
A piece, in the shape of a Shiva-linga, cut from the original mountain, is visible from miles away. It is calling you. This is a very incomplete work of making a rock-temple in the Paramaras' time, which could not be completed. On that cut hill, images were being carved. Then, for some reason, this project must have been stopped. Now, draping green cloths on it, it is being given the shape of dargahs.
C07-JAGTA-EN-010   ≈ p. 40 (Hindi original)
It was a small and unseen temple. Peeping from behind the bushes, this splendid temple was gazing into the blue sky like some missile — as though just ready for the missile to launch. It was carved so beautifully that it seemed like some feat of metal, designed by computer and cast from some factory. If, by technical trickery on the computer, a metal colour were put over the colour of the stones, it would look exactly like a missile. On a seven-foot-high platform, in a corner of the vast precinct surrounded by rubble and bushes, up a narrow lane in front of the temple, was that temple.
C07-JAGTA-EN-011   ≈ p. 93 (Hindi original)
On a seven-foot-high platform, this is a temple of a very narrow design, on whose door is a worn figure of Ganesha-ji. It is so small and thin that, within, it seems less like a temple and more like some ascetic's place of meditation — some place prepared for the *sadhana* of some accomplished practitioner. The outer design is such as though it had been prepared on a computer.
C07-JAGTA-EN-012   ≈ p. 95 (Hindi original)
The famous archaeologist Narayan Vyas, seeing its photos, has assessed that it is of the 16th century. That is, after Udayaditya, the construction of temples continued for centuries, whereas Udaipur, during this [time], had become a victim of the Islamic invaders.
C07-JAGTA-EN-013   ≈ p. 95 (Hindi original)
Like Gwalior and Chanderi, the presence of Jain idols upon the hilly rocks tells us that this whole region held within itself religious traditions of every kind.
C07-JAGTA-EN-014   ≈ p. 151 (Hindi original)
But soon this will stop being visible, because on a building of the single narrow lane leading to the temple, a board of the Jama Masjid has been hung. On its small minarets, large minarets of iron and plywood have been put on like caps. Now, not the temple, but this mosque will begin to be visible from afar.
C07-JAGTA-EN-015   ≈ p. 23 (Hindi original)
Grope and see the stones of the pillars of the mosque built in one part of it — they too tell of the Paramaras. The mosque too is built in the vast square precinct of the palace. It must originally have been a part of the palace itself, but after the Muslims' occupation, this court-like structure was turned into a place of worship (*ibadatgah*).
C07-JAGTA-EN-016   ≈ p. 53 (Hindi original)   also Ch 10
One of the two mosques, the people of Udaipur have been calling "kachahari" (the court) since childhood; their fathers and grandfathers too told just this — that it is a kachahari. People remember that once a signboard of the name "kachahari" too used to be put up. When a kachahari got transformed into a mosque, no one knows. Not merely a mosque, but a Jama Masjid. It is remembered by the name of Emperor Jahangir. Something written on stone from Jahangir's time must have been found.
C07-JAGTA-EN-017   ≈ p. 101 (Hindi original)   also Ch 10
The special thing is that this kachahari — or the Jama Masjid of the Muslim brothers — has clearly been a part of the Paramaras' palace, which was once on the right side of the palace, in the four-bigha long-and-wide courtyard encircled by four bastions and twenty-foot-high walls.
C07-JAGTA-EN-018   ≈ p. 102 (Hindi original)
The work of making a mosque from the temple's remnants began. Peer behind the stones of the mosque, and some idol or other will be stuck [there]. A blueprint was drawn up to cover over the temple. Standing in the temple precinct and looking, the half-finished walls of the mosque bear witness to this.
C07-JAGTA-EN-019   ≈ p. 160 (Hindi original)   also Ch 10
No one saw those inscriptions on which our own ancestors had written who-knows-what. No one went up to those stepwells and tanks where, in one age, the liveliness of people from far and wide must have been.
C07-JAGTA-EN-020   ≈ p. 10 (Hindi original)
On Ghatera Road there is a goddess-temple, where you will see a stepwell exactly like Vidisha's Bija Mandal, and, in the whole precinct, several broken images. Here, recently, in the excavation of a mound, one more stepwell older than that has been found, in which you will see a 45-degree descent leading down below the surface of the ground. Once people must have gone straight down to the stepwell from here. Horses went down to the bottom through it.
C07-JAGTA-EN-021   ≈ p. 40 (Hindi original)
They took us to an old stepwell near a brick-kiln, whose design is astonishing. One could go right down by the curving, sturdy stone steps. There too we saw an inscription.
C07-JAGTA-EN-022   ≈ p. 70 (Hindi original)
We brought an exclusive section of three inscriptions, by which the centuries-old, experience-rich journey of Udaipur could be understood in a timeline. We focused separately on the water-bodies. The photos of the splendid stepwells were recalling the famous Rani-ki-Vav of Patan in Gujarat.
C07-JAGTA-EN-023   ≈ p. 77 (Hindi original)   also Ch 6
Before departing from Udaipur, we went toward that Uday Samudra, where a week earlier INTACH's team had already worked and had measured its length as 980 metres. The steps of the splendid ghats of carved stones now descend not into Uday Samudra but into those fields where, in the passing, warming month of March, the wheat crop stood ready for harvest. They are now fertile black-soil fields spread far and wide, where once the vast tank built by Udayaditya struck the banks like a sea. INTACH's team's work, that day, the Ganj Basoda heritage team captured in their cameras, and helped in digging down to one or two steps. In just one or two feet of digging on the upper surface of the ground, plenty of conches and shells, and a copper coin, were found — as though, from across the centuries, Maharaj Udayaditya himself had given his awakened subjects the reward of coming here.
C07-JAGTA-EN-024   ≈ p. 110 (Hindi original)
In Udaipur, besides Uday Samudra, countless stepwells had been built, where the craftsmen of the Paramaras have made a strong display of their skill.
C07-JAGTA-EN-025   ≈ p. 110 (Hindi original)
The third corner of that desolate three-way junction of Udaipur takes you into an extremely narrow lane, barely six or seven feet wide. This single crooked and extremely narrow lane, entering the settlement from the main road, goes upward along a slope. This is a painful path to reach the greatest identity of a Udaipur that, in its own time, was magnificent and more than a thousand years old. On both sides of this roughly three-hundred-metre-long lane are decades-old shops made of stone — of cloth, groceries, sweets.
C07-JAGTA-EN-026   ≈ p. 8 (Hindi original)   also Ch 12
With the passing centuries, Udaipur must have kept spreading and sprawling here and there over its old plan. Broad and orderly roads turned into disorderly narrow lanes.
C07-JAGTA-EN-027   ≈ p. 8 (Hindi original)
On both sides of the temple's finest precinct are astonishing constructions built in the time of the Paramara kings. Old houses built from the arrangement of massive carved stones. The narrow lanes too must once have been quite broad, straight, clean roads, which, after the Paramaras, shrank and grew narrow on account of illegal encroachments.
C07-JAGTA-EN-028   ≈ p. 10 (Hindi original)   also Ch 11
Instead of going into the temple, the two of us first went to a building which is there called Bija Mandal. Seen from outside, it is a residential compound like the ordinary houses of a village; but only on going inside does one understand that the original building must once have been very magnificent indeed. The door of one of its cellar-like rooms is just like that of some old temple — almost as old as the old temple.
C07-JAGTA-EN-029   ≈ p. 11 (Hindi original)
The two of us, talking together, reached another corner of Udaipur, and, going along a stony footpath, arrived at the ruins of an old palace in front. It had been left utterly abandoned, on the brink of collapse — let it survive at its own peril, let it fall at its own peril. Even after centuries, in the inner part of the rubble-filled courtyard, its rooms and verandas were in fairly good condition. Standing on the uneven stones, Dr. Mishra cast a sweeping glance all around the palace. Leaping and jumping, I went some way inside and reached a room which must certainly, in the Paramaras' time, have been the residence of some very important person. On the walls were splendid shelves of carved stone, in which the articles of daily use must have been kept.
C07-JAGTA-EN-030   ≈ p. 13 (Hindi original)
On the way, we also sketched an outline of the actual map of Raja Udayaditya's time — how these roads must then have been made, cutting one another at 90 degrees. On these roads there must have been the bustle of chariots, bullock-carts, horses and elephants. Those huge doors, which the security guards must have shut after evening, and, holding torches on the fort walls, they must have kept watch. Separate detachments must have been posted for the palace's security.
C07-JAGTA-EN-031   ≈ p. 15 (Hindi original)   also Ch 4
Suresh Mishra, I wrote that in this settlement of eight thousand people, buildings of a thousand, twelve hundred years still survive to a large extent. The whole town exists on its old design in an uneven condition, where there are walls, doorways, temples, palaces, tanks, stepwells, rooms, walls and lanes built according to the architecture of the Paramara dynasty.
C07-JAGTA-EN-032   ≈ p. 18 (Hindi original)   also Ch 12
In the ruins of the palace, go as far as you can, leaping and jumping. Roam in those inner areas that are being lost in the rubble. The niches (*aale*) surviving in their doors and walls make a splendid display of the Paramaras' design. The stone walls raised between the pillars of the corridors will tell you clearly that these are encroachments of a later period.
C07-JAGTA-EN-033   ≈ p. 39 (Hindi original)
When you roam the lanes seeing all this, at several places you will pass through huge old stone doorways. In the Paramaras' orderly Udaipur, these doors were fitted on the roads. Around them you will see rows of those shops in which centuries of rubble is filled. These must have been part of a lively bazaar.
C07-JAGTA-EN-034   ≈ p. 40 (Hindi original)   also Ch 8
The broken remains of the temple were visible everywhere, outside and inside his house. In a narrow old room at the front of the house was his small grocery shop. Praveen-ji took us inside. The sight of the inner rooms was ten centuries old. It was exactly like the sanctum of some temple, on which carved stones of splendid images were embedded.
C07-JAGTA-EN-035   ≈ p. 68 (Hindi original)
This house was once buried in a mound. They used to live in the front part. When, for more living space, the clearing of the mound began, these rooms — built of splendidly carved stones — were found made [there].
C07-JAGTA-EN-036   ≈ p. 69 (Hindi original)
— [the story of history's storms,] in whose excavation, decades ago, an old residence with splendid artworks lay buried. With devotion and labour it was cleaned. That very [place] is now their house, which is a published page of the story of Udaipur.
C07-JAGTA-EN-037   ≈ p. 72 (Hindi original)
But behind them, from the arrangement of the stones, the story of several different periods could clearly be seen.
The stones of the rear of a Ram temple were the oldest, near which the buildings of the old shops of the big market must have been erected later. But all these too must have been more than five hundred years old.
C07-JAGTA-EN-038   ≈ p. 93 (Hindi original)
In the whole Udaipur town-area and around it, within a radius of five km, important archaeological wealth lies scattered. Even within a 500-metre radius of the temple, the houses that stand are built from ancient antiquarian material (*purasamagri*).
C07-JAGTA-EN-039   ≈ p. 166 (Hindi original)
His team went and saw, from every corner, the thousand-year-old tanks, stepwells, the Nataraja Shiva present on a 27-foot rock in Ravan Toll, the Pisanhari temple, the hill's fortification, Bija Mandal, Malukdas Jain's residence, the old doorways, the big market, and, most importantly, the ruins of the palace. They found Udaipur to be such a place where, truly, immediate large-scale conservation was needed.
C07-JAGTA-EN-040   ≈ p. 86 (Hindi original)   also Ch 5, Ch 11
But the INTACH team, in its ground work, has identified 42 new monuments whose care is very necessary, and which are being ruined with the passage of time. Five or six ancient stepwells have survived, in much better condition, until now.
C07-JAGTA-EN-041   ≈ p. 151 (Hindi original)

## The Cultural Heritage of Madhya Bharat — Patil, D. R. (MADHYABHARAT, 14)
Sher Khan ki Masjid.—The town was surrounded with a fortification wall pierced with a number of gates. These fortifications are continued towards the south so as to be connected with a hill, the top of which is also fortified. Just outside the eastern gate of the city wall called Moti Darwaza is situated a small mosque with a graveyard of a common raised plinth now in ruins.
C07-MADHYABHARAT-003   p. 118
Bara-khambi.—This is the surviving portion (Sabhamandapa) of a now ruined 11th century temple standing on the outskirt of the village. The hall is surrounded by raised seats with back rests, and has a massive slab as its ceiling.
C07-MADHYABHARAT-004   p. 117
There are some other rock sculptures in the vicinity of Udaypur such as a huge but unfinished image of Shiva carved in boulder locally known as Ravan tor and a panel of Sapta Matrikas in the side of an adjoining hill the natural shape of which resembles that of a Buddhist Stupa.
C07-MADHYABHARAT-005   p. 118   also Ch 5
by a woman out of her earning made by grinding flour for the workmen employed for the construction of the Udayeshvara temple. This tradition is, however, belied by the style of the architecture of the temple which shows that it is a much later structure.
C07-MADHYABHARAT-006   p. 118   also Ch 9
1206-1526) brings the impact of Islam in Madhya Bharat, felt more keenly by the fertile plateau of Malwa, still observed at Mandu, Dhar, Nalcha, Sadalpur, Sarangpur and Ujjain. In Bhilsa and Chanderi the same conditions were obtained as reflected in the numerous Muslim monuments at Chanderi, Basoda, Udaypur and Bhilsa.
C07-MADHYABHARAT-008   p. 70   also Ch 10
Shahi Masjid and Mahal.—Nearly a furlong to the east of the great temple is a big mosque, an imposing edifice locally known as Shali Masjid now in ruins. According to Persian inscription on it, the construction of the mosque was commenced in the reign of Jehangir and completed in that of Shah-jahan in A. H. 1041 (A. D. 1632).
C07-MADHYABHARAT-011   p. 118
The mosque is built in the Mandu style of architecture. It has inscriptions (both Persian and Sanskrit) which refer to the construction of the mosque by an agent of Sherkhan during the rule of Chiyas Shah Khilji, Sultan of Mandu, in A. H. 894 (A. D. 1488).
C07-MADHYABHARAT-012   p. 118   also Ch 10
The temple referred to in the inscription is of course the present monument, the town is the one in which the temple stands and the ruins of the tank Udayasamudra are seen at a short distance to the north-east of the town.
C07-MADHYABHARAT-015   p. 115
A short distance further east of the mosque is a big step-well locally known as a Ghoddaud-ki-bacdi, so called because it has a spacious and easy flight of steps along which horses could run down to reach the water.
C07-MADHYABHARAT-016   p. 118
Bijamandal or Ghadiyalan-ka-makan.—This is the remnant of an interesting two-storeyed house close to, and probably contemporary with the Udayeshvara temple. As its name implies it was probably the house of the time-keeper or clockman on the establishment of the great temple. This is perhaps corroborated by an unfinished Sanskrit inscription on the building which opens with the praise of the Sun-god.
C07-MADHYABHARAT-018   p. 117
Close to the mosque are the remains of a spacious mahal or mansion which was probably the residence of the local governor during the Mughal rule. The mahal is built in the simple and elegant style of the early Mughal period and the existing ruins contain some good jali work in stone. In front of the mosque is a large platform which supports a number of tombs. The mosque and the graveyard are evidently adjuncts of the mahal.
C07-MADHYABHARAT-019   p. 118
Next to it is the north-eastern region of Bhilsa district which, considering its limited area and the importance of the monuments situated in it, is naturally the richest district in Madhya Bharat. The well-known places such as Besnagar, Ulaygiri, Bhilsa, Gyaraspur, Udaypur, Badoh, Pathari, Kappur, etc., are within this area.
C07-MADHYABHARAT-020   p. 68

## The Udayeśvara Temple: Art, Architecture and Philosophy of the Śaiva Siddhānta — Anupa Pande (PANDE, 9)
Pisanhārī kā Mandir, Udayapur, Vidisha, Madhya Pradesh. 92
C07-PANDE-001   p. 7
Near the Udayeśvara temple is a small temple, later in date, plain and unostentatious, called Pisanhārī kā Mandir, i.e. the temple constructed by a grinder of flour (pl. 129). It is said that a grinder gradually saved money to get this temple constructed. It is interesting that though on the one hand, royalty could patronize temples, on the other, even a grinder could commission a temple where Brahmins performed rituals.
C07-PANDE-002   p. 92   also Ch 8
128. View of the mihrab of the mosque on the western side. 91
C07-PANDE-003   p. 7
Thus, in the 14th century CE, the western rear of the temple was converted into a mosque (pl. 2). Two archways were built to the left and right of the garbhagrha. These seemed to have served as symbolic entrances to the mosque. They are not only small in size but there is plenty of open space around, making them quite redundant. In fact, one houses a vibrant Garuḍāsana Viṣnu image which probably dates only a couple of hundred years back.
C07-PANDE-004   p. 88   also Ch 10
"The temple is built of red sandstone and stands in the centre of a large paved and raised platform; on all sides of the platform there once ran walls, with seats as in section, but this has long disappeared except in the masjid at the back: surrounding the great temple stood at one time seven minor temples as at Pathari, but somewhat differently arranged. All of them have now disappeared or been converted into, and
C07-PANDE-005   p. 88   also Ch 5, Ch 11
"Muhammadan invaders
mutilated the figure sculptures of the temples. Muhammad Tughluq between AD 1336-38 damaged the attendant temples and erected a
mosque at the back of the temple, using the material from the demolished Persian inscriptions in the mosque record its construction by him."130
C07-PANDE-006   p. 88   also Ch 10
The temple at the back, if one existed, as I believe it did, judging from the example at Pathari, does not now exist in its place, but, occupying nearly the whole extent of the projecting piece of platform on this side, stands a Muhammadan masjid consisting of a hall with flat roof, supported by four rows of Hindu Pillars, the last row consisting of dwarf pillars resting on the seat, which runs along the back and side walls; there are twelve pillars in each row, the two extreme ones being dwarf ones, resting on the seats; they are not all alike, but some attempt at a symmetrical arrangement of the dissimilar pillars appears to have been made with partial success; the pillars are fine, massive ones, but not otherwise remarkable; at the centre of the back wall exists the niche of the *melrrab*; a few masons' marks and short lines occur on many of the pillars in this masjid, and conclusively prove, if such evidence were at all necessary, that they are Hindu.
C07-PANDE-007   p. 89
Beglar has made an error as regards his statement that Aurangzeb built the masjid. It was Muhammad Tughlak, in the 14th century, who built the masjid and the inscriptions record his name.
C07-PANDE-008   p. 90   also Ch 10, Ch 11
The group of buildings consisted originally of the great central temple standing in the midst of a square court-yard with a small temple at each of the four corners and a square bedi, or hall for reading the beds, in the middle of each side.
The north-west corner temple and the western bedi were knocked down in the time of Muhammad Tughlak and a masjid was erected in their place, as recorded in the two inscriptions over the two small doorways to the right and left of the temple, which are dated respectively in 737 and 739 of the Hijira. The gateway on the west is not in the middle, and, as it is built of old materials, I have no doubt that it was built at the same time as the masjid.'¹³²
C07-PANDE-009   p. 91   also Ch 10, Ch 11

## Corpus Inscriptionum Indicarum, Vol. VII, Part 2: Inscriptions of the Paramāras — Trivedi, Harihar Vithal (ed.) (CII, 1)
He got the tank constructed and the amount spent, mentioned both in words and figures, was 2,500 coins stamped with royal effigy.\(^{1}\)
Verses 10-15 describe the tank, stating that it had wide banks and it was a resort of aquatic animals like fishes, crocodiles and others. It was built with stainless (dressed) stones and for the use of bathing, offering prayers, worshipping gods and sages and presenting libations to the manes of deseased ancestors. It is also said that considering the great merit of making provision for water, it was built by Vikrama by spending the amount which he had earned by just means (suddhair dhanair) and by the strength of his arms (swa-bhuj-ōpārjita).

# Chapter 8. Religion and the Material Life of Udaypur: Ritual, Agriculture, Trade and Craft
## Jagta Hua Kasba / कथा उदयपुर — English translation (JAGTA-EN, 14)
His family is famous by the name of the Ghadiyali family. They relate that in some age, at the fixed times of the temple's worship, ablution and rites, bells and gongs (*ghante-ghadiyal*) were sounded from this very building. This was an announcement to the whole town that the time to come to the temple had arrived. It is possible that special signals were given at the times of the royal family's activity in the temple too. By this reckoning, it was an important institution connected with the temple's activities, which was possibly also used as the residence of some important person connected with the temple management or the worship-rites.
C08-JAGTA-EN-001   ≈ p. 12 (Hindi original)
The finding of one instrument was, at the least, giving the news that there must have been the teaching of music, or the presence of knowers of music, here. Certainly, for the praises of Shiva sung in the temple, some group of musicians must have lived here.
C08-JAGTA-EN-002   ≈ p. 69 (Hindi original)
From two kilometres before, we saw that a long throng was advancing toward the temple. Four hundred policemen had been specially deployed for the convenience and security of the thousands of devotees coming until evening.
C08-JAGTA-EN-004   ≈ p. 94 (Hindi original)   also Ch 9
Every year on this day a big fair is held in Udaipur, in which thousands of people come for the Shiva temple, from even the surrounding districts.
C08-JAGTA-EN-005   ≈ p. 94 (Hindi original)   also Ch 9
If we look at Udaipur's vastness and grandeur, then — in view of its economic and administrative aspect — its budget too must have been quite considerable. Since Udayadity was himself the ruler, the arrangement of resources for the temple must certainly have been made by this kind of vision. We have not found any writing giving detailed information especially about this. Whenever some such writing is found, some help may be had in understanding the economy of this temple.
C08-JAGTA-EN-007   ≈ p. 145 (Hindi original)
On this inscription the date is written as Samvat 1286 (1229 CE), Kartik Sudi ___, Friday. The last part of the date is broken. The purpose of this inscription is to record the donation of 4 *biswa* of land for the temple of Udayeshvardev by the treasury-officer Dhamdev, in the reign of Maharaja Devpaldev.
C08-JAGTA-EN-008   ≈ p. 146 (Hindi original)   also Ch 6
This inscription is on a pillar to the right of the temple's eastern gate.
In 1889 it appears in the *Indian Antiquary*, Volume 18, page 342, and Volume 20, page 83, serial no. 2; in the *Annual Reports of the Archaeological Department, Gwalior State, Samvat 1974*, serial no. 121; at serial no. 483 in Bhandarkar's list of Northern Inscriptions; and at serial no. 207 in Kielhorn's list.
C08-JAGTA-EN-009   ≈ p. 146 (Hindi original)   also Ch 6
> *for Raykuk (?) ____ taking water [in hand], donated 4 *biswa*.*
C08-JAGTA-EN-010   ≈ p. 147 (Hindi original)   also Ch 6
In this inscription of Devpal Dev, the measure of the land given in donation is written as 4 *biswa*, which at present is the twentieth part of one *bigha* of land — a very small plot. This unit of measuring land was locally in vogue in the Bhopal region.
C08-JAGTA-EN-011   ≈ p. 147 (Hindi original)
For instance, the mention of "*panchakul*" in the eighth line. He tells us that this word has often been used for the village-elders' assembly (*gram-vriddha-sabha*) as well. In the states of the south, it was also known by the name of "*pancha muvadu*." This was an institution rather like today's panchayat, which handled the local administration.
C08-JAGTA-EN-012   ≈ p. 148 (Hindi original)   also Ch 6
It is possible that this piece of land — appearing very small in size — had some special quality. It may have been full of a water source, so that the water-supply for this temple could be well ensured. It may have been given for the residential use of the royal priest or the chief manager; or upon it there may have been some splendid stepwell (*baoli*), whose water was specially preserved for worship, ablution (*abhishek*) or rituals in the sanctum!
C08-JAGTA-EN-014   ≈ p. 149 (Hindi original)
Udaipur and Patari in Vidisha district is a region rich from the quarries of stone. The stone here, after being cut and dressed, is sent abroad. Ganj Basoda is the nearest city, where there must be over a hundred millionaire businessmen of grocery, cloth, grain and stone.
C08-JAGTA-EN-015   ≈ p. 37 (Hindi original)   also Ch 2
Along with top-class engineering, the artisans of mathematics, sculpture and painting must have come from who-knows-where and displayed their art-and-skill. In the vast precincts, these places of worship were, in truth, developed and busy centres of education, culture and art, where, in the witness of the deities, a cultured new generation was shaped.
C08-JAGTA-EN-016   ≈ p. 71 (Hindi original)
Udaipur is also a proof of India's prosperous economic heritage, for such vast creations simply cannot be the mark of a destitute country. If King Udayaditya had the Udayeshvar temple built, then the common populace of Udaipur too contributed many creations here — and only an economically robust society could do such a thing.
C08-JAGTA-EN-017   ≈ p. 122 (Hindi original)
## कथा उदयपुर (Jagta Hua Kasba) — Hindi original (JAGTA-HI, 12)
आगंतुक भारतीयों की दिलचस्पी केवल मंदिर में थी। मंदिर भी नहीं मंदिर के गर्भगृह में विराजे नीलकंठेश्वर महादेव में। लोग सीधे अंदर जाते। पूजा-अभिषेक करते। अपनी अंतहीन मनोकामनाओं के विष की एक और श्रेणी भोलेनाथ के गले में टांगकर बाहर आ जाते।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Visiting Indians were interested only in the temple, and not even the temple but Nilkantheshvar Mahadev seated in its sanctum. People went straight in. They did worship and abhisheka, and came out hanging one more kind of the poison of their endless wishes on Bholenath's neck.
C08-JAGTA-HI-001   p. 10
वे बताते हैं कि किसी जमाने में मंदिर के पूजा, अभिषेक और अनुष्ठान के तयशुदा समय पर इसी इमारत से घंटे-घड़ियाल बजाए जाते थे। यह पूरे नगर को एक सूचना होती थी कि मंदिर में आने का समय हो गया है। संभव है राज परिवार की मंदिर में चहल-पहल के समय भी खास संकेत दिए जाते हों। इस हिसाब से यह मंदिर की गतिविधियों से जुड़ा एक महत्वपूर्ण संस्थान था, जो संभवतः मंदिर प्रबंधन या पूजा-अनुष्ठान से जुड़े किसी महत्वपूर्ण व्यक्ति के आवास के रूप में भी इस्तेमाल किया जाता हो।
*Gloss [my translation]:* They say that once, at the fixed times of the temple's worship, abhisheka and rituals, bells and gongs were sounded from this very building. It was a notice to the whole town that it was time to come to the temple. Perhaps special signals were also given when the royal family was at the temple. In this respect it was an important institution linked to the temple's activities, possibly also used as the residence of an important person connected with temple management or ritual.
C08-JAGTA-HI-002   p. 12   also Ch 5
इन सब हादसों के बाद भी हर दिन हजारों काफिर नीलकंठेश्वर महादेव के दर्शन करने आते रहे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Even after all these disasters, thousands of 'kafirs' kept coming every day for darshan of Nilakantheshwar Mahadev.
C08-JAGTA-HI-003   p. 52
एक वाद्य यंत्र का मिलना कम से कम यह तो खबर दे ही रहा था कि यहां संगीत की शिक्षा या संगीत के जानकारों की मौजूदगी रही होगी। निश्चित रूप में मंदिर में गाई जाने वाले शिव की स्तुतियों के लिए संगीतज्ञों का कोई दल यहां रहा होगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Finding a musical instrument at least told that there must have been teaching of music or music experts here. Certainly a group of musicians must have been here for the hymns to Shiva sung in the temple.
C08-JAGTA-HI-004   p. 69   also Ch 9
हर साल इस दिन एक बड़ा मेला उदयपुर में लगता है, जिसमें आसपास के जिलों तक से हजारों लोग शिव मंदिर के लिए आते हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Every year on this day a big fair is held in Udaypur, in which thousands of people, even from neighbouring districts, come for the Shiva temple.
C08-JAGTA-HI-005   p. 94   also Ch 9
उदयपुर भारत की समृद्ध आर्थिक विरासत का भी सबूत है, क्योंकि इतनी विशाल रचनाएँ किसी दरिद्र देश का परिचय हो ही नहीं सकती। अगर राजा उदयादित्य ने उदयेश्वर मंदिर का निर्माण कराया तो उदयपुर के आम जनसमाज ने भी अनेक रचनाओं का योगदान यहां दिया था और आर्थिक रूप से सुदृढ़ समाज ही ऐसा कर सकता था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Udaypur is also proof of India's rich economic heritage, since such huge creations cannot be the mark of a poor country. If King Udayaditya had the Udayeshvara temple built, the common people of Udaypur also contributed many works here, and only an economically strong society could do that.
C08-JAGTA-HI-007   p. 122   also Ch 10
उदयपुर की विशालता और भव्यता को देखें तो इसके आर्थिक और प्रशासनिक पक्ष के मदेनजर इसका बजट भी अच्छा-खासा रहा होगा। चूंकि उदयादित्य शासक रहे तो मंदिर के लिए संसाधनों की व्यवस्था इस प्रकार के दृष्ट द्वारा निश्चित ही की गई होगी। विशेष रूप से इस बारे में विस्तृत जानकारी देने वाला कोई लेख हमें नहीं मिला है। कभी ऐसा कोई लेख मिलता है तो इस मंदिर की अर्थव्यवस्था को समझने में कुछ सहायता मिल सकेगी।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Looking at the vastness and grandeur of Udaypur, its budget must have been quite large from its economic and administrative side. Since Udayaditya was the ruler, resources for the temple must certainly have been arranged through such grants. No inscription giving detailed information on this has been found. If one is ever found, it may help in understanding the temple's economy.
C08-JAGTA-HI-012   p. 146
इस अभिलेख पर तिथि संवत् 1286 (1229 ईस्वी) कार्तिक सुदि...शुक्रवार लिखी है। तिथि का अंतिम भाग टूटा हुआ है। इस अभिलेख का मकसद महाराजा देवपालदेव के शासनकाल में राजकोष अधिकारी धामदेव द्वारा उदयेश्वरदेव के मंदिर के निमित्त 4 बिस्वा भूमि के दान का उल्लेख करना है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The date on this inscription is written Samvat 1286 (1229 CE) Kartik sudi ... Friday. The last part of the date is broken. The purpose of this inscription is to mention the donation of 4 biswa of land for the temple of Udayeshvaradeva by Dhamadeva, treasury officer, during the reign of Maharaja Devapaladeva.
C08-JAGTA-HI-013   p. 146   also Ch 6, Ch 10
देवपाल देव के इस अभिलेख में दान में दी गई भूमि का परिमाण 4 बिसवा लिखा है, जो वर्तमान में एक बीघा भूमि का बीसवां भाग होता है। एक बहुत छोटा सा भूखंड जमीन को मापने का यह मात्रक भोपाल क्षेत्र में स्थानीय रूप से प्रचलन में था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* In Devapaladeva's inscription the quantity of land donated is written as 4 biswa, which at present is a twentieth part of a bigha of land. A very small plot; this unit of land measurement was locally current in the Bhopal region.
C08-JAGTA-HI-014   p. 147   also Ch 6
कूके उदकपूर्वके (कं)। वि(बि)स्वे ४ प्रदत____
रायकूक के लिये (?)____जल (हाथ में) लेकर ४ बिसवे दान की।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Rayakuka ... with water poured (in the hand) donated 4 biswa.
C08-JAGTA-HI-015   p. 147   also Ch 6
संभव है आकार में बहुत छोटे नजर आने वाले जमीन के इस टुकड़े की कुछ खासियत रही हो। वह जल के स्रोत से परिपूर्ण हो ताकि इस मंदिर के लिए जलापूर्ति भली भांति सुनिश्चित की जा सके। वह राजपुरोहित या मुख्य प्रबंधक के आवासीय उपयोग के लिए दी गई थी या उस पर कोई शानदार बावड़ी थी, जिसका जल गर्भगृह में पूजा, अभिषेक या अनुष्ठानों के लिए विशेष रूप से संरक्षित किया गया हो! अगर इतनी सी जमीन का जिक्र इतने विशाल मंदिर पर अंकित किया गया है तो उसका इस्तेमाल भी बहुत ही खास किस्म का ही होना चाहिए था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* It is possible that this plot, small in size, had some special quality. It may have been full of water sources so that water supply for this temple could be ensured. It may have been given for the residential use of the royal priest or chief manager, or there may have been a splendid stepwell on it whose water was specially reserved for worship, abhisheka or rituals in the sanctum. If mention of so little land is inscribed on such a huge temple, its use too must have been of a very special kind.
C08-JAGTA-HI-016   p. 149   also Ch 2, Ch 7
अब सवाल यह कि उदयपुर जैसे विशाल मंदिर को बनाने के बाद, जिसका निर्माण दो दशक तक हुआ है सिर्फ 4 बिस्वे जैसी छोटी सी जमीन को एक राजा द्वारा मंदिर को दान में देने का क्या अर्थ हो सकता है? वर्मा बताते हैं कि ऐसे मामूली से नजर आने वाले भूमिदान का यह अकेला उदाहरण नहीं है। उस दौर में राजस्थान और गुजरात में नाडौल और शाकम्भरी के चौहानों और गुजरात के चालुक्यों के कुछ लेखों में धार्मिक कार्यों के लिए छोटी भूमि के दान के लेख हैं। इस लेख से उस कीमती जमीन के टुकड़े की पहचान आज उदयपुर या उसके आसपास कहीं मुमकिन नहीं है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Now the question: after building a huge temple like Udaypur, whose construction took two decades, what could be the meaning of a king donating only a small piece of land like 4 biswa to the temple? Verma says this is not the only example of such seemingly minor land grants. In that period some records of the Chauhans of Naddula and Shakambhari in Rajasthan and the Chalukyas of Gujarat contain donations of small plots for religious purposes. From this inscription it is not possible today to identify that valuable piece of land in or around Udaypur.
C08-JAGTA-HI-017   p. 149   also Ch 6
