## The Udayeśvara Temple: Art, Architecture and Philosophy of the Śaiva Siddhānta — Anupa Pande (PANDE, 4)
three narrow outlets for water to seep out. It has a simple but elegant scroll border of lotuses alternating with lotus buds and is aesthetic and charming to behold. The other purpose, in the past, could have been to keep the drum. The time-keeper, in fact, was an important person in the temple retinue, and he struck the drum at the required regular intervals.
C08-PANDE-009   p. 83
According to tradition, immediately after the completion of the temple, the King ordered the vedī to be built for the performance of a yajña or ritual sacrifice. The function of the vedī was probably that yajñas, rituals etc. may be held there. The smoke would not gather inside as it has profuse ventilation. There are two high stone benches on the north and south side for seating of people, possibly the King and the nobility as is indicated by the elevated seats. It is possible that some music and dance may have also been organized with some of the rituals.
C08-PANDE-010   p. 87
This temple is a living one, where right up to the 21st century, there are the usual morning and evening pūja and aarti. The priests also perform, on request, special pūjas like Rudrāṣṭaka, Śiva Mahimā and Śiva Abhiṣeka. Religious fairs are held in the vicinity of temple on Śaiva festivals. The main festival of the year is on Śivarātri. All the villagers gather here, as also those of the adjacent villages. On this occasion, an unending stream of devotees lines up in front of the temple from the morning to the evening. The four Mondays of the Śrāvana month, i.e. around July (Sāvan ke Somavār) are also significant for the worship of Śiva.
C08-PANDE-011   p. 92   also Ch 9, Ch 12
Near the temple is a house called Ghariyālon Kā Makān, i.e. the house of the time-keepers of the temple, who claim to have been there for centuries. Their vocation had been to strike the enormous gong in the temple to keep time. Thus, the various rituals would be performed on time.
C08-PANDE-012   p. 92
## History of the Paramāra Dynasty — Ganguly, D. C. (GANGULY, 3)
Udayāditya is reported to have erected many other temples, caused tanks to be excavated, performed religious ceremonies, and granted lands productive of gold and corn to the Brahmans.
C08-GANGULY-013   p. 152
During his reign, Lûnapasâka was in charge of the government of this province. In 1172 A. D., Lûnapasâka, for the spiritual benefit of his father, granted the village of Umaratha, situated in the Pathaka known as Bhrngârikâ-Catuṣṣaṣṭi, in favour of the God Vaidyanâtha, at Udayapur. Here the record describes Udayapur as being situated in the province (manḍala) of Bhâillasvâmi-mahâdvâ-dasâka (modern Bhilsa).
C08-GANGULY-015   p. 189   also Ch 10, Ch 6
The charge of the eastern division was entrusted to the Mahâsâdhanika Râjyapâla, whose headquarters were at Udayapur in Bhilsa. In 1163 A. D., when this chief was in office, a certain personage, named Vasantapâla, made some donations for the maintenance of the temple of Udalesvara at the town of Udayapur.
C08-GANGULY-016   p. 189   also Ch 10

And since the donee is not mentioned in the record, we may conclude that the donation was made in favour of the deity himself, in whose temple the inscription was found. The donor was Dhāmadēya(va?), an officer in charge of the treasury of the king Dēvapāla, during whose reign the record was engraved.

## The Cultural Heritage of Madhya Bharat — Patil, D. R. (MADHYABHARAT, 2)
That Shaivism had grown to be a still more powerful and flourishing sect in the succeeding period of 10-12th centuries A.D. is obvious from the fact that most of the beautiful temples of this time in Madhya Bharat such as at Udaypur, Nemawar, Kadwaha, Un, Mitaoli, Suhania, Surwaya, Jamli, Badnawar, Naresar, Kherat, etc., still enshrine lingas of Shiva actively worshipped by the people. Some of these structures are sheer marvels in the art of temple building and stone carving and could not certainly have been raised without an inspiring faith and devotion which alone produce such great works of art.
C08-MADHYABHARAT-007   p. 72   also Ch 5
The liuga is now covered with a brass sheet fitted on the front with a face in relief. As an inscription on it states, this cover was presented by Khande Rao Appaji (a General of Mahadaji Scindia), in V. S. 1841 (A.D. 1775).
C08-MADHYABHARAT-008   p. 116   also Ch 10
# Chapter 9. Society and Cultural Life: Communities, Languages, Festivals and Oral Traditions
## Jagta Hua Kasba / कथा उदयपुर — English translation (JAGTA-EN, 8)
In the population of eight thousand, there are one-to-one-and-a-half thousand Muslims, who, in the stories of their Muslim past, will take you up to some mosque. But in the time of the Paramara king Udayaditya, their ancestors were the very people who gave shape to this town — some craftsman, some guard, some trader, some courtier, some priest. Like the breaking and re-making of images, identity too was broken.
C09-JAGTA-EN-001   ≈ p. 40 (Hindi original)   also Ch 12
Mallu Chand-ji told us that only three Jain families are left in Udaipur.
C09-JAGTA-EN-002   ≈ p. 68 (Hindi original)   also Ch 12
This image is considered that of a royal dancer, who is also called Bedni Mata. About this vermilion-smeared image, a popular story among the local people is that she had to dance on a thin thread tied across the reservoir. Once someone broke that thread, and that dancer could not complete the dance. She died from falling. It is her samadhi.
C09-JAGTA-EN-003   ≈ p. 111 (Hindi original)
We do not know since when this story is current. Who was that royal dancer, in whose kingdom she was, whether she is some character of Udayaditya's golden period or of some later feudatory's time — we know nothing about this.
C09-JAGTA-EN-004   ≈ p. 111 (Hindi original)
**The first thread:** This was a Garhi of dacoits. From here orders would go out that so much loot was wanted, and at once the demand would be met. That is, some ancestors of Gabbar Singh ruled from here. Their edict would go to Ramgarh, and whatever they wished, they would get. Theirs was an organized power.
C09-JAGTA-EN-005   ≈ p. 155 (Hindi original)
**The second thread:** There was a village named Daulatpur below, which is now desolate. There were other such villages too, of which there is no trace. There are only the names; there are no villages. Once, markets used to be held here. But how did the villages become desolate?
C09-JAGTA-EN-006   ≈ p. 156 (Hindi original)
**The fourth thread:** "Bagdi" was a branch of local Rajputs who, by rebelling or revolting, did not obey the orders of the Muslim rulers. They lived here in an organized way.
C09-JAGTA-EN-007   ≈ p. 156 (Hindi original)
Is this a worn-down fragment of some "Gyasuddin," and was some ruler (*hakim*) of that name ever an occupier here? In that case there are two possibilities: this must have been some Rajput's principality, and after the Muslim occupations at Chanderi, Raisen or Mandu, some petty ruler may have come to seize the principality here. Fit one piece of the story here: that this very Gyasuddin used to send decrees to collect tax from the surrounding villages. Obviously, in the eyes of the village people he would be a looter or dacoit, not any king.
C09-JAGTA-EN-008   ≈ p. 157 (Hindi original)
## Geo-Heritage of Udaypur, Vidisha District, Madhya Pradesh: The Third Eye (GEOHER, 7)
We find People of all religions and faiths staying in the village, the fine antiquities of the Jains, the Mohammadan, Hindu temples, mahals, large havelis, etc.
C09-GEOHER-001   p. 46   also Ch 7
The Bhujariya lake probably got its name from the rituals that happen on the next day of Raksha Bandhan or Shravan Poornima according to the Hindu calendar. The festival has a high cultural significance in central India, especially in some parts of Bundelkhand and similarly in Udaypur.
C09-GEOHER-002   p. 75
Another oral tradition also states that the hilly region was the territory of the Gonds. Udayaditya conquered it and renamed it Udaypur.
From the Mauryans to the Marathas, it was controlled by dynastic dominions, but surprisingly, the Gonds remain obscure in this list. That's because of the poor record-keeping of historians in that era.
C09-GEOHER-003   p. 13   also Ch 4
The local folklore talks about a local chief by the name of Bhringraj, and the village was called Bhringrajpur. On the east side of the hill was a basti.
C09-GEOHER-004   p. 46   also Ch 4
The local folklore says that this was used as a jail by the earlier rulers, a claim that needs more research.
The local folklore says that this was used as a prison by various ruling dynasties of this area. Being inaccessible from all sides due to the steep climb off the hill, it could serve as an ideal location for a jail for political opponents. it is said that at one time, iron chains were hanging all around it and were used to chain the culprits, but that is something that needs to be confirmed.
C09-GEOHER-005   p. 70
Another popular folklore associated with the lake is that there used to be a famous dancer in the court of King Udayaditya. She was also well-skilled in religious texts and spells. The king challenged that if she to dance from one end of the lake to the other on a string, then he would reward her with half of his kingdom. The dancer accepted the offer and danced gracefully.
C09-GEOHER-006   p. 74
He ordered his men to get the rope cut. The dancer, who knew the spells, tied their weapons, and the string could not be cut. A Harijan, seeing the situation, offered the king to cut the string. He was successful, and a dancer fell nearby. The dancer later came to be known as Berni Mata, and a reclining sculpture is found on the embankment of the lake along with a temple. There is a temple, and people worship.
C09-GEOHER-007   p. 75
## कथा उदयपुर (Jagta Hua Kasba) — Hindi original (JAGTA-HI, 6)
उदयपुर की गलियों के कोनों, तिराहों, सड़कों और दीवारों पर हरी चादरों के टुकड़े हाल ही में डाले गए हैं। मासूम ग्रामवासी हिंदू किसी परमजने मौलवी के हवाले से यहां किसी पीर कोई अज्ञात (रहमतउल्ला अलैह) के प्रकट होने की ब्रेकिंग न्यूज हाथ जोड़कर थमा देते हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* At the corners, junctions, roads and walls of Udaypur's lanes, pieces of green sheets have recently been laid. Innocent Hindu villagers, citing some maulvi, reverently hand over the 'breaking news' of the appearance of some unknown pir (Rahmatullah Alaih).
C09-JAGTA-HI-001   p. 21   also Afterword
आठ हजार की आबादी में एक-डेढ़ हजार मुसलमान हैं, जो अपने मुस्लिम अतीत की कहानियों में किसी मस्जिद तक ले जाएंगे। लेकिन परमार राजा उदयादित्य के समय इनके पुरखे इसी नगर को आकार देने वाले लोग थे। कोई शिल्पी, कोई प्रहरी, कोई व्यापारी, कोई दरबारी, कोई पुरोहित। मूर्तियों के खंडन-मंडन की तरह पहचान भी खंडित की गई।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Of a population of eight thousand, one to one and a half thousand are Muslims. But in the time of Paramara king Udayaditya their ancestors were the people who shaped this town: some craftsman, guard, trader, courtier or priest. Identity was broken like the images.
C09-JAGTA-HI-002   p. 39   also Ch 12
मल्लूचंदजी ने बताया कि सिर्फ तीन ही जैन
जगता हुआ कस्बा
परिवार उदयपुर में बचे हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Malluchandji told us that only three Jain families are left in Udaipur.
C09-JAGTA-HI-004   p. 68
मंदिर के बाहर आते ही गली के बाईं तरफ राजा मियाँ का आशियाना है। और आगे कोई हमीद है। सामने कोई बशीर है। बीच में रमेश हैं। प्रवीण हैं। राजकुमार हैं। सबके महान पुरखों ने एक साथ ये हैरतअंगेज निर्माण कार्य हज़ार साल पहले ही कर दिखाए थे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Outside the temple on the left of the lane is Raja Miyan's home, further on a Hamid, opposite a Bashir, in between Ramesh, Praveen, Rajkumar. The great ancestors of all of them together performed these amazing constructions a thousand years ago.
C09-JAGTA-HI-005   p. 71
यह राज नर्तकी की मूर्ति मानी जाती है, जिसे बेड़नी माता भी कहा जाता है। सिंदूर पुत्री इस प्रतिमा के बारे में स्थानीय लोगों में एक प्रचलित कहानी यह है कि उसे जलाशय में बंधे एक पतले धागे पर नृत्य करना था। एक बार किसी ने वह धागा तोड़ दिया था और वह नर्तकी नृत्य पूरा नहीं कर सकी। गिरने से उसकी मृत्यु हो गई। वह उसी की समाधि है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* It is considered the image of a royal dancer, also called Bedni Mata. A popular story among locals about this sindoor-smeared image is that she was to dance on a thin thread tied across the reservoir. Once someone broke the thread and the dancer could not finish her dance. She died from the fall. This is her samadhi.
C09-JAGTA-HI-008   p. 111
प्रवीण शर्मा अंदर से एक पुराने वाद्य यंत्र के टुकड़े को उठाकर लाए। वह लकड़ी का बना हुआ किसी सितार या सारंगी का ऊपर का हिस्सा था, जो टीले के अंदर दफन कमरे के कबाड़ में बचा रह गया था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Praveen Sharma brought from inside a piece of an old musical instrument. It was the wooden top of a sitar or sarangi that had survived in the junk of the room buried in the mound.
C09-JAGTA-HI-014   p. 69
## Madhya Pradesh District Gazetteers: Vidisha — Government of Madhya Pradesh (GAZ, 4)
Amongst the important religious fairs, 15 days' Sankrant fair in January at Rajghat Barat (Basoda Tahsil), 15 days' Ramlila Pilgrim Centres fair in December/January at Basoda Mandi (Basoda Tahsil), and Yatras 12 days' Ramlila fair in January at Gyaraspur (Vidisha Tahsil) 9 days' Navratri fair in March/April at Ghatwar (Sironj) and 7 days' Shivaratri fair in February/March at Udaipur may be mentioned.
C09-GAZ-008   p. 89   also Ch 8
In Vidisha District 23 fairs are held, the most important being at Vidisha (Ramhila Mela), Lateri (cattle-fair), Ganj Basoda (cattle fair), Shivratri Mahadeo Mela at village Udaipur, Ramnavami Mela at village Udaigiri and Ramlila Mela at Gyaraspur.
C09-GAZ-009   p. 161   also Ch 8
The Garuda--dhwaja or Kham Baba and the ground around it were given in inam by Gwalior.Darbar to a,priest of the Shaiva sect, with whom originated the worship of the deified column called Kham Baba, a favourite deity of the Bhois or Dhimars. The column was besmeared by devotees with the paint formed of red-lead and oil. The priest of the deity is known as Babaji and he has his house near the pillar.
C09-GAZ-011   p. 329
The Pisanari-ka mandir is a remnant of an old temple traditionally said to have been built by a woman, out of her earnings obtained by grinding flour for the workmen employed on the construction of Udayeshwara temple. But from architectural point this building is of much later date.
C09-GAZ-012   p. 348   also Ch 8, Ch 5
## History of the Paramāra Dynasty — Ganguly, D. C. (GANGULY, 4)
The caste-system was at the basis of the Hindu social structure. The Paramāra Kings lent their powerful support to the maintenance of its integrity. Udayāditya and Naravarman declared that their swords were ever ready to protect 'varna' (caste).
C09-GANGULY-002   p. 258   also Ch 4
With this object in view he set off from his capital, halting on his way at the present site of Udayapur at that time only a tiny village. Here he was sleeping in his tent, watched over by the queen, when another snake, which was guarding a treasure in a hole under a tree near by, peeped out to see what was happening. The first snake saw this and thereupon addressed the second from the stomach of the king, warning it that it would at once die, if anyone were to pour oil down into its hole. The treasure-guardian grew very angry, and retorted that the other snake was in no very safe position itself, since if anyone administered to the king a dose of pepper, salt, and buttermilk, that would be sufficient to put an end to its life.
C09-GANGULY-003   p. 266
Tradition gives a legendary story in connection with the establishment of this city. It runs as follows:—
One day the king, in the course of a hunting excursion, came upon a snake surrounded by fire and writhing in agony. He rescued the reptile, which at once begged for a drink of water. This being unobtainable, the king on the earnest request of the snake, put its mouth into his own in order to afford it temporary relief from its pain. But the snake betrayed his trust and slipped down into his stomach, which so upset and distressed the king that he determined to pass the rest of his life in Benares.
C09-GANGULY-004   p. 266   also Ch 4
conversation, proceeded immediately to profit by the information she received, and administered the suggested dose to the king, whereupon he at once brought up the snake dead, as had been fortold; after which he proceeded to pour oil down the hole of the other snake, and having thus killed that also, took possession of the treasure. To commemorate this event, he built there a city and a temple and named them after himself.
C09-GANGULY-005   p. 267
## The Cultural Heritage of Madhya Bharat — Patil, D. R. (MADHYABHARAT, 1)
It should however be remembered that there are stories and dialogues current in the chronicles of the Muslim historians themselves, how, even the Muslim rulers hesitated to destroy these precious … works of art. The human in them could not fail to make them admire these beauties created by man though it was at last suppressed by the overawing spirit of their faith. It is to one of such interesting episodes that we owe, it is alleged, the survival of one of the most beautiful products of the architects of Madhya Bharat, e. g., the temple at Udaypur.
C09-MADHYABHARAT-002   p. 48–49   also Ch 10

## Some Paramāra Temples From Madhya Pradesh: A Case Study of Village Un — Swati Mondal Adhikari (PARTEMPLES, 1)
As a mark of his gratefulness, the king built ninetynine temples at the place. Since the number of temples built was one hundred less one (un), the place was named as Un\( ^{1} \). The currency of identical legends at Lalitpur and Udaypur of Madhya Pradesh makes it difficult to give any credence to such stories\( ^{2} \). As a matter of fact no historically reliable information is available regarding the concentration of large number of temples at Un.
C09-PARTEMPLES-001   p. 5   also Ch 1
# Chapter 10. Political Change and Economic Decline: Udaypur under Successive Regimes
## कथा उदयपुर (Jagta Hua Kasba) — Hindi original (JAGTA-HI, 26)
परमारों का वैभव जब खत्म हुआ तब उदयपुर भी यतीम हो गया। 11 वीं सदी में दिल्ली पर इस्लाम के अनुयायी तुर्कों के कब्जे के बाद की सदीयों में दूर तक हुए बदलावों की चपेट में उदयपुर भी आया। पुराने निर्माण कार्यों में नए चिन्ह प्रकट होने लगे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* When the Paramara splendour ended, Udaipur too became an orphan. In the centuries after the occupation of Delhi by the Turks, followers of Islam, Udaipur too was caught in the changes that spread far. New marks began to appear on the old constructions.
C10-JAGTA-HI-001   p. 12
इस्लामी हमलावरों से यह नगर अछूता नहीं रहा है। इसके सबूत मंदिर में ही दिखाई देते हैं, जहां मूर्तियों को तोड़ा गया है। यह आश्चर्य की बात है कि फिर भी यह मंदिर अपने मूल आकार में बचा रह गया और यह नगर भी।
*Gloss [my translation]:* This town has not been untouched by Islamic invaders. Evidence of this appears in the temple itself, where images have been broken. It is surprising that even so this temple remained in its original form, and this town too.
C10-JAGTA-HI-002   p. 19   also Ch 5
यह राजा उदयविजय के 258 साल बाद का प्रसंग है। यह लेख हमें एक ऐसी घटना का साफ इशारा करता है जब उदयपुर में तुगलक के लुटेरों की मौजूदगी हम देखते हैं। बेशक वे मंदिर में पूजा करने या शानदार मूर्तिकला का अवलोकन करने नहीं बल्कि शहर पर कब्जा जमाने और लूटपाट करने ही आए होंगे। इस समय तक परमारों का वैभव खत्म ही हो रहा था। शायद उदयपुर वह आखिरी शहर रहा होगा, जहां परमारों के राजवंश के ही लोग आखिरी तक मुकाबला करते रहे होंगे, क्योंकि धार और मांड में वे घेरकर मार डाले गए थे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* This is 258 years after King Udayavijay. The inscription points clearly to the presence of Tughlaq's raiders in Udaipur, who must have come to occupy and plunder the city. By then Paramara splendour was ending; Udaipur may have been the last city where Paramara kin kept resisting, since at Dhar and Mandu they were besieged and killed.
C10-JAGTA-HI-003   p. 25
मांड में अलाउद्दीन खिलजी के इशारे पर आए एनुलमुल्क मुल्तानी नाम के एक सरदार के हाथों आखिरी परमार राजा महलकदेव की मौत के बाद उनके राज्य के दूसरे समृद्ध कस्बों-शहरों तक दिल्ली पर कब्जित तुर्क मुस्लिम लुटेरों के जत्थे मंडराने लगे होंगे। उदयपुर के असल अभिभावक भी एक दिन या तो किसी युद्ध में मारे गए होंगे या किसी समझौते के तहत शहर छोड़कर चले गए होंगे या यहीं कहीं बस गए होंगे। उदयपुर यतीम की तरह अपने हिस्से के आने वाले और बुरे दिनों का इंतजार ही करने के लिए पीछे छूट गया होगा। यह निश्चित है कि परमारों के खानदान के बचे-खुचे लोग ही तब वहां सामंत रहे होंगे और उनकी अपनी ताकत रही होगी। तुगलक के लुटेरों का सीधा मुकाबला उनसे ही हुआ होगा। विदेशी हमलावर मुसलमानों द्वारा मंदिर को नुकसान पहुंचाने का उदयपुर में नामजद नजर आया यह पहला प्रमाण है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* After the last Paramara king Mahlakdeva died at Mandu at the hands of Ainul Mulk Multani sent by Alauddin Khilji, bands of raiders must have begun to hover over other prosperous towns. Udaipur's guardians must have died in battle, left, or settled nearby. It is certain that surviving Paramara kin were then the feudatories, who must have faced Tughlaq's raiders. This is the first named evidence in Udaipur of foreign Muslim invaders damaging the temple.
C10-JAGTA-HI-004   p. 25   also Ch 4
मी लॉर्ड, कानून की भाषा में हत्यारों का सीधा कोई सबूत नहीं है। एक शिलालेख से ही यह साबित नहीं हो जाता कि मुहम्मद तुगलक ने ऐसा किया या कराया होगा। मुमकिन है कि इसके पहले अलाउद्दीन खिलजी या शम्सुद्दीन इल्तुतमिश के भटके हुए लुटेरों ने कोई धावे बोले हों और यह भी नामुमकिन नहीं है कि कोई बाद में ऐसा करके चला गया हो!
*Gloss [my translation]:* In the language of law there is no direct proof against the killers. One inscription alone does not prove Muhammad Tughlaq did or had this done. It is possible that stray raiders of Alauddin Khilji or Shamsuddin Iltutmish attacked earlier, and equally possible that someone did so later.
C10-JAGTA-HI-005   p. 27
यहां तुगलक का शिलालेख गवाह है कि मंदिर को लूटा गया होगा और तोड़ने के लिए लोगों ने चारों तरफ से घेरकर रखा होगा! साफ शब्दों में यह इस बस्ती का इस्लाम के साथ पहला परिचय रहा था, जिसके बाद बलपूर्वक केवल स्मारकों की ही पहचानें नहीं बदली गईं, लोगों से उनके पुरखों की पहचान भी छीनने का आतंक आरंभ हुआ!
*Gloss [my translation]:* Here Tughlaq's inscription is witness that the temple must have been looted and surrounded to be broken. Plainly this was the settlement's first introduction to Islam.
C10-JAGTA-HI-006   p. 27
कई लोग अपनी प्राण रक्षा के लिए ऊपर पहाड़ पर छिप गए। आक्रमणकारियों के समूह कुछ अजीब से नारे लगाते हुए मंदिर के चारों तरफ के रास्तों पर भर गए। राज्यों के लिए ये संघर्ष नए नहीं थे। हमने समझा नगर के नए स्वामी आ गए हैं। अब यहां परमार नहीं रहेंगे। संभवतः कलचुरियों ने आधिपत्य कर लिया है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Many people hid on the hill to save their lives. Groups of invaders filled the roads around the temple shouting strange slogans. Such conflicts were not new for kingdoms. We thought the town's new masters had come: the Paramaras would no longer be here. Perhaps the Kalachuris have taken over.
C10-JAGTA-HI-007   p. 28
नारायण व्यास ने संभवतः उदयपुर को वीरों की भूमि इसलिए कहा कि उदयपुर के पुरखों ने मंदिर को अपनी पूरी ऊंचाई में इल्तुतमिश के सौ साल बाद आए तुगलक के वैसे ही लालची लुटेरों के हाथों नेस्तनाबूत होने से बचा लिया था। इस इलाके में ध्वस्त मंदिरों को देखें तो यह एक बड़ा चमत्कार ही है कि राजा उदयदित्य का बनवाया यह मंदिर काफी हद तक बचा रह गया।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Narayan Vyas perhaps called Udaipur a land of heroes because Udaipur's ancestors saved the temple in its full height from being razed by the equally greedy raiders of Tughlaq, who came a hundred years after Iltutmish. Looking at ruined temples in this region it is a great miracle that the temple built by Raja Udayaditya survived largely.
C10-JAGTA-HI-019   p. 106
इसका मतलब यह है कि मालवा के खिलजियों और शेरशाह सूरी के लंपट लुटेरों के मातहत आने के बावजूद बाद के दौर में मंदिर का बाल बांका नहीं हो पाया। वे मंदिर से दूर महलों के आसपास प्राचीन स्मारकों के मलबे से मस्जिदें बनाकर अपनी ताकत दिखाते रहे होंगे लेकिन परमारों के वंशजों या परमारों की प्रजा ने जरूर उदयपुर की रक्षा में कोई कसर नहीं छोड़ी होगी। उदयपुर वाकई वीरों की भूमि रही होगी, जिन्होंने उन जाहिल इस्लामी जत्थों के विकट हमलों से भी मंदिर को बचाया और वे बाद में भी बचाए रहे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* It means that despite coming under Malwa's Khiljis and Sher Shah Suri's raiders, the temple was not harmed in later periods. They must have shown power by building mosques from debris of ancient monuments near the palaces, away from the temple, but the descendants or subjects of the Paramaras must have left no stone unturned in defending Udaipur.
C10-JAGTA-HI-020   p. 107
लेकिन ताकतवर स्थानीय निवासियों ने मंदिर परिसर में कब्जा जमाने वालों को कड़ी टक्कर देकर ही निकाला होगा और यह संघर्ष सशस्त्र ही रहा होगा, जिसमें कई योद्धा मारे भी गए होंगे मगर आखिरकार वे कामयाब हो गए थे। यह घटना अवश्य ही घटी है। अन्यथा यह मंदिर बचना ही नहीं था। वे अधूरी दीवार छोड़कर भागे या मार डाले गए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Powerful local residents must have driven out those who occupied the temple complex by hard fighting, an armed struggle in which many warriors must have died but who finally succeeded. This event must have happened; otherwise the temple would not have survived. They fled leaving the wall unfinished or were killed.
C10-JAGTA-HI-021   p. 108
विदिशा पर दिल्ली के तुर्क कब्जेदार शम्सुद्दीन इल्लतुमिश के भीषण हमलों और खिलजी की लूट के दौरान बेहद बुरी खबरें उदयपुर को डराने के लिए जरूर ही आई होंगी लेकिन उदयपुर अछूता रहा। उन हमलों में निश्चित ही उदयपुर के योद्धा इन दोनों लुटेरों के जत्थों से परमारों की सेना के साथ टकराए होंगे और अपने नगर को बचाने में सफल हुए होंगे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* During Iltutmish's fierce attacks on Vidisha and Khilji's plunder, very bad news must have come to frighten Udaipur, but Udaipur remained untouched. Certainly in those attacks Udaipur's warriors must have clashed with the bands of these two raiders alongside the Paramara army and succeeded in saving their town.
C10-JAGTA-HI-022   p. 111
डॉ. उपाध्याय के इस कथन पर मेरा मानना है कि उदयपुर की हर खंडित प्रतिमा, वह हिंदू देवी-देवताओं की हों या तीर्थंकरों की, यह बताती हैं कि इस्लाम के जड़ बुद्धि अनुयायियों के घोड़ों की टापें उदयपुर ने भी सुनी थीं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* On Dr. Upadhyay's statement, my belief is that every broken image of Udaypur, whether of Hindu deities or Tirthankaras, shows that Udaypur too had heard the hoofbeats of the horses of the dull-minded followers of Islam.
C10-JAGTA-HI-024   p. 152
मोहम्मद तुगलक के सैकड़ों तुनकमिजाज, हदयहीन, विवेकहीन और बुद्धिहीन
अनुचरों के हथियार बोधे हो गए होंगे।
सिर्फ़िरे थे वे। देवालयों को तोड़कर खुदा का घर बसाना चाहते थे। हजारों लोगों के आराधना स्थल को तोड़कर अपनी इबादतगाह बनाने चले थे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The weapons of hundreds of Muhammad Tughlaq's irritable, heartless, senseless followers must have become blunt. They were mad. They wanted to break temples and settle the house of God. They set out to break the place of worship of thousands and make their own place of prayer.
C10-JAGTA-HI-028   p. 160–161
किसी कालखंड में कुछ आततायी यहां आए थे। किसी कलाधर्मत्मा के बनाए अभूत शिल्पों पर उन्होंने हथौड़े चलाए थे। आराध्यों पर छेनी प्रहार किए थे। वे विधर्मी नहीं थे, धर्म के संस्कार से रहित लोग थे। ऐसे ही लोगों को हमने बामियान के बुद्ध को नष्ट करते अपनी आंखों से देखा है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* At some period some tyrants came here. They struck hammers on the matchless sculptures made by some artist devoted to art. They struck chisels at the worshipped images. They were not of another faith but people devoid of religious culture. People like these we have seen with our own eyes destroying the Buddhas of Bamiyan.
C10-JAGTA-HI-029   p. 160
उनका तर्क था कि अगर जहांगीर या शाहजहां ने इन्हें यह जगह दी है और यह इनकी निजी संपत्ति मान ली जाती है तो इस दावे के अनुसार मौजूदा सरकार को वे 84 गांव भी इन्हें देने होंगे, जिनकी जमींदारी इनके अनुसार इन्हें मुगलों ने दी थी। किले पर अपनी निजी संपत्ति का ऐलान करने वाले साइनबोर्ड की सफाई में इनके पास जहांगीर-शाहजहां का यही किस्सा है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* His argument: if Jahangir or Shah Jahan gave them this place and it is deemed private property, then by that claim the government would have to give them the 84 villages whose zamindari they say the Mughals granted. For the signboard claiming private ownership of the fort, they have only this Jahangir-Shah Jahan story.
C10-JAGTA-HI-032   p. 32
कैमरामैन ने सवाल पूछा तो निजी संपत्ति के दावेदार एक साहब 2021 के जनवरी महीने से बिना रुके सीधे बादशाह जहांगीर के दरबार में जा पहुंचे और फरमाया कि जहांगीर ने इस महल की संगे-बुनियाद उदयपुर में रखी थी। शाहजहां के समय यह बनकर तैयार हुआ और काजी साहब के खानदान को दे दिया गया ताकि वे मस्जिद में नमाज पढ़ाते रहें। इसके साथ ही 84 गांवों की काजियात भी उन्हें दी गई थी।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The claimant said Jahangir laid the foundation stone of this palace in Udaipur. It was completed in Shah Jahan's time and given to the Qazi's family to lead prayers in the mosque. With it the qaziyat of 84 villages was also given to them.
C10-JAGTA-HI-033   p. 32
महाराज उदयादित्य का राजमहल भी आने वाली सदियों में लुटेरों के हवाले हो गया। काजी-वाजी के नाम से कुछ नई पहचानें उजागर हो गईं। उन्हें बादशाह जहांगीर के पीछे कुछ दिखाई नहीं देता। गोविंद हैरत में हैं कि पुरातत्व विभाग का एक कारिदा अहमद अली परमार राजा उदयादित्य के इस शहर में सिर्फ मुगलों को और मांडू के सुलतानों का अतिक्रमण योगदान के रूप में देखता है। उसकी नजर मुगलों के पीछे नहीं देखती, जब ये शहर बसाया गया, मंदिर और महल बनाए गए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Maharaj Udayaditya's palace too fell into the hands of plunderers in later centuries. New identities emerged under the name of Qazi. Govind is astonished that an archaeology department official, Ahmad Ali, sees in this Paramara town only the Mughals' and Mandu sultans' encroachment as contribution, not seeing beyond the Mughals to when the town was settled and the temple and palace built.
C10-JAGTA-HI-034   p. 44   also Ch 11
उदयपुर के भाग्यविधाता अब परमार नहीं रहे
थे। परमारों को इतिहास से विदा हुए तीन सौ साल से ज्यादा बीत चुके थे।
उदयपुर का संबंध अब चंदेरी से जुड़ गया।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Udaipur's fortune-makers were no longer the Paramaras. More than three hundred years had passed since the Paramaras departed from history. Udaipur's connection was now with Chanderi.
C10-JAGTA-HI-035   p. 47   also Ch 4
अब यह तो तय है कि महाराज उदयदित्य के करीब चार-पांच सदियों बाद

था, जो चंदेरी सूबे का एक हिस्सा था। इस फारसी फरमान में यही बताया गया है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* It is certain that four-five centuries after Maharaj Udayaditya Udaipur's existence continued but it had become a neglected faraway town, part of the Chanderi province. This is what the Persian farman says.
C10-JAGTA-HI-036   p. 47
इस दौर में परमारों का वैभवशाली उदयपुर एक दूरदराज कसबे के रूप में चंदेरी सूबे में ठहरा हुआ सा दिखलाई देता है, जहां इस फरमान के मुताबिक बाबर के पड़पोते जहांगीर के समय एक मस्जिद बनाई गई है। मस्जिद में नमाज करने वालों के लिए अब बाबर को फातिहा की दुआ के साथ याद रखे जाने का हुक्म होता है। इतना ही नहीं, मौजूदा जमाने के बादशाह के लिए भी दुआ का आदेश है। यानी एक साथ बाबर और शाहजहां के लिए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Paramara Udaipur appears as a remote town stuck in Chanderi province, where according to this farman a mosque was built in the time of Jahangir. Worshippers in the mosque are now ordered to remember Babur with a fatiha prayer. The present emperor too is to be prayed for, that is, Babur and Shah Jahan together.
C10-JAGTA-HI-037   p. 49   also Ch 7
उदयपुर के उस पहले शिलालेख पर अंकित साल 1080 के 448 साल बाद 1528 में फरौदस मकानी बाबर जिस चंदेरी को तबाह कर रहा है, उसी चंदेरी सरकार के इलाके में उदयपुर कस्बा अपने एक बुरे दौर में दाखिल हो गया, जिसके नए मालिक अब मुगलों के द्वारा नियुक्त होने वाले स्थानीय कब्जेदार थे। ये हो सकता है कि यह पहले से काबिज मुसलमानों ने आगरे में नए हुकूमत मुगलों को अपना समर्थन देकर उदयपुर के इलाके की कब्जेदारी अपने पास ही रखी हो और यह भी मुमकिन है कि चंदेरी पर अपना परचम फहराने के बाद कोई नया लुटेरा यहां आकर बैठा हो।
*Gloss [my translation]:* 448 years after the year 1080 on Udaipur's first inscription, in 1528, when Firdaus Makani Babur was destroying Chanderi, Udaipur entered a bad period within that Chanderi sarkar, its new masters being local occupiers appointed by the Mughals. It may be that Muslims already in possession backed the new Mughal rulers at Agra and kept possession, or a new raider sat here after raising his flag over Chanderi.
C10-JAGTA-HI-038   p. 51
विदिशा में राजनीति और प्रशासन की ऊंची कुर्सियों पर तीन या पांच सालों के लिए जमे रहने वाले अनगिनत महानुभाव वहां लगातार आते रहे लेकिन किसी को यह भनक तक नहीं थी कि महल के खंडहरों के पास कहीं मुगल हमलावर लुटेरों के सरदार बाबर के लिए बारंबार दुआएं भी हो रही हैं, जो मरणोपरांत बाइज्जत फिरोज़ मकानी हैं। उदयपुर में सबकी नज़रों के सामने महल के एक हिस्से में बेजा कब्जे का एक साइनबोर्ड मध्यप्रदेश के गठन के बाद से ही प्रशासनिक ओहदों पर आए अफसरों के दोनों गालों पर जोरदार तमाचे की तरह टंका रहा। वहां नाजायज कब्जा जमाने वालों की सल्तनत चलती रही, जो अब भी मुगलकाल में जी रहा था और अभी-अभी अपना सामान समेटकर उदयपुर की गलियों से निकला है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Countless officials came, but none had an inkling that near the palace ruins prayers were repeatedly being offered for Babur. In Udaipur, in everyone's sight, an illegal-occupation signboard on a part of the palace hung since the formation of Madhya Pradesh. The occupiers had just packed up and left.
C10-JAGTA-HI-039   p. 52   also Ch 11
भारत से मुगलों का बचा-खुचा काम तमाम हो गया था। लेकिन उदयपुर में मुगलों के नाम पर एक फारसी फरमान के सहारे टिका मुगलिया था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The last remnant of the Mughals in India was finished. But in Udaipur a Mughal remnant survived in the name of the Mughals on the strength of a Persian farman.
C10-JAGTA-HI-040   p. 55   also Ch 9
इस इलाके में शमसुद्दीन इल्लतुतमिश, अलाउद्दीन खिलजी, मोहम्मद तुगलक, शेर शाह सूरी के हमले, लूट और कब्जे क्रम से हैं। मुगलों के बाद नवाबों के नाम से नए कब्जेदारों की घातें हैं। बाबर का चंदेरी पर हमला 1528 में हुआ और तब तक दूर से चुपचाप भारत की तबाही देख रहा उदयपुर चंदेरी सूबे का हिस्सा हो गया।
*Gloss [my translation]:* In this area the attacks, plunder and occupation of Shamsuddin Iltutmish, Alauddin Khilji, Muhammad Tughlaq and Sher Shah Suri come in order. After the Mughals come new occupiers under the name of nawabs. Babur's attack on Chanderi was in 1528, and Udaipur, till then silently watching, became part of Chanderi suba.
C10-JAGTA-HI-041   p. 71
और ये वही मस्जिद है, जहां नमाज करने वालों को हजरते फिरोज़ मकानी जहीरुद्दीन मोहम्मद बाबर को फातिहा की दुआ के साथ याद करने का हुक्म एक फरमान में दर्ज था, जिसे महल पर अपनी मिल्लियत के सबूत के तौर पर पेश किया गया था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* And this is the same mosque where a decree recorded the order for those who offer namaz to remember Hazrat Firoz Makani Zahiruddin Muhammad Babur with the Fatiha prayer, which was presented as proof of ownership over the palace.
C10-JAGTA-HI-042   p. 102
उदयपुर का अर्थ भारत की उन आपबीतियों को भी सुनना है, जो एक लंबे काले कालखंड के रूप हम पर गुजरी हैं। आज उजाड़ दिखाई देने वाली इस बस्ती ने भारत के दामन में आए बेहतरीन उजाले देखे तो बेहिसाब अंधेरों की आहट से भी वह अछूता नहीं रहा।
उदयपुर भारत की अद्भ्य इच्छाशक्ति का धड़कता हुआ प्रमाण है। पुरुषार्थ और पराक्रमी पूर्वजों का साक्षात् प्रमाण। वह वाकई वीरों की भूमि रही होगी, जिसने सदियों तक सिर पर मंडराते रहे शत्रुओं से मुकाबला किया होगा और हर बार वे विजयी हुए होंगे वरना मध्यकाल के उन भीषण आघातों से उदयपुर बच नहीं सकता था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Udaypur means also listening to the troubles India underwent in a long dark period. This settlement, which looks desolate today, saw the best of lights and was not untouched by countless darknesses. It is a throbbing proof of India's indomitable will. It must have been a land of heroes who faced enemies for centuries, otherwise Udaypur could not have survived the terrible blows of the medieval period. What happened to the Vijay temple at Vidisha?
C10-JAGTA-HI-044   p. 123
## Madhya Pradesh District Gazetteers: Vidisha — Government of Madhya Pradesh (GAZ, 11)
In 1239 A. D. Ilutumish, during his invasion of Vidisha, demolished Vijaya temple. Raja Shivprasad “Sitare Hind”, a renowned literary figure of Hindi, gave a good description of this Mandir of Vidisha. Again in 1290 A. D., Alauddin Khalji captured Vidisha and destroyed a number of temples and idols.
C10-GAZ-013   p. 254
Muhammadan invaders mutilated the figure sculptures of the temples. Muhammad Tughluq, between A. D. 1336-38 damaged the attendant temples and erected a mosque at the back of the temple, using the material from the demolished temples. Two Persian inscriptions in the mosque record its construction by him.
C10-GAZ-014   p. 347   also Ch 7
During Akbar's reign, Vidisha was a part of the Jagir of Mirja Khan. In 1682, Aurangzeb converted Vijay Mandir and other temples into mosques and renamed the town as Alamgirpur after his own name, though it never came into general use.
C10-GAZ-021   p. 254
Work on this Shahi-masjid, according to the
Persian inscription in it was commenced during the reign of Jehangir and completed in that of Shahjahan in A. D. 1632.
C10-GAZ-023   p. 348   also Ch 7
In the 18th century, Bhilsa passed into the hands of the Marathas and remained with them since then. The early Maratha rule offers no definite evidences of an established system of education in the region. It can, however, be conjectured that the task of education was then entrusted to the religious institutions of the region.
C10-GAZ-037   p. 254
It enshrines a large lingam, covered with a brass sheet presented by a general of Mahadji Sindhia in 1775, as is recorded in a contemporary record.
C10-GAZ-038   p. 347   also Ch 5
In Gwalior State, Vidisha (Bhilsa) was a tahsil of Isagarh pargana. In 1904 Vidisha was raised to a District having two tahsils of Vidisha and Basoda till the formation of Madhya Bharat in 1948. The District was enlarged in 1949 by the merger of small States of Kurwai, Mohammadgarh and Pathari which formed Kurwai Tahsil. The Sironj sub-division with two tahsils of Sironj and Lateri, formerly in Kota district of Rajasthan State, and the small Pargana of Piklon consisting of 11 villages belonging to Bhopal State were added to the District in 1956 with the formation of new Madhya Pradesh. At the same time the town and the District were renamed as Vidisha.
C10-GAZ-039   p. 14
Vidisha (Bhilsa) was a part of Prant Malwa of the former Gwalior State. It was a part of Ujjain Division and then of Gwalior Division in former Madhya Bharat. Since the formation of Madhya Pradesh Vidisha remains a part of Bhopal Division. At present Vidisha District is divided into five tahsils, viz., Vidisha, Basoda, Kurwai, Sironj and Lateri.
C10-GAZ-040   p. 15
In the year 1905, the District was traversed by the midland section of the Great Indian Peninsula railway with stations at Bhilsa, Sumer, Gulabganj, Pabai-Basoda, Kulhar and Bareth.
By this time the only metalled roads were two short feeder roads from Bareth station to Udayapura (6.4 km.) and Kulhar station to Pathari near Baro (23 km.).
C10-GAZ-049   p. 170
Bhilsa zila of the Gwalior State was placed in charge of a Subah who was assisted by a Kamasdar in each pargana. The Subah was the zila magistrate and the chief revenue officer. The Kamasdars were subordinate magistrates (class II) who also acted as nunsifs. The chief civil judicial authority was the sadr amin in the zila. The police force was headed by a Superintendent.
C10-GAZ-050   p. 198
Udayapur, once a place of considerable importance is a large village in Basoda Tahsil.
C10-GAZ-056   p. 346
## Jagta Hua Kasba / कथा उदयपुर — English translation (JAGTA-EN, 10)
This town has not remained untouched by the Islamic invaders. The proof of this is visible in the temple itself, where the images have been broken. It is a matter of astonishment that even so this temple survived in its original form, and this town too.
C10-JAGTA-EN-002   ≈ p. 19 (Hindi original)
This is the first named evidence, seen in Udaipur, of damage to a temple by foreign Muslim invaders — although this was not the first Islamic terrorist attack in this region.
First, in 1235, Iltutmish, and after him, in 1291, the plundering armies of Alauddin Khilji had already made bloody pounces in this region. Those who need to know the truth of these attacks must certainly read what Minhaj Siraj and Ziauddin Barani wrote, who were writing the reality of these attacks at that very time. Both attacks were directly on Vidisha, which I will mention in the pages ahead of this book, in the heritage walk of Vidisha. But then Udaipur was, for some reason, spared.
C10-JAGTA-EN-003   ≈ p. 25–26 (Hindi original)
When the cameraman asked a question, one gentleman, the claimant of the private property, went straight — without stopping — from the month of January 2021 to the court of Emperor Jahangir, and pronounced that Jahangir had laid the foundation stone (*sang-e-buniyad*) of this palace in Udaipur. In Shah Jahan's time it was completed, and given to the family of the Qazi Sahib so that they might keep leading prayers (*namaz*) in the mosque. Along with this, the qaziyat (Qazi's office) of 84 villages too had been given to them.
C10-JAGTA-EN-014   ≈ p. 32 (Hindi original)   also Ch 7
To prove the encroachment on the palace legal, a Hindi translation of a Persian text was presented in the tehsil. It is some *farman* [of the period] after Emperor Jahangir; the headline above [reads] — "Documents of the Jama Masjid and Mahal Qubba Udaipura."
C10-JAGTA-EN-015   ≈ p. 47 (Hindi original)   also Ch 7
Now this much is settled — that about four or five centuries after Maharaj Udayaditya, Udaipur's existence remained just as established, but it had become a neglected town somewhere far off, which was a part of the Chanderi suba. This is exactly what is stated in this Persian *farman*. Now, in this era, Chanderi was a "garden" (*chaman*), where the representatives of the Muslims who had already seized Delhi had been installed in occupation. The systems of the old Hindu dynasties, in a creaking condition, had already come into their grip.
Udaipur's fate-makers were now no longer the Paramaras. More than three hundred years had already passed since the Paramaras departed from history.
Udaipur's connection now became joined to Chanderi.
C10-JAGTA-EN-016   ≈ p. 48–49 (Hindi original)
[In] the court of the slave of God, refuge of majesty, Shahabuddin Mohammad Karan Sani [Shah Jahan], the Badshah and Ghazi, who has offered *namaz* in this mosque.
Now consider the next line — "Whatever person offers *namaz* in this Jama Masjid, let him remember Hazrat Firdaus-Makani [the posthumous title of Babur] with the Fatiha, and keep the rule of the present-day emperor in remembrance in prayers of welfare. Dated the 10th of Zilhijja...
1041 Hijri Qudsi."
C10-JAGTA-EN-017   ≈ p. 48 (Hindi original)   also Ch 6
In the time of Emperor Jahangir, Auliya Ibn Abdul Samad had laid the foundation of this mosque and the Mahal Qubba of Udaipur — Sarkar Chanderi, Suba Malwa, the border of Gondwana. The construction of the palace and mosque had been only half completed when the emperor passed away.
C10-JAGTA-EN-018   ≈ p. 48 (Hindi original)   also Ch 7, Ch 6
448 years after the year 1080 inscribed on that first inscription of Udaipur, in 1528, the Chanderi that Firdaus-Makani Babur is devastating — in the area of that same Chanderi Sarkar, the town of Udaipur entered one of its bad periods, whose new masters were now the local occupiers appointed by the Mughals. It is possible that the already-occupying Muslims, giving their support to the new Mughal rule in Agra, kept the occupation of the Udaipur area with themselves; and it is also possible that, after hoisting his banner on Chanderi, some new plunderer came and sat here.
C10-JAGTA-EN-019   ≈ p. 52 (Hindi original)
The qaziyat of 84 villages was disposed of in one stroke.
C10-JAGTA-EN-020   ≈ p. 54 (Hindi original)
For these 75 years after freedom, the Qazi Sahib had been in the intoxication of the qaziyat of 84 villages — received in Mughal times, from the ruins of the palace — while acting upon the decree of Emperor Jahangir. But before this government order printed on a single paper, in accordance with the new laws and rules, he had already handed over the keys of Udaipur Palace and gone.
C10-JAGTA-EN-021   ≈ p. 56 (Hindi original)
## The Udayeśvara Temple: Art, Architecture and Philosophy of the Śaiva Siddhānta — Anupa Pande (PANDE, 4)
This temple is one of the few in north India that has survived the onslaught of Islamic invasions and one of the best specimens of central Indian medieval architecture. According to local tradition, in 1325 CE, after the conquest of Udayapur, Sultan Muhammad Tughlaq had given orders to raze the temple. However, the temple was not razed and only a part of the Vedic-hall on the west was converted to a mosque, the mehrab of which can still be seen (pl. 128).
C10-PANDE-001   p. 91   also Ch 7
The legend regarding this masjid is that the Emperor Aurangzeb, on his return from the Dakhin, passed this place, and seeing this temple, ordered its demolition and the construction of a masjid with its materials. In obedience to his orders the work of demolition was begun by cutting off or otherwise mutilating the sculptured statues that adorn it, and at the present day most of the statues are so mutilated; but at night, the deity of the temple, Mahadeva, appeared to him in a dream, and ordered him on pain of death to desist from his work of demolition, and as an earnest of his threat he would, he said, cause his listener to fall ill. The next morning Aurangzeb found himself ill, and accordingly, afraid of further irritating the deity of the temple, he ordered the stoppage of the work of destruction; vast quantities of gunpowder that had been stored in the temple to blow it up, were taken out and the temple left unharmed, but a masjid was ordered to be erected at the back of the temple, which had been described above, and a mandate of Aurangzeb directed all those who came to pray in the masjid to enter the temple first and having done reverence there to Mahadeva, to enter the masjid.
Whatever the credit attaching to this legend, certain it is that at the present day the temple is frequented by both Hindus and Muhammadans, and the lingam within equally reverenced by both.
C10-PANDE-002   p. 90   also Ch 7, Ch 9
The different parts of the pīṭha are described in the text.¹²⁵ The Śivaliṅga on top is covered with a brass sheet or cover. This brass cover has a mukha (face) on it, and thus it is
fashioned like a mukhaliṅga. This was a form popular in ancient times. It seeks to represent both Śiva's iconic and aniconic forms together in a single image, the former represented by his face (one or more) and the latter by his phallic symbol. This brass cover was presented by Khanderao Appaji, a general of Mahadji Scindia, the local ruler, in
C10-PANDE-003   p. 85   also Ch 8, Ch 5
1775 CE.¹²⁶ A crude image of a mother goddess, of a much later date, may be seen in the rear wall of the sanctum.
C10-PANDE-004   p. 85
## History of the Paramāra Dynasty — Ganguly, D. C. (GANGULY, 3)
Immediately after the conquest of Udayapur, Sultan Muhammad Tughluq (A. D 1325) is said to have ordered this structure to be blown up. Gunpowder was heaped in and around the temple, but the priests, by secretly introducing streams of water upon them, rendered the ammunition ineffective. The Sultan took it for a miracle, and revoked his order. One of the halls, for reading Vedas, in the corner of the courtyard, was, on that occasion, converted into a mosque.
C10-GANGULY-006   p. 269   also Ch 7
inscription of the sixteenth century A. D. \( ^{1} \) describes it as the most beautiful temple in India. It is one of the few temples in Malwa which were spared complete demolition by the Muhammadan iconoclasts.
C10-GANGULY-007   p. 269   also Ch 5
Tradition tells us that \( ^{2} \) the Mughal emperor Aurangzeb, on his way back to Delhi from his Deccan wars, halted at Udayapur, and was amazed at the supreme beauty of the temple. He gave orders for its destruction, in order to build a mosque out of its materials. But Mahadeva, the deity of the temple, appeared to him in a dream and threatened him with the penalty of death should his orders be carried out. To enforce his threat, moreover, the god smote him with illness, after which the emperor, becoming alarmed, rescinded his order.
C10-GANGULY-009   p. 269
## The Cultural Heritage of Madhya Bharat — Patil, D. R. (MADHYABHARAT, 2)
The temple was roughly handled and its figure sculptures mutilated by Mohammedan invaders. A popular legend is current that Aurangzeb is responsible for the damage done to this temple but it seems more probable that this had already been done by Mohammad Tughlaq, Sultan of Delhi who built the mosque at the back of the temple with material of demolished shrines, between A. H. 737-39 (A.D. 1336-38) as known from the two Persian inscriptions related to this mosque.
C10-MADHYABHARAT-006   p. 117   also Ch 7, Ch 11
Udaypur (now a small village) was in the medieval period a place of considerable importance, as is evidenced by the existing remains of monuments both Hindu and Mohammedan.
C10-MADHYABHARAT-010   p. 115
## Art of the Paramāras of Malwa — Sharma, R. K. (ARTPAR, 2)
मुख्य मंदिर के गर्म-गृह में भग्रमूर्ति प्रथवा शिवलिंग की स्थापना है । वर्तमान में यह शिवलिंग की स्थापना है । पताल के खोल से ढंका हुभा है । इस पर खुदे हुये लेखों से बात होता है कि प्रठारह्यीं । (१७५४ द्वि) पताल के खोलमुखर महाराज सिंधिया के सेनापति खांद्ररात प्रत्याजी में ग्वालिंग सरकार के सैनापति खांद्ररात प्रत्याजी के सैनापति खांद्ररात के दोनों ओर पटे यह्छेलो खांद्ररात जाता है प्रभ्य दिनों केवल सिंधराशि पर्ब पटे यह्छेलो खांद्ररात के दोनों ओर सिंधराशि पर्ब पटे यह्छेलो खांद्ररात के दोनों ओर सिंধराशि पर्ब पटे यह्छेलो खांद्ररात के दोनों ओर सिंधराशி पर्ब पटे यह्छेलो खांद्ररात के दोनों ओर सिंधराशि पर्डे जये सिंधराशि पर्ठे जये सिंधराशि पर्डे जये सिंधराशि पर्डे जये सिंधरাশि पर्डे जये सिंधराशि पर्डे जये सिंधराशि पर्डे जयेसिंधराशि पर्डే जयेसिंधराशि पर्डे जयेसिंधराशि पर्डे जयेसिंधराशி पर्डे जयेसिंधराशि पर्डे जयेसिंधराशि पर्डे जयेसింధराशि पर्डे जया

नारायण व्यास, जिन्होंने आर्कियोलॉजिकल सर्वे ऑफ इंडिया (एसआई) में अपनी सेवाएं 1972 में शुरू की थीं और अपनी नौकरी की शुरुआत में सीधे उदयपुर भेजे गए थे। उन्हें अच्छी तरह याद है कि तब मंदिर के तीन तरफ लोगों ने बिल्कुल ही सटकर अपने मकान बना रखे थे। वे कई दशकों पुराने थे। हम सोच भी नहीं सकते कि यह किस हाल में कब से पड़ा रहा होगा। तब उन लोगों को मुआवजा देकर हटाया गया था। तीनों तरफ चार फुट मलबा हटाया तो मंदिर की शानदार बुनियाद बाहर निकली थी, जो आज भी देखी जा सकती है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Dr. Narayan Vyas, who began his service in the Archaeological Survey of India in 1972 and was sent straight to Udaypur at the start of his job. He remembers well that then people had built their houses completely adjoining the temple on three sides. They were decades old. Then those people were removed after being given compensation. When four feet of debris was removed on all three sides, the splendid foundation of the temple came out, which can be seen even today.
C11-JAGTA-HI-012   p. 98   also Ch 7
जब वे उदयपुर पहुंचे तो खुलासा किया कि 1972 में नीलकंठेश्वर भगवान ने सबसे पहले अपने आसपास की सफाई के लिए ही बुलाया था। उदयपुर में अब वे दूसरी बड़ी सफाई देखने आए थे। आजादी के बाद एसएसआई द्वारा मंदिर को अपनी पनाह में लेने के बाद वह पहला बड़ा काम था, जो दिखाई दिया। लेकिन उसके बाद इतने सालों में उदयपुर की सुध किसी ने नहीं ली थी।
*Gloss [my translation]:* When he reached Udaypur he revealed that in 1972 Lord Nilakantheshwar had first called him for cleaning of his surroundings. Now he had come to see another big cleaning in Udaypur. After the ASI took the temple under its shelter after independence, that was the first major work that was visible. But after that, in so many years, no one took care of Udaypur.
C11-JAGTA-HI-013   p. 99
बजट की फाइलें देखने वाले एक अधिकारी ने मुझे बताया कि एक करोड़ 10 लाख की रकम में से सबसे ज्यादा 67 लाख रूपए तो एक मस्जिद पर ही उदारतापूर्वक दिल और जेबें खोलकर खर्च किए गए, जबकि हजार साल पुराने महल बरबाद होने के लिए बिल्कुल ही लावारिस छोड़ दिए गए थे। कई टूटे हुए मंदिरों के अवशेष उदयपुर के आसपास बेहिसाब फैले हुए थे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* An officer who looked at the budget files told me that out of one crore ten lakh, the most, 67 lakh rupees, was spent on one mosque alone, while thousand-year-old palaces were left completely abandoned to ruin. Remains of many broken temples were lying scattered around Udaypur beyond count.
C11-JAGTA-HI-014   p. 101
एक दिन उदयपुर में जारी हमारे इन मामूली से प्रयासों की खबर इंडियन नेशनल ट्रस्ट फॉर आर्ट एंड कल्चरल हेरिटेज (INTACH) को लगी। यह पुरातात्विक स्मारकों को सहजने वाला देश का एक सक्षम गैर सरकारी संगठन है, जो इंटेक के नाम से जाना जाता है। इसका हिंदी अनुवाद "भारतीय सांस्कृतिक निधि" है। इसके भोपाल कन्वीनर हैं डॉ. मदनमोहन उपाध्याय। वे मध्यप्रदेश में एक चिरपरिचित नाम हैं। भारतीय प्रशासनिक सेवा के अधिकारी रहे हैं। प्रमुख सचिव के पद से रिटायर होने के बाद अटलबिहारी वाजपेयी सुशासन संस्थान में हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* One day news of our modest efforts in Udaipur reached INTACH, a capable non-governmental organisation that preserves archaeological monuments. Its Bhopal convener is Dr Madanmohan Upadhyay, a retired IAS officer.
C11-JAGTA-HI-015   p. 75   also Afterword
भोपाल लौटकर इंटैक की टीम ने अपने फील्ड वर्क की कार्ययोजना बनाई। वो तीन दिन के अंदर ही उनका संदेश आया कि काम पूरा होने तक टीम उदयपुर में ही कैंप करेगी। तकनीकी रूप से दक्ष टीम के चार सदस्य उदयपुर रवाना हो चुके थे। प्रशासन को इनके काम और इनके काम में स्थानीय रेवेन्यू अफसरों की जरूरत के बारे में बताया गया। उदयपुर में डेरा डालते ही टीम के सदस्य अपने काम में जुट गए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Returning to Bhopal the INTACH team made a field work plan. Within three days came the message that the team would camp in Udaipur until the work was done. Four technically skilled members had left for Udaipur. The administration was told about their work and the need for local revenue officers. As soon as they camped in Udaipur the team set to work.
C11-JAGTA-HI-016   p. 87   also Afterword
इंडियन नेशनल ट्रस्ट फॉर आर्ट एंड कल्चरल हेरिटेज (INTACH) के कन्वीनर डॉ. मदनमोहन उपाध्याय ने अपनी तकनीकी टीम के साथ उदयपुर का बारीकी से मुआयना किया है। उनकी टीम एक लंबा कैंप करने वाली है। यह उदयपुर के लिए कई दर्शकों बाद मिला एक शुभ समाचार है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Dr. Madanmohan Upadhyay, convener of the Indian National Trust for Art and Cultural Heritage (INTACH), has closely inspected Udaypur with his technical team. His team is going to hold a long camp. This is good news for Udaypur after several decades.
C11-JAGTA-HI-017   p. 97   also Afterword
नारायण व्यास उस दिन महल के खंडहर भी देखने साथ गए, जहां ईटैक की टीम के चार सदस्य नीचे किसी अंधेरे तहखाने की नापजोख कर रहे थे। उनका काम आखिरी चरण में था और मैं उनके अनुभव दर्ज करने के लिए उत्सावला था। हमने थोड़ी देर उनसे वहीं बात की। उन्हें दस दिन से ज्यादा हो गए थे और वे यहां आकर रोमांचित थे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Dr Narayan Vyas went along to see the palace ruins where four members of the INTACH team were measuring a dark cellar below. Their work was in its final phase. They had been there for over ten days and were thrilled to be here.
C11-JAGTA-HI-018   p. 109
उदयपुर में ही इटैक ने सौ से ज्यादा शिलालेखों का पता लगाया है। लेकिन न उदयपुर वालों को पता, न विदिशा वालों को।
*Gloss [my translation]:* In Udaypur itself INTACH has traced more than a hundred inscriptions. But neither the people of Udaypur know, nor the people of Vidisha.
C11-JAGTA-HI-019   p. 127   also Ch 6
हजार-बारह सौ सालों के
लंबे कालखंड में आए और गए हर शासक के समय के अवशेषों को पहचाना था,
शिलालेख गिने थे और इनका दस्तावेजीकरण किया था। एक साल बाद अब उदयपुर
पर केंद्रित इस सबसे ताजा शोधकार्य का प्रकाशन भी होने जा रहा है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* They had identified the remains from the time of every ruler who came and went over a long period of a thousand to twelve hundred years, counted the inscriptions and documented them. After a year the publication of this latest research centred on Udaypur is now also going to appear.
C11-JAGTA-HI-020   p. 151
उदयपुर में हमारे एकल प्रयास उनके संज्ञान
में आते ही उन्होंने इंटैक की एक विशेषज्ञ टीम को उदयपुर में तैनात किया था। इस
टीम ने पहली बार उदयपुर के चपे-चपे को खंगाला था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* As soon as our solitary efforts in Udaypur came to his notice, he deployed an expert team of INTACH in Udaypur. This team combed every inch of Udaypur for the first time.
C11-JAGTA-HI-021   p. 151
वह ढहने की कगार पर बिल्कुल ही लावारिस छोड़ दिया था। बचे अपनी बला से, गिरे अपनी बला से। सदियों बाद भी मलबे से भरे आंगन के भीतरी हिस्से में उसके कमरे और बरामदे काफी अच्छी हालत में थे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* It had been left completely abandoned on the verge of collapse. 'Let it survive on its own, let it fall on its own.' Even after centuries, inside the rubble-filled courtyard its rooms and verandas were in quite good condition.
C11-JAGTA-HI-022   p. 13   also Ch 7
यहां कई इमारतें, महल और मंदिर बचे हुए हैं, जो भारत के सदीयों पुराने स्थापत्य का एक शानदार परिचय दे रहे हैं। अगर इन्हें बचाया गया तो हमारी आंखों के सामने यह धूल में मिल रहा है। अब कोई विदेशी आक्रांता यह बरबादी नहीं कर रहा। हम इसके जिम्मेदार हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Many buildings, palaces and temples survive here, giving a splendid introduction to India's centuries-old architecture. If they are not saved, it is turning to dust before our eyes. Now no foreign invader is doing this destruction. We are responsible for it.
C11-JAGTA-HI-023   p. 18   also Ch 7
सुरेश मिश्र ने रेखांकित किया कि परमार वंश के महाराजा उदयादित्य के राजमहल की एक दीवार पर टंगा वह साइनबोर्ड नई मिल्कियत की घोषणा कर रहा है- 'निजी संपत्ति, उदयपुर पैलेस, वार्ड नंबर-14, खसरा नंबर-822, काजी सैयद इरफान अली, काजी सैयद महबूब अली, काजी सैयद अहमद अली।'
हम नहीं जानते थे कि ये साहेबान कौन हैं, कहां के हैं, कब से हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Dr. Suresh Mishra underlined that the signboard hung on a wall of the palace of Maharaja Udayaditya of the Paramara dynasty announces a new ownership: 'Private property, Udaypur Palace, Ward No.-14, Khasra No.-822, Qazi Syed Irfan Ali, Qazi Syed Mehboob Ali, Qazi Syed Ahmed Ali.' We did not know who these gentlemen were, where from, or since when.
C11-JAGTA-HI-024   p. 21–22   also Ch 12
उदयपुर के प्राचीन राजमहल के खंडहरों पर यही पाया गया।
दरअसल उदयपुर की सचित्र सच्चाई प्रधानमंत्री, केंद्रीय पर्यटन मंत्री और मुख्यमंत्री को भी ट्वीट की गई थी। पत्रिका अखबार ने मुहिम के रूप में अपनी जिम्मेदारी समझी और फौरन असर यह हुआ कि एक तहसीलदार को राजमहल के खंडहरों पर जाना पड़ गया है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* This is what was found at the ruins of Udaipur's ancient palace. The pictorial truth of Udaipur was tweeted to the Prime Minister, the Union tourism minister and the Chief Minister. The Patrika newspaper took up a campaign and a tehsildar had to visit the palace ruins.
C11-JAGTA-HI-025   p. 31   also Ch 7, Ch 12
यह कम बड़ी राहत की बात नहीं है कि सिर्फ दो हफ्ते में हुई बहुत तेजरफ्तार
कार्रवाई में आज उदयपुर के प्राचीन राजमहल के खंडहरों से गैरकानूनी कब्जा करने
वालों को हटा दिया गया है। हटा नहीं दिया गया है, कब्जेदार खुद ही अफसरों को
चाबी सौंपकर निकल लिए हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* It is no small relief that in a very fast action in just two weeks, illegal occupiers have been removed from the ruins of Udaipur's ancient palace. Rather, the occupiers themselves left, handing the keys to officials.
C11-JAGTA-HI-026   p. 45   also Ch 7
अब तक यहां जहांगीर का फरमान जारी था। मगर जब जागे तब सवेरा।
आइए हमारे समकालीन इतिहास के इस बेशकीमती दस्तावेज़ को जस का तस पढ़ें और पढ़कर आगे बढ़ें। अकेले इस एक कागज़ की बदौलत नायब तहसीलदार दौजीराम अहिरवार इतिहास में अमर चुके हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Until now Jahangir's farman was in force here. Let us read this priceless document of contemporary history as it is. Because of this one paper Naib Tehsildar Dauji Ram Ahirwar has become immortal in history.
C11-JAGTA-HI-027   p. 53   also Ch 10
एक नायब तहसीलदार की ये मज़ाल कि वह फिरोज़ मकानी के लिए दुआएँ पढ़ने वाले काजी साहब जो कि खुद को इस्लाम में ऊँचे दर्जे के माने जाने वाले सैयद भी लिखते हैं, उन्हें सरेआम बेदखली का हुक्म जारी करे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* How dare a naib tehsildar issue an open eviction order against the Qazi, who reads prayers for Firdaus Makani and also writes himself as Sayyid.

क्यों मंदिर के विशाल
परिसर में संस्कृत मूल के शिलालेखों का शुद्ध हिंदी में अनुवाद नहीं दर्शाया गया
है?
*Gloss [my translation]:* Why has no pure Hindi translation of the Sanskrit-origin inscriptions been displayed in the temple's vast complex?
C11-JAGTA-HI-029   p. 73
जो मूर्ति जिस जगह से गायब हुई थी, वह चार दिन पहले ही उन्होंने कहीं से लाकर रखवाई थी। वे इंटक की टीम को यही दिखाने पहुंचे थे कि विरासत किस मांग में लावारिस पड़ी है और कैसी-कैसी कलात्मक कृतियों को बचाने की जरूरत है। वे पहुंचे तो बड़े जोश में थे लेकिन उमा-महेश्वर वहां नजर नहीं आए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The idol that had disappeared from that place he had brought back from somewhere four days earlier. They had come to show the INTACH team how heritage lies orphaned. They arrived full of enthusiasm but Uma-Maheshwar were not to be seen there.
C11-JAGTA-HI-030   p. 89
लेकिन जो कुछ बीते ढाई महीने में सामने आया है, वह पुरातत्व विभाग के जड़ बुद्धि सरकारी कारिंदों के काम करने के ढंग-ढर्रे का भी पर्दाफाश करने वाला है। यहां इतिहास का खजाना खुला हुआ था और वे कागजों का पेट बजट से भर रहे थे। कुछ नया निकालना तो दूर पुराने को भी बचा पाने की नाकामी का कलंक उनके माथे पर है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* But whatever has come out in the last two and a half months is going to expose the working style of the dull-witted government functionaries of the Archaeology Department. C11-JAGTA-HI-031   p. 97
लेकिन सामने के दो मकान छूट गए थे। उनमें आज तक लोग मजे से रह रहे हैं। शायद उन्हें मुआवजा कम लगा हो। सौदा पटा न हो। इसलिए उन्होंने हटने से इन्कार कर दिया होगा। नीलकंठेश्वर महादेव मंदिर का यह अतिक्रमण नहीं हटा तो नहीं ही हटा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* But two houses in front were left. People are living in them comfortably to this day. Perhaps the compensation seemed less to them. The deal may not have been settled. So they must have refused to move. This encroachment on the Nilakantheshwar Mahadev temple was not removed, and has not been.
C11-JAGTA-HI-032   p. 98

*Gloss [my translation]:* The last sign of their existence too was being destroyed. This is the most painful picture of Udaipur's fresh ruin happening before the eyes of a government department that spent 76 lakh rupees on two mosques.

*Gloss [my translation]:* A madrasa came to occupy Udayaditya's thousand-year-old palace. Cultivation began on Udayasagar. Encroachment gripped the Nilakantheshwar temple.
C11-JAGTA-HI-036   p. 161   also Ch 7
- बहरहाल, इस गुमनाम से शहर के परमारकालीन मन्दिर, राजमहल व अन्य स्थानों पर पहचान मिटाने के चिह्नों को देखकर इतिहासकार मिश्र चकित थे। मन्दिर प्रांगण में एक अन्य धर्म के कथित आराधना स्थल की गवाही, राजमहल में मदरसा संचालित करने के साक्षात प्रमाण, हरी चारदों की
बाजीगरी आदि की बातें जब मीडिया में आईं तब प्रशासन सोते में जागा।
बीते बरस जब मैं यहाँ आया था तो विशाल मन्दिर का पहुँच मार्ग अतिक्रमण के चलते बेहद संकीर्ण था। वहाँ से मन्दिर की भव्यता नज़र तक नहीं आती थी।
*Gloss [my translation]:* However, seeing the marks of erasure of identity at the Paramara-period temple, palace and other places of this obscure town, historian Mishra was astonished. When the evidence of a so-called place of worship of another religion in the temple courtyard, direct proof of a madrasa running in the palace, and the trickery of the green cloths came in the media, the administration woke from sleep. Last year when I came here, the approach road to the huge temple was extremely narrow because of encroachment. From there the temple's grandeur was not even visible.
C11-JAGTA-HI-037   p. 164–165; also at p. 165   also Ch 7
सुरेश मिश्र और राज्य सूचना आयुक्त विजय मनोहर तिवारी इस इतिहास नगरी में हेरिटेज-वाक पर थे। वहाँ इस जोड़ी ने जो कुछ देखा, वह हैरत में डालने वाला था। इस दौरान उन्होंने पाया कि पुराने राजमहल के भग्नावशेष पर निजी सम्पत्ति की सूचना चस्पा थी।

*Gloss [my translation]:* At the beginning of this year the famous historian Dr. Suresh Mishra and state information commissioner Vijay Manohar Tiwari were on a heritage walk in this city of history. What this pair saw there was astonishing. During this they found that a notice of private property was pasted on the ruins of the old royal palace. Not only this, Dr. Mishra was also surprised to see an unusual greenery in places in the town. This greenery was not of the 'crop-green' kind but of green cloths with gold-trimmed borders spread over random stones among the ruins.

## Jagta Hua Kasba / कथा उदयपुर — English translation (JAGTA-EN, 34)
I feel that, after well over a century since Garde sahib, the archaeology officer of Gwalior's Scindia State, this was the first time that anyone had come to take heed of Udaipur at this level.
C11-JAGTA-EN-003   ≈ p. 87 (Hindi original)
It is not as though no one knew about this inscription before. It has been in the ASI's records for quite a long time.
C11-JAGTA-EN-004   ≈ p. 129 (Hindi original)
First of all, Major General Alexander Cunningham mentioned it in the *Archaeological Survey of India Report*, Volume 10, page 83. This was first printed as long ago as 1880. The famous historian Devadatta Ramakrishna Bhandarkar gave a description of this inscription on page 22 of the *Progress Report of the Archaeological Survey, Western Circle, 1913–14*. Apart from this, M.B. Garde mentions it in the *Annual Report of the Archaeological Department in Gwalior State, Samvat 1974*.
C11-JAGTA-EN-005   ≈ p. 130 (Hindi original)   also Ch 6
Here neither had the central government felt the need for protection, nor is it included even among the state government's protected monuments — this we came to know later in this campaign.
C11-JAGTA-EN-006   ≈ p. 14 (Hindi original)
Suresh Mishra fixed a meeting with the ASI's Regional Director Dr. P. K. Mishra, which had been pending for many days. He was continually on tour. Staying in Bhopal, he has the charge of three states.
C11-JAGTA-EN-008   ≈ p. 87 (Hindi original)
In Udaipur, the government of Madhya Pradesh spent one crore ten lakh rupees in the past five years. Among the four protected monuments, two mosques three hundred years old are also in the charge of the state government. Of the other two, one is Pisanhari's temple and [the other] the 27-foot-long image of Nataraja Shiva built on the rock of Ravan Toll. An officer who sees the budget files told me that, of the sum of one crore ten lakh, the most — 67 lakh rupees — were spent generously, opening heart and pockets, on one mosque alone, whereas the thousand-year-old palace was left completely ownerless, to be ruined.
C11-JAGTA-EN-009   ≈ p. 100 (Hindi original)
Perhaps this is the first time that anyone has knocked at this level, after the temple's maintenance done under Garde sahib's supervision in the time of the Scindia royal house.
C11-JAGTA-EN-010   ≈ p. 96 (Hindi original)
Narayan Vyas, who had begun his services in the Archaeological Survey of India (ASI) in 1972 and, at the start of his job, had been sent directly to Udaipur. He remembers well that then, on three sides of the temple, people had built their houses stuck completely [against it]. They were several decades old. We cannot even imagine in what condition it must have lain, and since when. Then those people were removed by being given compensation. When four feet of rubble was removed on the three sides, the temple's splendid foundation came out, which can be seen even today.
C11-JAGTA-EN-011   ≈ p. 98 (Hindi original)   also Ch 12
When he reached Udaipur, he revealed that in 1972 Neelkantheshwar Bhagwan had, first of all, called [him] just for the cleaning of his own surroundings. In Udaipur now, he had come to see the second big cleaning. After independence, after the ASI took the temple under its shelter, that was the first big work that was visible. But after that, in so many years, no one had taken heed of Udaipur.
C11-JAGTA-EN-012   ≈ p. 99 (Hindi original)
One day, the news of these ordinary efforts of ours going on in Udaipur reached the Indian National Trust for Art and Cultural Heritage (INTACH). It is a capable non-governmental organization of the country that looks after archaeological monuments, known by the name INTACH; its Hindi rendering is "Bharatiya Sanskritik Nidhi." Its Bhopal convener is Dr.
C11-JAGTA-EN-013   ≈ p. 74 (Hindi original)   also Afterword
Returning to Bhopal, INTACH's team made the work-plan of its fieldwork. Within three days their message came that the team would camp in Udaipur itself until the work was completed. Four members of the technically skilled team had left for Udaipur. The administration was told about their work, and about the need for local revenue officers in their work. As soon as they set up camp in Udaipur, the team members got busy in their work.
C11-JAGTA-EN-014   ≈ p. 87 (Hindi original)   also Afterword
The moment our solitary efforts in Udaipur came to his notice, he deployed an expert INTACH team in Udaipur. This team, for the first time, combed through every nook and corner of Udaipur. They went into every lane many times over, and went and peered into every ruin. They identified the remnants from the time of every ruler who came and went across the long span of a thousand to twelve hundred years, counted the inscriptions, and documented them. Now, a year later, the publication of this most recent research work — centred on Udaipur — is also about to take place.
C11-JAGTA-EN-015   ≈ p. 150 (Hindi original)   also Afterword
As soon as we turned left from one bastion (*burj*), we saw a second bastion in a straight line. In front of this bastion, Udaipur's present crowded settlement begins. But between these two bastions we saw something that was quite distinct and attention-grabbing. The old stone protective wall of the palace, about twenty feet high, between the two bastions, was broken in the middle. A long, high cement frame had been made. On it was fitted a big iron gate. A signboard on the wall was declaring it to be private property, on which, along with the khasra and ward numbers, some phone numbers too were written.
C11-JAGTA-EN-016   ≈ p. 14 (Hindi original)   also Ch 7
Suresh Mishra underlined that the signboard hung on one wall of the palace of Maharaja Udayaditya of the Paramara dynasty was declaring a new ownership — "Private Property, Udaipur Palace, Ward No. 14, Khasra No. 822, Qazi Syed Irfan Ali, Qazi Syed Mahboob Ali, Qazi Syed Ahmad Ali."
C11-JAGTA-EN-017   ≈ p. 22 (Hindi original)   also Ch 10
Within just four days, a Tehsildar from Ganj Basoda set foot in the lanes of Udaipur with police personnel. A cameraman too, catching wind of it from somewhere, tagged along behind them. The information that came was shocking. The administration came to know that no one lives inside the self-declared private property on the ruins of the Paramaras' ancient palace — there, a madrasa is run!
C11-JAGTA-EN-018   ≈ p. 31 (Hindi original)   also Ch 12
He is saying that yesterday someone had put up a board of private property on the gate, which has been removed. This palace, spread over three-and-a-half bigha, is, according to the records of the Revenue Department, no one's private property.
C11-JAGTA-EN-019   ≈ p. 32 (Hindi original)
Govind is amazed that a functionary of the archaeology department, Ahmad Ali, sees, in this city of the Paramara king Udayaditya, only the encroachment of the Mughals and the sultans of Mandu as a contribution. His eye does not look behind the Mughals — to when this city was settled, and the temples and palaces were built.
C11-JAGTA-EN-020   ≈ p. 45 (Hindi original)
It is no small matter of relief that, in the very rapid action carried out in just two weeks, the illegal encroachers have today been removed from the ruins of Udaipur's ancient palace. They have not [merely] been removed — the encroachers themselves, handing over the key to the officers, have made off.
C11-JAGTA-EN-021   ≈ p. 47 (Hindi original)
In a corner of the ruined palace of the Paramaras' architecture, some Qazi family had hung a signboard of private property. From the Patwari to the Tehsildar and from the SDM to the Collector, very many people came and went on the chairs, and that board kept challenging them for decades.
C11-JAGTA-EN-022   ≈ p. 47 (Hindi original)   also Ch 12
In Udaipur, before everyone's eyes, a signboard of illegal occupation in one part of the palace remained hung — like a resounding slap on both cheeks of the officers who came to the administrative posts ever since the formation of Madhya Pradesh. There the sultanate of the illegal occupiers kept running — which was even now living in the Mughal age, and has just now gathered up its belongings and left the lanes of Udaipur.
C11-JAGTA-EN-023   ≈ p. 53 (Hindi original)
To proceed from the tehsil office toward Udaipur, an order of eviction was prepared under Section 248 of the Indian Land Revenue Code. The name-and-address recorded on it is — Mohammad Qazi Syed Irfan Ali, son of Qazi Syed Mubarik Ali, resident of Udaipur, present address Bhopal.
C11-JAGTA-EN-024   ≈ p. 54 (Hindi original)
For making illegal occupation on government land and keeping the land in your possession, you are evicted from the said land.
Under Section 248(1), a fine of 5000 rupees is imposed. It is further ordered that, before one week from the date of issue of this order — that is, before 22.01.2021 — you remove your occupation from the said land.
C11-JAGTA-EN-025   ≈ p. 55 (Hindi original)
"It has been reported by the *halka patwari* that you have made illegal occupation on 20-by-40 square feet out of Khasra Number 822, area 0.795 hectare, of village Udaipur. This court, through a duly-[conducted] revenue case, issued you a show-cause notice in regard to the said act."
C11-JAGTA-EN-026   ≈ p. 55 (Hindi original)
Because of keeping the land in possession even after the date of eviction, there will be a liability upon you at the rate of 500 rupees per day from 23.01.2021.
3. If the occupation is not removed within the time-limit, then, under Section 248(2A), civil-jail proceedings will be proposed against you.
On this order of 15 January 2021 are affixed the signature and seal of the Naib Tehsildar — which I received a few days later.
C11-JAGTA-EN-027   ≈ p. 55–56 (Hindi original)
Some people believe that the ground-blasts done for the legal-and-illegal mining of stone have created an existential threat for the temple. The savage plunderers of stone are pocketing lakhs of rupees every day.
C11-JAGTA-EN-028   ≈ p. 85 (Hindi original)   also Ch 12
A splendid image of Uma-Maheshwar had gone missing. Who knows when, before this, a report of the theft of any image had been registered from Udaipur, and who had got it registered. Praveen-ji immediately sent us a splendid photograph of that image on WhatsApp. Such a scene, before INTACH's team, was enough to tell that idol-thieves too are hovering here. All around Udaipur, up to the surrounding villages, centuries-old images lie ownerless in the bushes, forests, stones, mounds, fields and hills. Who knows who took away what, when, and from where?
C11-JAGTA-EN-029   ≈ p. 88 (Hindi original)
We told him that it is the very result of the awareness suddenly arisen in the people of Udaipur, that one person is taking a team gone from Bhopal from place to place, giving them a whole day of his; and, in the process of saving the images lying here and there, he too is coming to know what kinds of astonishing deity-images lie scattered everywhere in the courtyard of Udaipur. Dr. Mishra immediately asked that an FIR of this be got registered.
C11-JAGTA-EN-030   ≈ p. 90 (Hindi original)
I did not take lightly the theft of a splendid image from Udaipur that very day, and the local police's such disappointing and immature investigation. Immediately, this important and sad information was given at the DGP's office, and before night fell, that gentleman sat in the police station, on his changed frequency, to properly register the image-theft report in the daily register (*rojnamcha*).
C11-JAGTA-EN-031   ≈ p. 91 (Hindi original)
But the two houses in front were left out. In them, people are living comfortably to this day. Perhaps they found the compensation too little; the deal was not struck; so they must have refused to move. This encroachment of the Neelkantheshwar Mahadev temple, if it was not removed, was simply not removed.
C11-JAGTA-EN-033   ≈ p. 98 (Hindi original)
The ASI employees are completely failing to tell those who come to the temple about the four surrounding things of this city. Whatever their jobs may be, they could at least be made capable enough that they would give those who come for darshan at the temple the advice to go to Udaipur's four finest places — and, if not even that, then tell some facts about the temple for ten minutes.
C11-JAGTA-EN-034   ≈ p. 103 (Hindi original)
The meeting of the district archaeology association at the Vidisha district headquarters has kept ending, once in two or four years, as a comfortable session of laughter-and-jokes with the officers over tea and coffee. I learned that in Vidisha, the association's meeting was last held one-and-a-half years ago.
C11-JAGTA-EN-035   ≈ p. 104 (Hindi original)
This is the most painful picture of the fresh ruin of Udaipur, taking place before the eyes of the government department that spent 76 lakh rupees on two mosques.
C11-JAGTA-EN-036   ≈ p. 109 (Hindi original)
On Udaydit's thousand-year-old palace, a madrasa took possession. On the Udaysagar, cultivation began. The Nilkantheshvar temple was gripped by encroachment.
C11-JAGTA-EN-037   ≈ p. 161 (Hindi original); also at ≈ p. 161 (Hindi original)
