## Geo-Heritage of Udaypur, Vidisha District, Madhya Pradesh: The Third Eye (GEOHER, 14)
Nestled within the serene landscape, this village is home to the ASI-protected Neelkanthséhwar Temple, a testament to the town's historical significance.
C11-GEOHER-001   p. 7
The other important aspect is archaeological conservation. The geo hills site is very rich from the archaeological point of view and has a very large number of antiquities scattered from the Lower hill to the middle hill and to the top hill.
This documentation report can be shared with the department of archaeology to further work upon and come up with a plan for the proper presentation of these antiquities. Conservation of the site as a protected monument by a notification will be a very significant part of this because this is a continuous stretch track off 1400 m.
C11-GEOHER-002   p. 143
Already INTACH Bhopal chapter has initiated steps to conserve the entire complex by notifying this as an archaeological site of historical significance and national importance.
C11-GEOHER-003   p. 153
INTACH team had an opportunity to excavate and to observe places in the fortification wall where first-hand repair work of a collapsed section of pitching can be carried out by a team of expert masons under the supervision of Conservation Architects, Architects, and Engineers.
The method typically employed is to replace the original stone with a new stone that is strengthened with extensive use of focused placement. New stone coursing and profiles will need to be aligned with the original.
C11-GEOHER-004   p. 100
The journeys of discovery of the geo heritage in Udaypur began about 3 years ago. INTACH Bhopal was carrying out the documentation on the heritage sites in Udaypur village. It explored and identified about 50 heritage sites built more than 1000 years ago.
C11-GEOHER-005   p. 18
INTACH in the year 2021 carried out extensive documentation and published the book "Udaypur, the architectural splendor". this was referred as the base document. Other publications brought out by the district administration and other scholars were also referred to.
C11-GEOHER-006   p. 45
In fact, the extensive illegal sandstone mining in this area severely damages the natural landscape and ecosystem of this area apart from causing damage to the heritage in Udaypur.
C11-GEOHER-007   p. 14
The site is not protected and is near a privately owned stone quarry.
C11-GEOHER-008   p. 75
While the past few decades of continuous sandstone mining and the natural wear of materials due to lichen have certainly contributed to the physical deterioration of the Fortification wall, this process has been dramatically accelerated over the past decade. One of the main culprits has been the illegal mining of stone quarries along the walls and mountain bedding.
C11-GEOHER-009   p. 99   also Ch 7
Effects of the monsoon: Special precautions need to be taken to prepare for the upcoming monsoon. Work that has begun to clean the mori and drainage channels must be continued so that all outlets are opened to ensure maximum possible drainage. Interim interventions, utilizing measures such as shoring and netting to stabilize precarious structures, are imperative. If not acted upon promptly, there could again be a loss of human life due to local sources of tourism.
C11-GEOHER-010   p. 101   also Afterword
General Stability: The main objective is to provide general stability for all fortification structures in order to prevent further failures and to retard deterioration. Causes of problems must first be understood and treated before obvious effects are remedied. Not treating the causes can likely lead to the reappearance of problems, as is evident in a crack that has appeared in a recently repaired section of pitching at the southwest corner of the fortification wall.
C11-GEOHER-011   p. 103
This we can see in the pictures, at significant petroglyphs, the inscriptions, and sculptures are subject to vandalism. The current vandalism at Udaypur is of the type that people write their names over this ancient site. In earlier times, the sites were vandalized by way of mutilating or defacing the deities and sculptures. This can be seen both in the Jain caves and the other sculptures on the walls, even on the Lajja Gauri petroglyph.
The other form of vandalism is causing harm to the landscape by way of carving out new steps, concrete work has been done, and these concrete steps go inside the reserve forest and may later date pose a challenge to the natural ecosystem of the area.
C11-GEOHER-012   p. 145
These 8th, 9th, and 10th century CE sculptures have been vandalized, and the temples no longer exist on the ground from the top story to the middle story, and the bottom story vandalism can be extensively seen. This is a common problem across unprotected heritage sites that needs intervention at the community level.
Illegal mining is the most significant issue at this rich geo-site. The geo heritage site around the lower hill fortification hall is severely threatened by illegal sandstone mining carried on all over this area over the past many years. Though on record they no mine exists today, but occasional dynamite blasts can be heard. These dynamite blasts pose a threat not only to the protected Natraj sculpture but also to this ancient fortification wall.
C11-GEOHER-013   p. 145
and cattle, goat, and sheep come up to the top of the hill for grazing on a regular basis. This damages the young plants, apart from causing harm to the antiquities in this area. The illicit grazing also causes erosion of the land and degradation of the natural plant system of the area, degradation of the forest cover, or the tree cover.
C11-GEOHER-014   p. 146   also Ch 2

## A Serpentine Scimitar of Letters (the varṇanāgakṛpāṇikā inscription at Udaypur) — Singh, A. (SERPENT, 1)
It is in Nāgari script and is engraved on a rectangular slab of pinkish sandstone 70cm high. The slab is built into the outer parapet wall (prākāra) of the temple, where its quadrangular courtyard projects out to the south. This stone is evidently not in its original position, for its flat inscribed surface is turned inside out while its carved face with an ornamental balustrade (vedikā) is turned outside in, contrary to what we find in other well-preserved sections of the courtyard wall. It is likely that the stone was reset into its present position during one of the restoration campaigns at the temple complex carried out under the Gwalior State (1923-24, 5; 1925-26, 5-6; 1928-29, 5-6) and then under the Archaeological Survey

# Chapter 12. Udaypur Today: A Living Community and Its Historic Environment
## कथा उदयपुर (Jagta Hua Kasba) — Hindi original (JAGTA-HI, 38)
आठ हजार आबादी का हाशिए पर मौजूद एक ऐसा उजाड़ सा कस्बा जो बीते कई दशकों से कुछ कहना चाहता था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* A marginalised, desolate-looking town of eight thousand people, which for several decades had wanted to say something.
C12-JAGTA-HI-001   p. 2
मध्यप्रदेश की राजधानी भोपाल से डेढ़ सौ किलोमीटर के फासले पर यह उदयपुर है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* This Udaipur is at a distance of a hundred and fifty kilometres from Bhopal, the capital of Madhya Pradesh.
C12-JAGTA-HI-002   p. 2
मगर आजादी के बाद देश में पैदा हुई उम्मीदों से उदयपुर दशकों तक अछूता ही रहा। आजादी देश में और देश के लोगों के लिए, अगर किसी नई सुबह को लेकर आने वाली थी तो बाकी देश का पता नहीं, वह नई सुबह उदयपुर की ढह चुके दुर्ग की दीवारों तक कभी नहीं आई। वह उदासी से घिरा हुआ उजाड़ ही बना रहा। उसकी पुरातन भव्यता में झांकने की जरूरत किसी को महसूस नहीं हुई।
*Gloss [my translation]:* But Udaipur remained untouched for decades by the hopes born in the country after independence. If independence brought a new dawn, it never reached the walls of Udaipur's fallen fort. It remained a desolation wrapped in sadness; no one felt a need to look into its ancient grandeur.
C12-JAGTA-HI-003   p. 4   also Ch 11
धूल खाता हुआ एक बेतरतीब और बरबाद तिराहा, जिसके सामने की डामर रोड 16 किलोमीटर दूर जाकर गंज बौसादा से जोड़ती है। गंज बासीदा सबसे नजदीक रेलवे स्टेशन और छोटा कारोबारी शहर है। उदयपुर विदिशा जिले की इसी तहसील में आता है। तिराहे की दूसरी सड़क 90 डिग्री का मोड़ लेती हुई 22 किलोमीटर दूर मंडी बामौरा की तरफ चली जाती है, जो दूसरा रेलवे स्टेशन है और एक छोटा सा कारोबारी कस्बा है। इसी सड़क से मंडी बामौरा से सात किलोमीटर दूर कुरवाई है, जिसकी विधानसभा सीट के दायरे में उदयपुर आता है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* A dusty, ruined three-way junction whose tarmac road in front joins Ganj Basoda 16 km away. Ganj Basoda is the nearest railway station and a small trading town; Udaipur falls in this tehsil of Vidisha district. The other road turns 90 degrees and goes 22 km to Mandi Bamora, the second railway station. On this road, seven km from Mandi Bamora, is Kurwai, within whose assembly constituency Udaipur falls.
C12-JAGTA-HI-004   p. 7   also Ch 7
वह एक ग्राम पंचायत है। पत्थर की खदानों के इस मालामाल इलाके की यह हालत बिल्कुल नहीं होनी चाहिए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* It is a gram panchayat. This condition should not be at all in this area rich in stone quarries.
C12-JAGTA-HI-005   p. 9
सुरेश मिश्र जैसे इतिहास के जानकार के साथ हुई इस हेरिटेज वॉक के बारे में अपने तरोताजा अनुभवों पर मैंने लिखा कि आठ हजार आबादी की इस बस्ती में हजार-बारह सौ साल की इमारतें आज भी काफी हद तक बची हुई है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* I wrote that in this settlement of eight thousand people, buildings a thousand to twelve hundred years old survive to a considerable extent even today.
C12-JAGTA-HI-006   p. 18   also Ch 7
- लगभग समूचे उदयपुर की नींव में इतिहास दबा हुआ है। बहुत कुछ तो खण्डहरों में दिखता है, मगर बहुत से खण्डहरों को पक्के मकान में तब्दील किया जा चुका है। प्राचीन और अर्वाचीन के इन पैबन्दों को पहचानने के लिए किसी विशेषज्ञ की नज़र नहीं चाहिए। ये किसी भी सामान्य पर्यटक की आँख
भी पहचान सकती है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* History lies buried in the foundations of almost the whole of Udaypur. Much can be seen in the ruins, but many ruins have been converted into pucca houses. No expert's eye is needed to identify these patches of ancient and modern. The eye of any ordinary tourist can also identify them.
C12-JAGTA-HI-007   p. 163   also Ch 7
- विदिशा से करीब सत्तर किलोमीटर दूर उदयपुर को आज कृष्बा भी नहीं कहा जा सकता। एक सोया हुआ सा गाँव जो अपने अतीत से जागने की बात जोह रहा है... आज से एक हजार वर्ष पहले जब वह सुमावस्था में गया था, तब जिसका वैभव प्राचीन दशाणर्ण राज्य के मन्दिर-नगर का रहा होगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* About seventy km from Vidisha, Udaypur today cannot even be called a qasba. A sleeping village that waits to wake from its past... A thousand years ago when it went into slumber, its splendour must have been that of a temple-town of the ancient Dasharna kingdom.
C12-JAGTA-HI-008   p. 163   also Ch 7
ज्यादातर स्थानीय लोग अपनी रोजी-रोटी और खेती-बाड़ी के अलावा कुछ और सोचते ही नहीं हैं। वे लोग भी जिनकी रोजमर्रा की जरूरतें दूसरों से ज्यादा पूरी हो चुकी हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Most local people think of nothing beyond their daily bread and farming. Even those whose daily needs are better met than others.
C12-JAGTA-HI-009   p. 15
गोविंद सक्सेना की एक ग्राउंड रिपोर्ट ने बताया कि ढाई हजार हेक्टेयर से ज्यादा वन विभाग की जमीन पर नाजायज खदानें चल रही हैं, जिनमें से लाखों रुपए का पत्थर हर दिन निकाला जाता है। स्थानीय गांवों के लोग जिंदा रहने लायक दिहाड़ी पर मजदूरी के लिए लगाए जाते हैं। पत्थर के मालदार लुटेरे बेददी से खदानों में धमाके करते हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* A ground report by Govind Saxena said illegal mines are running on more than two and a half thousand hectares of forest department land, from which stone worth lakhs of rupees is extracted every day. People of local villages are put to labour at wages barely enough to live. The wealthy stone looters blast the mines ruthlessly.
C12-JAGTA-HI-011   p. 85   also Ch 2
जो लोग उदयपुर गए हैं या इस इलाके के बाशिंदे हैं, वे जानते हैं कि महाराणा उदयान्दित्य का बनवाया हुआ मंदिर पांच किलोमीटर दूर से दिखाई देने लगता है। जब आप मुख्य सड़क से उदयपुर की तरफ बढ़ते हैं तो मंदिर की भव्यता देखने लायक होती है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Those who have gone to Udaypur or live in this area know that the temple built by Maharana Udayaditya becomes visible from five kilometres away. As you proceed toward Udaypur from the main road, the temple's grandeur is worth seeing.
C12-JAGTA-HI-012   p. 23
आप आज भी देवी प्रतिमाओं के आभूषणों से मंडित वक्ष और स्तन कटे हुए देख सकते हैं। नाक और कान टूटे हुए दिखाई देंगे। चेहरों को विकृत ही पाएंगे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Even today you can see the breasts and chests of goddess images, adorned with ornaments, cut off; noses and ears broken; faces disfigured.
C12-JAGTA-HI-013   p. 27
कहानी है, जिसकी खुदाई में दशकों पहले शानदार कलाकृतियों वाला एक पुराना
आवास दफन था। श्रद्धा और परिश्रम से उसकी सफाई की गई थी। वही अब उनका
घर है, जो कथा उदयपुर का एक प्रकाशित पृष्ठ है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* An old dwelling with splendid artwork lay buried decades ago in the excavation. It was cleaned with devotion and effort. That is now their home, a lit page of the Udaipur tale.
C12-JAGTA-HI-014   p. 73
इस साल सबसे बड़ा बदलाव यह आया है कि गंजबासौदा के जागरूकजनों ने नए सिर से नई दृष्टि से अपने पड़ोस के इस उदास नगर को देखना शुरू किया है। उसका हालचाल पूछना शुरू किया है। युवाओं की एक टोली हर दूसरे-तीसरे दिन उदयपुर के आसपास भटक रही है। हर जगह उदयपुर अपने बारे में कुछ नई जानकारी दे रहा है। सामाजिक कार्यकर्ता प्रवीण शर्मा तो रोज ही कभी किसी खंडहर, कभी किसी पुराने मकान और कभी किसी झाड़-झंखाड़ में जा-जाकर उदयपुर की भूली-बिसरी कथा के पन्ने पलट रहे हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The biggest change this year is that aware people of Ganj Basoda have begun to look anew at this sad town in their neighbourhood. They have begun to enquire after its welfare. A group of youths wanders around Udaypur every second or third day. Everywhere Udaypur is giving some new information about itself. Social worker Praveen Sharma goes every day, sometimes to some ruin, sometimes some old house, sometimes some thicket, turning the pages of Udaypur's forgotten story.
C12-JAGTA-HI-015   p. 96   also Afterword
पिछले एक साल में उपाध्याय का कई बार उदयपुर जाना हुआ। वे मानते हैं कि स्थानीय लोगों की एक अलग दुनिया है और अपनी बेशकीमती विरासत के प्रति उनका जीवंत संबंध ऐसा नहीं रहा है कि वे अपनी ओर से कोई कारगर पहल करें। एक किस्म की उदासीनता से भरी दूरी ही रही है। जब तक वे इससे जुड़ाव महसूस नहीं करते तब तक केवल बाहरी प्रयासों या सरकारी कोशिशों से अपेक्षित नतीजे नहीं आ सकते।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Upadhyay visited Udaypur many times in the last year. He believes that local people have a separate world and their living connection with their priceless heritage has not been such that they take any effective initiative on their own. There has been a distance full of a kind of indifference. Until they feel attached to it, expected results cannot come from outside efforts or government efforts alone.
C12-JAGTA-HI-016   p. 152   also Afterword
उन्होंने इसे अंधेरी वीधियों से निकालने का निश्चय किया। इतिहास विशेषज्ञ डॉ. सुरेश मिश्र को लेकर गए और अतीत के घटनाक्रम के धागों को बुनकर समय के दुश्चक्र को बूढ़ा। कुछ उत्साही युवाओं की टोली संगठित हुई। उसके बाद से वे लोगों को इस धरोहर के प्रति सजग करने में जुटे हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* He resolved to bring it out of the dark lanes. He took the history expert Dr. Suresh Mishra and, weaving the threads of past events, searched the cruel cycle of time. A group of enthusiastic youths organised. Since then they have been busy making people aware of this heritage.
C12-JAGTA-HI-017   p. 161   also Afterword
मानों सदियों का खोया हुआ गौरव और वैभव लौटा लाने के लिए उदयपुर उठ खड़ा हुआ हो।
इस नवनिर्मित चौड़े, विस्तर्ण और जनसहयोग से बने रास्ते को देखकर लगता है कि जैसे बाहरी आक्रमणकारियों को दक्कन की राह का ऐसा ही प्रशस्त वैभव बार बार लोलुप बनाता था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* As if Udaypur had risen to bring back centuries of lost glory. Seeing this newly built wide, broad road built with public cooperation, it seems that such a broad splendid road to the Deccan again and again made outside invaders greedy.
C12-JAGTA-HI-018   p. 165
आजादी के इतने साल बाद तक किसी पंचायत के निर्वाचित प्रतिनिधियों को नहीं लगा कि यह कुछ ऐसा है, जिसकी बेहतर देखभाल की जानी चाहिए। सरकारी अमले में कोई ऐसा नहीं था, जो यहां के बारे में कागजों पर ही कोई प्रस्ताव बनाकर आगे बढ़ा देता। किसी स्कूल टीचर को भी कुछ दिखाई नहीं दिया, जिसकी बात आगे कहीं ले जाई जाती। नागरिकों में से किसी के मन में यह ख्याल नहीं आया कि वह उदयपुर की नष्ट होती संपदा के लिए कुछ छोटी-मोटी पहल ही करे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Even after so many years of independence, none of the elected panchayat representatives felt this was something to be better looked after. No one in officialdom drew up even a paper proposal and forwarded it. No school teacher saw anything whose word could be carried further. It did not occur to any citizen to take even a small initiative for Udaypur's perishing wealth.
C12-JAGTA-HI-019   p. 15   also Afterword
मैंने लिखा कि एक हज़ार साल पुराना राजमहल किसी की निजी संपत्ति कैसे हो सकता है? मगर ऐसा है। भोपाल से डेढ़ सौ किलोमीटर दूर विदिशा जिले के उदयपुर की तीन तस्वीरें इसकी गवाह हैं। जो कुछ उस साइन बोर्ड पर दर्ज था, उसे ही हेडलाइन बनाकर अपने फेसबुक पेज पर एक लेख लिखा, इनहीं तीनों तस्वीरों के साथ।
5 जनवरी 2021 की उस पोस्ट का शीर्षक था- "उदयपुर पैलेस, खसरा नंबर-822, निजी संपत्ति।"
*Gloss [my translation]:* I wrote: how can a thousand-year-old palace be someone's private property? But it is so. Three pictures of Udaypur in Vidisha district, 150 km from Bhopal, bear witness. Whatever was recorded on that signboard I made the headline of an article on my Facebook page, with the same three pictures. The title of that post of 5 January 2021 was 'Udaipur Palace, Khasra Number-822, Private Property.'
C12-JAGTA-HI-020   p. 17
यह एक अलग ही दुनिया है। अतीत से कटी हुई। वर्तमान से बेफिक्र। भविष्य की धुंधली कल्पनाएं भी नहीं हैं। वे हर छोटे बड़े काम के लिए सरकारों के भरोसे हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* This is an altogether different world. Cut off from the past. Careless of the present. Not even hazy imaginations of the future. They depend on governments for every small and big task.
C12-JAGTA-HI-021   p. 19
राजधानी भोपाल की नाक के नीचे सिर्फ़ डेढ़ सौ किलोमीटर दूर एक पूरा पुराना नगर अपने एक हजार साल पुराने वैभव में बचा हुआ जैसे दम तोड़ रहा हो। लेकिन ग्राम पंचायत से लेकर संसद तक की यात्रा करने वाले जनता के प्रतिनिधियों और प्रशासनिक पराक्रमियों ने अब तक इसे बेशर्मी से घूरे की तरह रखकर छोड़ा है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Only a hundred and fifty kilometres away, right under the nose of the capital Bhopal, an entire old town, preserved in its thousand-year-old splendour, seems to be gasping. But the people's representatives and administrative heroes, who journey from gram panchayat to Parliament, have shamelessly left it like a dung heap until now.
C12-JAGTA-HI-022   p. 21   also Afterword
यह गनीमत है कि उदयपुर का मंदिर इस हश्र को प्राप्त होने से बच गया। वह इस इलाके में प्राचीन भारतीय स्थापत्य का कोहिनूर है, जिसे घूरे पर डालकर रखा गया है और मुझे ताज्जुब है कि यहां आने वाले अफसर इसे ऐसा ही छोड़-छोड़कर जाते रहे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* It is fortunate that the Udaipur temple was spared such a fate. It is the Kohinoor of ancient Indian architecture in this region, yet it has been kept on a rubbish heap, and I am astonished that visiting officials keep leaving it that way.
C12-JAGTA-HI-023   p. 23   also Afterword
संकरी गली में दोनों तरफ दस-दस फुट सफाई की गई थी। जिनके मकान और दुकानों के अगले हिस्से हटाए गए थे वे नए सिरों से निर्माण शुरू करा रहे थे। लेकिन रास्ते में बाईं तरफ वह मकान बीच में सड़क पर निकला हुआ ही था, जिस पर हमने जामा मस्जिद का साइन बोर्ड टंगा देखा था। उसका दस फुट हिस्सा अकेला था, जिसे छुआ नहीं गया था।
*Gloss [my translation]:* In the narrow lane ten feet on each side had been cleared. Those whose house and shop fronts were removed were starting to rebuild. But on the left stood the house projecting into the road on which we had seen a Jama Masjid signboard. Its ten-foot portion alone had not been touched.
C12-JAGTA-HI-024   p. 67   also Ch 7, Ch 11
खुश होकर बताया कि उन्होंने अपनी ज्यादा जगह खाली की है। बिना किसी आदेश
के। उदयपुर की भलाई के लिए कमाया गया यह उनके हिस्से का सवाब है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Happily he told that he had vacated more of his space, without any order. This earned merit of his share is for Udaipur's good.
C12-JAGTA-HI-025   p. 73   also Afterword
जिन लोगों से भी हम अपनी यात्राओं में मिले थे, उनमें एक किस्म की जिज्ञासा जरूर जाग रही थी। वे इसे उदयपुर के ऐसे विकास की शुरुआत मान रहे थे, जब यहां बाहर के लोगों का आना-जाना बढ़ेगा। उनके कारोबार चमकेंगे। यानी कुलमिलाकर बात अपने फायदे की थी।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Everyone we met had a curiosity awakening. They saw it as the start of the kind of development when outsiders would come more often. Their business would shine. In short it was about their own benefit.
C12-JAGTA-HI-026   p. 88
बहुत ही कम लोग मंदिर के हैरतअंगेज स्थापत्य पर ध्यान देते। न के बराबर इसके असल इतिहास में झांकते। सारे ही मंदिर से बाहर आकर बिना समय गंवाए उदयपुर से बाहर हो जाते।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Very few people paid attention to the temple's astonishing architecture. Almost none looked into its real history. All came out of the temple and left Udaipur without wasting time.
C12-JAGTA-HI-027   p. 10
मंदिर के स्थापत्य को दूर से ही देखकर पहली बार उदयपुर आए डॉ. सुरेश मिश्र की आंखें चकाचौंध हो गईं। उन्हें यह अंदाजा भी नहीं था कि भोपाल के इतने पास चौबीस कैरेट का एक ऐतिहासिक स्थान इतनी भव्यता के साथ मौजूद है, जिसे स्थानीय बेपरवाही ने बिल्कुल खराब परिवेश के बीच उपेक्षित रख छोड़ा है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Seeing the temple's architecture from afar, Dr. Suresh Mishra, visiting Udaypur for the first time, was dazzled. He had no idea that a 24-carat historic place existed in such grandeur so near Bhopal, left neglected amid a very poor setting by local indifference.
C12-JAGTA-HI-028   p. 15
हर दिन दूर-दूर से लोग उदयपुर आते हैं। ज्यादातर सिर्फ 11 वीं सदी के मंदिर के दर्शन करने आते हैं और वे भी सिर्फ महादेव की पूजा-अर्चना के लिए। गर्भगृह से बाहर आकर वे हक्के-बक्के से गर्दन ऊंची करके मंदिर के स्थापत्य को देखते हैं। मोबाइल आजकल सबके पास हैं तो फोटो खींचना फूल चढ़ाने जैसी आवश्यक रस्म बन गई है ताकि सोशल मीडिया पर प्रसारित कर सकें। इक्का-दुक्का ही कोई पत्थरों पर उकेरे इस चमत्कार की डिटेल्स में जाना चाहता होगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Every day people come to Udaypur from far away. Most come only to see the 11th-century temple, and only to worship Mahadeva. Coming out of the sanctum they look up amazed at the temple's architecture. Mobiles are with everyone nowadays, so taking photos has become a necessary ritual like offering flowers, so that they can circulate on social media. Only a rare one would want to go into the details of this wonder carved on stone.

*Gloss [my translation]:* This is the only such place in the whole district that can be developed into a splendid tourist centre where people can spend a full day peering into their past.

*Gloss [my translation]:* Every SDM and tehsildar of Ganj Basoda wishes to bring the officer with family for darshan of Nilakantheshwar Mahadev. In this VIP darshan tradition the officer goes straight to the sanctum. Perhaps interest in history makes him pause briefly to view the temple's fine architecture. Hardly any collector notices the neglect around the temple. No one will have gone into Udaipur's lanes, where a thousand years of known history peeps from every ruin, building and doorway.
C12-JAGTA-HI-031   p. 34

*Gloss [my translation]:* Govind Saxena came to Udaipur one day by bike over 70 kilometres. वहां है।

*Gloss [my translation]:* From two kilometres earlier we saw a long throng advancing toward the temple. रावणटोल और पिसनहारी भी उदयपुर के दबे-कुचले अतीत का एक उपेक्षित हिस्सा भर हैं। बाहर से आए ज्यादातर लोगों को इनके बारे में कुछ भी बताने वाला तक कोई नहीं। एएसआई के कर्मचारी मंदिर में आने वालों को इस नगर की चार ओर चीजों के बारे में बताने में बिल्कुल ही नाकाम हैं। उनकी नौकरियां जो भी हों लेकिन उन्हें इस लायक तो बनाया ही जा सकता था कि वे मंदिर में दर्शन करने आने वालों को उदयपुर की चार बेहतरीन जगहों पर जाने की सलाह ही दे दें और इतना भी नहीं तो, मंदिर के बारे में दस मिनट कुछ तथ्य बता दें।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Ravanatol and Pisanhari are also only a neglected part of Udaypur's suppressed past. Most people coming from outside have no one to tell them anything about them. ASI employees are completely failing to tell visitors to the temple about the other things of this town. Whatever their jobs, they could at least have been made capable of advising visitors to the temple to visit the four best places of Udaypur, or at least telling ten minutes of facts about the temple.
C12-JAGTA-HI-036   p. 103   also Afterword
कहते हैं कि यह वाकई एक मिसाल बन सकता है। उदयपुर में यह क्षमता है कि उसे
अंतरराष्ट्रीय स्तर पर एक शानदार टूरिस्ट स्पॉट की तरह लाया जाए। एकमुश्त इतने

*Gloss [my translation]:* Dr. Upadhyay says that it can truly become an example. Udaypur has the capacity to be brought up as a splendid tourist spot on the international level. I have not seen so many diverse monuments together anywhere in the world.

*Gloss [my translation]:* The days of Udaypur (Vidisha) are about to turn. *Gloss [my translation]:* I feel Udaypur is going to get the importance of ancient heritage. Though it will not happen 'right now', certainly the history that slept for ten centuries is gently turning over. Even the residents of this sweet town, who have a little murmur of Udaypur's history but are unaware of its ancient glory, have now begun to gird themselves to become tourism attendants.

## Jagta Hua Kasba / कथा उदयपुर — English translation (JAGTA-EN, 19)
A ruined little town of eight thousand people, existing on the margins, which for several past decades had wanted to say something.
C12-JAGTA-EN-001   ≈ p. 2 (Hindi original)
Suresh Mishra and I got off at that corner which — hemmed in by lumps and shanties — was Udaipur's wretched little bus stand. A dust-eating, disorderly and ruined three-way junction, in front of which the tar road runs 16 kilometres away to join Ganj Basoda. Ganj Basoda is the nearest railway station and a small commercial town. Udaipur falls in this same tehsil of Vidisha district. The other road of the junction takes a 90-degree turn and goes off 22 kilometres away toward Mandi Bamora, which is another railway station and a small commercial town. On this same road, seven kilometres from Mandi Bamora, is Kurwai, in whose assembly-constituency Udaipur falls.
C12-JAGTA-EN-002   ≈ p. 7 (Hindi original)
The network of the powerful, greedy plunderers engaged in the illegal mining of sand and stone is strong everywhere. Wherever they are, they all believe in advancing together. The better coordination of the local agents of the political parties with the forest department, the police and the administration comes into use in the distribution of the spoils (*maal-e-ganimat*).
C12-JAGTA-EN-003   ≈ p. 85 (Hindi original)
During this fresh stir of Udaipur, Praveen Sharma had come to the fore among those aware people who had begun to spend some time among the temple and the images, taking it out of their farming work — peeking around the unknown places, and, if some useful stone or image appeared, taking its photo and getting it placed in the right place.
and sending it on social media, so that people would know what all is present here, and in what condition. The image that had gone missing from that spot, he had brought from somewhere and had placed there only four days earlier. He had reached [there] to show INTACH's team just this — in what condition the heritage lies ownerless, and what kinds of artistic works need to be saved.
C12-JAGTA-EN-004   ≈ p. 88 (Hindi original)
But from the hopes born in the country after Independence, Udaipur remained untouched for decades. If freedom was to bring some new dawn for the country and for the people of the country — leave the rest of the country aside — that new dawn never came up to the walls of Udaipur's collapsed fort. It remained a desolation encircled by gloom. No one felt the need to peer into its ancient magnificence.
C12-JAGTA-EN-005   ≈ p. 4 (Hindi original)   also Ch 11
For so many years after Independence, not a single elected representative of any panchayat felt that this was something that ought to be better looked after. In the government machinery there was no one who would even make a proposal on paper about this place and push it forward. To no schoolteacher either did anything appear that could be taken further somewhere. In none of the citizens' minds did the thought arise that he should take at least some small initiative for Udaipur's perishing wealth. It is possible that some correspondence too had taken place; but it had no effect here. Most of the local people think of nothing besides their own livelihood and farming — even those people whose everyday needs have been fulfilled more than others'.
C12-JAGTA-EN-006   ≈ p. 15 (Hindi original)
On the corners, three-way junctions, roads and walls of Udaipur's lanes, pieces of green cloth have recently been laid. Innocent Hindu villagers, on the authority of some outside maulvi, hand you, with folded hands, the breaking news of the appearance here of some pir, some unknown (*rahmatullah alaih*). I am no knower of astrology, but I can make a prophecy: in the coming years, gaudy mazars (tombs) will be built here, where, at the urs and mushairas of "communal unity," these same people's representatives will grace [the occasion] and these same greatly-glorious officers will busy themselves with their arrangements.
C12-JAGTA-EN-007   ≈ p. 21 (Hindi original)   also Ch 9
That narrow, six-to-seven-foot, suffocating lane leading up to the temple had been widened by ten feet each on both sides. The front portions of the houses and shops of the people on both sides had been removed.
C12-JAGTA-EN-008   ≈ p. 63 (Hindi original)   also Ch 11
In the narrow lane, ten feet each had been cleared on both sides. Those whose houses' and shops' front portions had been removed were getting fresh construction started. But on the way, on the left, that house was still jutting out into the middle of the road — the one on which we had seen the Jama Masjid signboard hung. Its ten-foot portion stood alone, which had not been touched.
C12-JAGTA-EN-009   ≈ p. 67 (Hindi original)   also Ch 11
After coming out of the temple, on the left was his very shop. He did not wait for any government order to remove the old construction at this place, and gave eight feet more space.
C12-JAGTA-EN-010   ≈ p. 68 (Hindi original)   also Ch 11
The 57-year-old Raja Mishra [apparently the same "Raja Miyan" mentioned earlier — the source spells it both ways] came forward and gave his share of eight feet more land to make the temple's road wider and better. Raja Mishra did not wait for the government machinery to come and give instruction or advice. The work that is necessary, he did not put off to tomorrow. His initiative opened the way for 40 businessmen to set back those shops and houses, built over several decades, by which a 30-foot-wide road had turned into a ten-foot-wide narrow and suffocating lane.
C12-JAGTA-EN-011   ≈ p. 70 (Hindi original)   also Ch 11
In all the people we met on our journeys, a kind of curiosity was certainly awakening. They were taking it as the beginning of such a development of Udaipur as would increase the coming and going of outsiders here; their businesses would shine. That is, on the whole, the matter was one of their own benefit.
C12-JAGTA-EN-012   ≈ p. 87 (Hindi original)
A band of a few enthusiastic youths got organized. Since then they have been engaged in making people alert toward this heritage.
Every month, taking along some presiding figure, connoisseur or curious soul, their band goes to Udaipur.
C12-JAGTA-EN-013   ≈ p. 161 (Hindi original)   also Afterword
- …And truly, some aware citizens sprang into action… the enthusiastic Praveen Sharma, Kaustubh Shirhonkar, Kamlesh Sharma, Gagan Dubey and Amit Shukla of Ganjbasoda;
- Raja Miyan of Udaipur;
- Ramakant Srivastava of Mandi Bamora… and many other people. On the initiative of all these, it was, first of all, the local people themselves who began removing the encroachments. After that, they took up the task of clearing the obstructed path leading to the temple. …And, in no time at all, in a small town, through public cooperation, a nearly three-foot-wide cemented road was built and made ready.
C12-JAGTA-EN-014   ≈ p. 165 (Hindi original)   also Ch 11
The interest of the Indian visitors was only in the temple — not even the temple, but in the Neelkantheshwar Mahadev seated in the sanctum of the temple. People would go straight in, perform worship and ablution (*abhishek*), and, hanging one more category of the poison of their endless desires around the neck of Bholenath [Shiva], come out — a son's job, a daughter's marriage, court matters, land disputes, the wish to become wealthy.
Ever since cameras became easy and ever since the mobile came into hand, people would stop outside for a while to take photos and the like. Very few people would pay attention to the astonishing architecture of the temple. Next to none would peer into its real history. All would come out of the temple and, without wasting time, get out of Udaipur.
C12-JAGTA-EN-015   ≈ p. 10 (Hindi original); also at ≈ p. 10 (Hindi original)   also Ch 8
Every day people come to Udaipur from far and wide. Most come only to have darshan of the 11th-century temple — and that too only for the worship of Mahadev. Coming out of the sanctum, they stare up dumbfounded, craning their necks, at the temple's architecture. Everyone has a mobile these days, so taking photos has become a necessary rite like offering flowers — so that they can broadcast it on social media. Only a rare one or two would want to go into the details of this miracle carved on stones — next to none.
C12-JAGTA-EN-016   ≈ p. 20 (Hindi original)
Govind's hard work appeared on *Patrika*'s portal within a few days. But it was a big thing that his video of Udaipur was watched by more than a lakh of viewers.
C12-JAGTA-EN-017   ≈ p. 46 (Hindi original)
More than a hundred people came into the hall of the Tribal Museum, available for only one-and-a-quarter hours.
C12-JAGTA-EN-018   ≈ p. 75 (Hindi original)
Udaipur is a sad little town at such a distance as one may set out from Bhopal in the morning and return by evening, where an effective echo of history seems to have come to rest.
C12-JAGTA-EN-019   ≈ p. 81 (Hindi original)
## Geo-Heritage of Udaypur, Vidisha District, Madhya Pradesh: The Third Eye (GEOHER, 5)
According to the 2011 census, the population of Udaypur is 6,383 people. Out of this, the male population is 3,405, and the female population is 2,978. The literacy rate of Udaypur village is 54.28%, out of which 60.70% of males and 46.94% of females are literate. There are about 1,247 houses in Udaypur village.
C12-GEOHER-001   p. 20
The cultural landscape of this area shows that it is largely an agrarian economy growing wheat, which caters to the big local Mandi at Ganj Basoda.
C12-GEOHER-002   p. 11   also Ch 8
There are many local villagers who go to the religious sites all over the place. So proper track needs to be defined for the purpose of the safety and security of all the pilgrims who go up to this site.
C12-GEOHER-003   p. 143
This is the expansion of the village population and the agricultural activity across the region. This is rapidly growing, and so is the population. The area adjacent to this Geo heritage site is either forest land or revenue land. As the intensity of agriculture increases, there is greater pressure on community resources, and an increase in the population of the village over the past years poses a threat to the natural terrain of the reserve forest area.
C12-GEOHER-004   p. 144
Along the trek route, adventurers are treated to glimpses of the hill fort's rugged beauty, with sharp chiseled marks and naturally coloured texture patterns of deposition and sedimentation adorning the sandstone cliffs.
As word spreads of the abandoned historical hill fort and the adventure-filled trek required to reach it, tourists from far and wide are drawn to the region, eager to experience the thrill of discovery for themselves. With proper infrastructure and conservation efforts in place, this site has the potential to become a premier geological and historical attraction showcasing the incredible diversity and beauty of the region's landscapes while providing a thrilling venture for all who dare to explore its hidden depths.
C12-GEOHER-005   p. 144   also Afterword
## Madhya Pradesh District Gazetteers: Vidisha — Government of Madhya Pradesh (GAZ, 4)
The primary schools at Shamshabad,
Udaipur and Gulabganj were converted into middle schools in 1950-51.
C12-GAZ-005   p. 270
In 1963-64, the middle schools at Udaipur and Gulabganj were converted into higher secondary schools and the number of these schools thus rose to 13 in the District.
C12-GAZ-006   p. 272
Its nearest railway station is Bareth, situated at a distance of about 6.5 km. to the west of Udayapur. The latter is connected by road with Bareth railway station on Bhopal-Bina main line of the Central Railway. The village is also connected by road with Basoda, lying to the south-west of Udayapur at a distance of about 13 km. It is about 55 km. to the north-east of Vidisha, the District headquarters.
C12-GAZ-007   p. 346
It covers an area of about 1,966 hectares. Its population was 928 in 1901 while it increased to 2025 in 1971 as against 1619 in 1901.
C12-GAZ-008   p. 348
## The Architectural Splendor of Udaypur — INTACH Bhopal (INTACH, 3)
Udaypur village is located in Basuda Tehsil of Vidisha district in Madhya Pradesh, India.
C12-INTACH-001   p. 12
"The total geographical area of the village is 1966 hectares. Udaypur has a total population of 6,383 people. There are about 1,247 houses in the Udaypur village."1 The total population of Udaypur village is 6,383 out of which 3,405 and 2,978 are male &
C12-INTACH-002   p. 36
The most interesting part of Udaypur Heritage is that the large number of them are still in relatively good condition which makes it a place for future Tourism Development of the other adjoining villages.
C12-INTACH-004   p. 3   also Afterword
## Art of the Paramāras of Malwa — Sharma, R. K. (ARTPAR, 1)
उदस्युट जो पर्बं माध्यमा में पर्यात् महत्वपूर्ण स्थान रहा है संदूल रेलवे की बीना और विविधा रेलवे स्टेशनों के बीच पड़ने वाली बरेठ स्टेशन से छः किलोमीटर दूर वस माग से जुड़ा हुआ है ।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Udaypur, which has held an important place in Mālwā, lies six kilometres from Bareth station on the Central Railway between Bina and Vidisha stations, and is connected by bus.
C12-ARTPAR-001   p. 60
## The Cultural Heritage of Madhya Bharat — Patil, D. R. (MADHYABHARAT, 1)
This town lies 4 miles by a metalled road to the east of the Bareth Station on the Central Railway between Bina and Bhilsa. It can also be visited from the next station Basoda, whence it is about 8 miles by a fair weather road.

# Afterword. Conserving Udaypur: Community, Heritage and the Future
## कथा उदयपुर (Jagta Hua Kasba) — Hindi original (JAGTA-HI, 30)
सुरेश विश्व ने अवसोस के साथ कहा कि केवल मंदिर ही नहीं, यहां तो पूरा नगर को नए सिर से सहजाने की जरूरत है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Dr. Suresh Mishra said with regret that not only the temple, the whole town here needs to be preserved anew.
C13-JAGTA-HI-001   p. 18
मैंने 2019 में विदिशा जिले के एक कलेक्टर को इस नगर में जीवित बचे रह गए इतिहास को सहजने की दृष्टि से कुछ सुझाव और निवेदन एक पत्र में लिखे थे। मैंने लिखा था कि यहां यह गुंजाइश है कि काशी विश्वनाथ कॉरिडोर की तर्ज पर अगर कुछ काम हो तो उदयपुर मध्यप्रदेश में राजधानी भोपाल के काफी पास पर्यटन मानचित्र पर चमक सकता है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* In 2019 I had written to a collector of Vidisha district, in a letter, some suggestions and requests with a view to preserving the history surviving in this town. I had written that there is scope here: if some work is done along the lines of the Kashi Vishwanath Corridor, Udaypur in Madhya Pradesh, quite near the capital Bhopal, could shine on the tourism map.
C13-JAGTA-HI-002   p. 20
प्राचीन द्वार-दरवाजों पर कान लगाएं। खंडहरों की सफाई करें। सब तरह का कूड़ा साफ करें।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Put an ear to the ancient gates and doorways. Clean the ruins. Clear all kinds of rubbish.
C13-JAGTA-HI-003   p. 24
मुझे लगता है कि किसी ज्ञापन की प्रतीक्षा के बगैर कलेक्टर को खुद जाकर देखना चाहिए और कानूनी कार्रवाई केवल कागजों पर नहीं करनी चाहिए। उदयपुर के चप्पे-चप्पे के प्राचीन स्मारकों के संरक्षण की कार्ययोजना बनानी चाहिए और अगले तबादले के पहले उसे अमल में लाना चाहिए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* I think the Collector should go and see for himself and not confine legal action to paper. A plan of action for conserving the ancient monuments of every corner of Udaipur should be made and implemented before the next transfer.
C13-JAGTA-HI-004   p. 34
इतना जरूर है कि अगर इन शिलालेखों के सुंदर और साफ-सुथरे अनुवाद मंदिर के परिसर में ही उपलब्ध होते तो न सिर्फ गांव के लोगों की समझ बेहतर बनाने में मददगार होते बल्कि यहां आने वाले पर्यटकों को भी स्थानीय इतिहास की जानकारी के लिहाज से सिद्ध होते। यह अजीब है कि जब ये शिलालेख पढ़ लिए गए हैं तो इन्हें सरकारी तिजोरियों में क्यों बंद करके रखा हुआ है? यह किसकी मंशा थी कि इन्हें बाहर आम लोगों के लिए न दिखाया जाए और अगर किस्मत से ये कहीं बचे रह गए हैं तो इन पर परदा ही पड़ा रहने दिया जाए। किसी लीडर या किसी अफसर के दिमाग में यह बात कभी क्यों नहीं आई कि मंदिरों, महलों और किलों में पाए शिलालेखों के अनुवाद वहीं लगाए जाने चाहिए और इनकी प्रस्तुति भी बदहाल टीन के टुकड़ों पर घिसे-पिटे तरीके से नहीं होनी चाहिए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* At least, had clean and good translations of these inscriptions been available in the temple complex, they would have helped improve the understanding of the villagers and been useful to visiting tourists for local history. It is strange that when these inscriptions have been read, they are kept locked in government vaults. Whose intention was it that they not be shown to the public outside, and, if by luck they survive somewhere, that a veil be left on them? Why did it never occur to some leader or officer that translations of inscriptions found in temples, palaces and forts should be put up right there, and their presentation should not be on worn tin pieces in a stale way.
C13-JAGTA-HI-005   p. 144   also Ch 11, Ch 12
ये तो हमारे आसपास के इतिहास की बेशकीमती इबारतें हैं। इन्हें बहुत सलीके और सम्मान से कीमती धातु या मजबूत पत्थर पर ही अंकित होना चाहिए। उदयपुर में तो ये इतने हैं कि दिलचस्पी रखने वालों को देर तक रोकने के लिए काफी हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* These are priceless writings of the history around us. They should be inscribed with great care and respect on precious metal or strong stone. In Udaypur there are so many that they are enough to hold interested people for a long time.
C13-JAGTA-HI-006   p. 144   also Ch 11, Ch 12
पीके मिश्रा से उनके भोपाल स्थित कार्यालय में मिले तो हम दोनों का यही सुझाव था कि उदयपुर के विशाल मंदिर परिसर में चुनिंदा ऐसे शिलालेखों का हिंदी-अंग्रेजी अनुवाद प्रदर्शित होना चाहिए जो पर्यटकों के लिए नई जानकारी देने वाला हो। अभी लोग आते हैं। दर्शन करते हैं। थोड़ा बहुत घूमते हैं और बिना किसी जानकारी के शून्य में ही चले जाते हैं। इसके अलावा अपने सबसे दक्ष कर्मचारियों को ऐसी जगहों पर तैनात करना चाहिए, जिनकी अपनी दिलचस्पी भी ऐसे स्थानों की चमक बढ़ाने में हो। वे मनरेगा के अकुशल श्रमिकों की तरह न हों।
*Gloss [my translation]:* In March 2021 we both met the ASI regional director Dr. P. K. Mishra at his office in Bhopal, and our suggestion was that in the huge temple complex of Udaypur Hindi-English translations of select inscriptions that give tourists new information should be displayed. Now people come. They have darshan. They stroll a little and leave without any information, into nothingness. Besides, the most skilled employees should be posted at such places, who themselves have an interest in raising the lustre of such places. They should not be like the unskilled workers of MNREGA.
C13-JAGTA-HI-007   p. 150   also Ch 11, Ch 12
यह सिर्फ उदयपुर के राजमहल के खसरा नंबर-822 की बात नहीं है। यह पूरे उदयपुर को नया जीवन देने का विषय है। वरना उदयपुर को अस्तित्व होते देर नहीं। मेरा मानना है कि पूरे जिले के सारे सामाजिक और कारोबारी संगठनों को दिल और जेब खोलकर जुटना चाहिए। हर महीने एक दिन उदयपुर को देना चाहिए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* This is not just about khasra number 822, the Udaipur palace. It is about giving new life to the whole of Udaipur; otherwise Udaipur will soon cease to exist. I believe all social and business organisations of the district should come together with open hearts and pockets, and give Udaipur one day every month.
C13-JAGTA-HI-010   p. 24   also Ch 7
जो संगठन अब जागरूक खड़े हो गए हैं, वे जरा अपने जनप्रतिनिधियों को भी हिलाएं और उनकी एक शोभायात्रा उदयपुर ले जाएं। स्कूल-कॉलेज के दल वहां लगातार पहुंचें। शिक्षण संस्थाएं भी कुछ रचनात्मक अभियान चलाएं। यह काम किसी एक के भरोसे नहीं छोड़ा जा सकता कि अखबार ने छाप दिया और अपना कलेक्टर को ज्ञापन देकर लौट गए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Organisations that are now awake should shake their elected representatives and take a procession to Udaipur. School and college groups should keep arriving; educational institutions should run constructive campaigns. This work cannot be left to one party.
C13-JAGTA-HI-011   p. 34
मैंने सुझाया कि हर हफ्ते उदयपुर की हेरिटेज टीम इन झड़ियों की सफाई करने का काम हाथ में ले। हम केवल ये जटाजूट ही साफ कर दें तो उदयपुर का साफ चेहरा सामने आ सकता है। हमें हर काम सरकार पर छोड़कर नहीं रखना चाहिए। भारत स्वच्छता अभियान का एक स्थानीय चरण यहां शुरू किया जाए, जिसमें कूड़ा साफ नहीं होगा। हम सदियों की जमी हुई धूल हटाएंगे।
*Gloss [my translation]:* I suggested that every week Udaipur's heritage team take up cleaning these thickets. If we only clean these tangles, Udaipur's clear face can emerge. We should not leave everything to the government. A local phase of the Swachhta Abhiyan could begin here, in which rubbish will not be cleaned but centuries of settled dust removed.
C13-JAGTA-HI-012   p. 94
क्या वह सिर्फ़ तराशे गए पत्थरों का एक ऐसा मंदिर भर है, जिससे भारत भरा हुआ है और जो भाग्य से बचा रह गया है और अब हम उसके लिए कुछ नहीं कर सकते, जो करना है सिर्फ़ सरकारों को करना है? क्या ये ऐसे स्मारक हैं, जिनसे हम आम लोगों का कोई लेना-देना नहीं है? मुझे लगता है कि उदयपुर हमारे लिए एक अर्थपूर्ण उद्देश्य है। मेरे लिए अपने आसपास की हर ऐसी धरोहर एक बड़ा प्रश्न है और उत्तर भी है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Is it merely a temple of carved stones, of which India is full, which has survived by luck and for which we can now do nothing, so that only governments must act? Are these monuments with which ordinary people have nothing to do? I think Udaypur is a meaningful purpose for us. For me every such heritage around me is a big question and also an answer.
C13-JAGTA-HI-014   p. 122   also Ch 12
उदयपुर महान भारत की सांस्कृतिक पहचान का प्रतीक है और अगर यह भाग्य से ही सही बचा रह गया है तो इस अमूल्य धरोहर को उसके मूल रूप में सुरक्षित रखने और सहजने का काम किसी और का नहीं है। वह सिर्फ़ उदयपुर की पंचायत के निवासियों का भी नहीं है। वह इस इलाके के हरেক नागरिक का है।
उदयपुर हमारे सृजनशील महान पूर्वजों की एक ऐसी रचना है, जो आज भी उनसे हमारे संबंध का स्मरण कराती है। वे अनगिनत शिलालेख उस संबंध के ही पत्थरों पर उत्कीर्ण दस्तावेज हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Udaypur is a symbol of India's great cultural identity; if it has survived by luck, preserving this priceless heritage in its original form is nobody else's task. It does not belong only to the residents of Udaypur's panchayat; it belongs to every citizen of the area. Udaypur is a creation of our creative ancestors that still reminds us of our bond with them. The countless inscriptions are documents engraved on the stones of that bond.
C13-JAGTA-HI-015   p. 122   also Ch 12
मैं भी ऐसी ही जिज्ञासाएं लेकर गत रविवार यहां पहुंचा तो पाया कि इस विरासत के संरक्षण की दिशा में कई लोग सक्रिय हुए हैं। ग्राम पंचायतें और ग्राम समूह अपने भीतर स्थित महान विरासत को सहजने के प्रति क्यों बेपरवाह रहते हैं, यह कांटा
चुभता है। तिवारी सुखद सूचना देते हैं कि हाल के कोरोना दौर में देवलोक चले जाने वाले डॉ. सुरेश मिश्र की स्मृति में अपने आसपास की धरोहर को संजोने के लिए और इतिहास चेतना जगाने के लिए कुछ ग्रास्रूट काम करने की योजना विचार में ली जा रही है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* When I too reached here last Sunday with such curiosities, I found that many people had become active in the direction of conserving this heritage. The thorn that pricks is why gram panchayats and village clusters remain unconcerned about preserving the great heritage lying within them. Tiwari gives the happy news that, in memory of Dr. Suresh Mishra who passed away in the recent Corona period, a plan to do some grassroots work to preserve the heritage around them and awaken historical consciousness is being considered.
C13-JAGTA-HI-018   p. 161–162   also Ch 12
विदिशा, गंजबासौदा, मंडी बामोरा के श्रेष्ठिगण ही यदि चाहें तो वे स्वयं मालवा-बुंदेलखंड के बीच लावारिस बिखरे इन स्मारकों के संरक्षक बन कर सामने आ सकते हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* If the leading merchants of Vidisha, Ganj Basoda and Mandi Bamora wish, they themselves can come forward as protectors of these unattended scattered monuments between Malwa and Bundelkhand.
C13-JAGTA-HI-019   p. 163
और सचमुच कुछ जागरूक नागरिक सक्रिय हो गये...गंजबासौदा के उत्साही प्रवीण शर्मा, कौस्तुभ शिरहोणकर, कमलेश शर्मा, गगन दुबे, अमित शुक्ला।
उदयपुर के राजा मियाँ।
मंडी बामोरा के रमिस्कान्त श्रीवास्तव...और भी अनेक लोग... इन सबकी पहल पर सबसे पहले तो स्थानीय लोगों ने ही अतिक्रमण हटाने शुरू किए। उसके बाद मन्दिर तक पहुँचने का अवश्रुद्ध मार्ग प्रशस्त करने का बीड़ा उठा लिया।...और देखते ही देखते एक छोटे से कस्बे में जनसहयोग से करीब तीन फुट चौड़ी सीमेंटेड सड़क बन तैयार हो गयी।
*Gloss [my translation]:* And truly some aware citizens became active: the enthusiastic Praveen Sharma, Kaustubh Shirhonkar, Kamlesh Sharma, Gagan Dubey and Amit Shukla of Ganj Basoda; Raja Miyan of Udaypur; Ramiskant Shrivastava of Mandi Bamora and many others. On their initiative first of all the local people themselves began removing the encroachments. After that they took up the task of clearing the blocked path to the temple, and in no time in a small town a cemented road about three feet wide was ready with public cooperation.
C13-JAGTA-HI-020   p. 165   also Ch 12
जब उदयपुर की हेरिटेज वॉक के अनुभव सुनाए तो उपाध्याय जी एक ऐसी भावदशा को उपलब्ध हो गए कि अभी पंख लगें और उदयपुर के राजमहल की छत पर ही जा उतरों। वे उदयपुर की कथा पर केंद्रित हमारा पावर पाइंट प्रेजेंटेशन भोपाल में अगले ही रविवार को कराने के लिए तैयार हो गए। यह इंटैक की ओर से उदयपुर के लिए एक बिल्कुल ही ताजा ऑफर था। तीन दिन बचे थे। मैंने निकटतम संभव तारीख दो मार्च रखने के लिए कहा, ताकि कुछ बेहतर तैयारी हो सके। उसी मीटिंग से श्यामला हिल्स पर जनजातीय संग्रहालय का हॉल बुक हो गया।
*Gloss [my translation]:* When we narrated the experience of the Udaipur heritage walk, Upadhyayji was ready to hold our PowerPoint presentation on the Udaipur story in Bhopal next Sunday. This was a fresh offer from INTACH for Udaipur. I asked for 2 March; from that meeting the hall at the Tribal Museum on Shyamla Hills was booked.
C13-JAGTA-HI-021   p. 76   also Ch 11
विदिशा के जिला मुख्यालय पर जिला पुरातत्व संघ की बैठक दो-चार साल में चाय-कॉफी के साथ अफसरों से हंसी-मजाक का एक आरामदेह सत्र बनकर खत्म होती रही हैं। मुझे पता चला कि विदिशा में संघ की बैठक में पिछली बार वे डेढ़े साल पहले बैठे थे। पता नहीं क्यों बैठे थे और कोई नहीं जानता कि उस बैठक से क्या निकला था?
*Gloss [my translation]:* The meeting of the District Archaeology Association at Vidisha district headquarters has been ending every two-four years as a comfortable session of joking with officers over tea and coffee. I learnt that the association's last meeting in Vidisha was a year and a half ago. Who knows why they sat, and no one knows what came out of that meeting.
C13-JAGTA-HI-022   p. 104
उदयपुर इतिहासकारों और पुरातत्ववेत्ताओं के लिए झुझझोड़ रहा है कि वे किताबों और फाइलों को छोड़कर एक बार आएं और ठहरकर हर कोने को गौर से देखें। कई नई कहानियाँ उसके गर्भ में दबी पड़ी हैं। अनगिनत पन्ने फड़फड़ा रहे हैं। वहां टीलों की सफाई जरूरी है। खंडहरों की खुदाई जरूरी है। हर जगह से इतिहास झांक-झांककर देख रहा है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Udaypur is urging historians and archaeologists to leave books and files, come once and look carefully at every corner. Many new stories lie buried in its womb. Countless pages are fluttering. Cleaning of the mounds is necessary. Excavation of the ruins is necessary.
C13-JAGTA-HI-023   p. 123   also Ch 11
लेकिन इतिहास के मामले में
हमारी दिलचस्पी और फिर यह होनी चाहिए कि स्थानीय लोगों को बहुत सरल
भाषा में कैसे समझाया जाए, हर नई खोज के साथ किताबें कैसे अपडेट की जाएं और
सबसे अहम विश्वविद्यालयों के इतिहास विभागों और संस्कृति और पुरातत्व विभाग
की नौकरियों में ठहरे हुए दिमाग कैसे गतिशील बना जाएं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* But in matters of history our interest should be how to explain things to local people in very simple language, how to update books with every new discovery, and most importantly how to make stagnant minds in the jobs of university history departments and the culture and archaeology department dynamic.
C13-JAGTA-HI-024   p. 135   also Ch 11
इस सघन ग्राउंड वर्क के आधार पर इंटेक की भावी योजना है कि उदयपुर को उभारने के लिए आर्कियोलॉजिकल सर्वे ऑफ इंडिया, राज्य पुरातत्व विभाग, जिला प्रशासन, पंचायत, निजी संस्थाएं, कॉर्पोरेट कंपनियां और स्थानीय जागरूक समाज को एक साथ लाया जाए।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Based on this intensive groundwork, INTACH's future plan is that to raise Udaypur, the Archaeological Survey of India, state archaeology department, district administration, panchayat, private organisations, corporate companies and the aware local society should be brought together.
C13-JAGTA-HI-025   p. 152   also Ch 11
उदयपुर का उद्धार केवल एक प्राचीन मंदिर या बस्ती को ऊपर-ऊपर चमकाना और इस दिखावे से दूसरों को आकर्षित करना भर नहीं है। पर्यटन का प्रसार तो एक भौतिक पहल है, जो अविलंब होना चाहिए। उदयपुर की पुकार हमारी आत्मा को न छू सकी तो ये प्रयास भी सतही ही होंगे और ऐसी पुकारें भारत के गांव-कस्बों और दूरदराज इलाकों में अभी भी जमीन से बाहर आने के लिए कसमसा रही हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Udaypur's revival is not merely polishing an ancient temple or settlement and attracting others with the show. Spreading tourism is a physical initiative that should happen without delay. If Udaypur's call cannot touch our soul, these efforts will also be superficial.
C13-JAGTA-HI-030   p. 123   also Ch 12
इससे दस गुना कम क्षमता के
ऐतिहासिक स्थान केवल शासन की नीतियों के कारण सैलानियों की चहल-पहल
से ज्यादा भरे हुए हैं। उदयपुर अपने-आप में एक मॉडल बन सकता है। ऐसा समझिए
कि उदयपुर में हम इतिहास के खजाने पर बैठे पर हुए हैं।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Historical places with ten times less potential are more full of tourist bustle only because of governance policies. Udaypur can become a model in itself. Understand that in Udaypur we are sitting on a treasure of history.
C13-JAGTA-HI-031   p. 151
सरकार पीपीपी मोड पर उदयपुर जैसे कस्बों को
राजस्थान और दक्षिण भारत के ऐतिहासिक स्थानों की तरह चमका सकती है। यहां इतना कुछ देखने और समझने लायक है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The government can make towns like Udaypur shine in PPP mode like the historical places of Rajasthan and South India. There is so much here to see and understand.
C13-JAGTA-HI-032   p. 152
- राज्य सरकार चाहे तो पर्यटन के क्षेत्र में कुछ नया कर सकती है। यदि ‘परमारकालीन मन्दिरों’ के संरक्षण के लिए उत्तर, मध्य, पूर्व, पश्चिम व दक्षिण क्षेत्र को मिला कर एक पर्यटन परिपथ (Tourism circuit) बनाया जाए तो बड़े पैमाने पर धार्मिक पर्यटन के लिए घरेलु पर्यटक तो आएँगे ही, यदा-कदा आने वाले अध्ययन वृत्ति वाले जिज्ञासु पर्यटकों की संख्या भी बढ़ेगी। यही नहीं, खजुराहो आने वाले विदेशी घुमक्कड़ों को भी यहाँ लाया जा सकेगा।
*Gloss [my translation]:* If the state government wishes it can do something new in tourism. If a tourism circuit is created for the conservation of 'Paramara-period temples', joining the north, central, east, west and south regions, domestic tourists will come for religious tourism on a large scale, and the number of occasional curious scholar-type tourists will also increase. Not only this, foreign travellers coming to Khajuraho can also be brought here.
C13-JAGTA-HI-033   p. 166
- परमारकालीन पर्यटन परिपथ का उदयपुर अध्याय शुरू करने का वक्त अब आ चुका है। भोजपुर पहले ही स्थापित हो चुका है। पब्लिक प्राइवेट पार्टनरशिप की अवधारणा अब नई नहीं है। यह पीपीपी मॉडल के तौर पर जाना जाता है।
- सरकार चाहे तो स्थानीय सहयोग से भी इसे शुरू कर सकती है।
*Gloss [my translation]:* The time has now come to begin the Udaypur chapter of the Paramara-period tourism circuit. Bhojpur has already been established. The concept of public private partnership is no longer new. It is known as the PPP model. The government can start this with local cooperation too.
C13-JAGTA-HI-034   p. 166   also Ch 12
कोई संकल्प धारण करके निकलिए।
अपने महान पुरखों की बची-खुची विरासत को सहजने का संकल्प। आसपास के
लोगों को जागृत करने का संकल्प। सच्चे इतिहास से सामना करने का संकल्प।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Leave with some resolve: a resolve to preserve the remnants of our great ancestors' heritage, to awaken the people around, and to face true history.
C13-JAGTA-HI-035   p. 40
उदयपुर की गलियों को स्कूल-कॉलेज के उन विद्यार्थियों की चहलकदमी का इंतजार है, जो भारत के अतीत की सुरंगों में झांकने का साहस दिखाएं। दादी-नानी की कहानियों से ज्यादा तथ्यपूर्ण कहने को बहुत कुछ है। इन कहानियों में राजाओं के अनेक राजवंशों की कथाएं हैं।
उदयपुर कल्पनाशील साहित्यकारों, लेखकों और कवियों के लिए एक ऐसी उर्वर विषय भूमि है, जिसका कोई हिसाब नहीं। उपन्यास, नाटक, कहानी और गीतों की रचना के लिए अनेक सिरें हैं। उदयपुर उत्सुक है कि कोई आए इन सिरों को थाम ले और कहीं पहुंचकर बताए कि उसकी कल्पनाशीलता उसे पीछे कहां-कहां तक लेकर गई।
*Gloss [my translation]:* Udaypur's lanes await students of schools and colleges daring to peer into the tunnels of India's past. There is much more factual to say than grandmothers' tales. These stories hold the tales of many dynasties of kings. Udaypur is a fertile field for imaginative writers, novelists and poets. There are many threads for novels, plays, stories and songs. Udaypur is eager that someone take these threads and tell how far back his imagination carried him.
C13-JAGTA-HI-036   p. 124   also Ch 12, Ch 9
सुरेश मिश्र ने उस दिन उदयपुर से लौटते हुए कहा था कि पूरा उदयपुर नगर ही हेरिटेज टाउन की तरह विकसित किया जा सकता है। हमारे पुरखे विजय मंदिर नहीं बचा पाए, हम उदयपुर ही बचा लें। तो अब गैर कानूनी गुमटियाँ हटाने का समय है...!”
*Gloss [my translation]:* Historian Dr. Suresh Mishra said that day while returning from Udaypur that the whole of Udaypur town could be developed like a heritage town. Our ancestors could not save the Vijay temple; let us save Udaypur. So now it is time to remove the illegal kiosks.
C13-JAGTA-HI-037   p. 165; also at p. 34
उदयपुर इतिहासप्रेमियों और पर्यटकों की चहल-पहल से आबाद एक विकसित हैरिटेज टाउन बने, एक यही उनका सपना था, जो वे हमें सौंपकर गए हैं। एक इतिहासकार का आखिरी सपना...
*Gloss [my translation]:* That Udaypur become a developed heritage town, bustling with history lovers and tourists, this alone was his dream, which he has handed over to us. A historian's last dream...

*Gloss [my translation]:* Udaypur is now a resolve for all of us. A resolve of awakening. A resolve to do something. A resolve to connect with our own history.

## Jagta Hua Kasba / कथा उदयपुर — English translation (JAGTA-EN, 23)
In 2019 I had written some suggestions and requests in a letter to a Collector of Vidisha district, with a view to looking after the history that had survived alive in this town. I had written that here there is scope such that, if some work were done on the model of the Kashi Vishwanath corridor, Udaipur — quite close to the capital Bhopal — could shine on the tourism map of Madhya Pradesh.
C13-JAGTA-EN-001   ≈ p. 20 (Hindi original)   also Ch 12
Why has the translation, in pure Hindi, of the Sanskrit-origin inscriptions not been displayed in the vast precinct of the temple? What expertise does this work require? Why did it not come into the mind of any officer or caretaker that this would prove helpful in increasing the knowledge of the devotees and tourists who come every day?
C13-JAGTA-EN-002   ≈ p. 72 (Hindi original)   also Ch 6
To break our carefreeness, our coming here again and again should have been made compulsory. For every school and college it should have been made obligatory that they continually bring every child of theirs here, and that the asking of questions be made mandatory. Let their questions be answered, and let the history here be told. History means not a half-and-incomplete truth, but the full and real history. What was there first, and then what happened — its full details, with documentary evidence, should be told to them.
C13-JAGTA-EN-003   ≈ p. 104 (Hindi original)
In a declared, memorable heritage walk every Sunday, some printed material too could be prepared to lure the tourists. In this very cheap and low-budget work, the Centre, the state government, the district archaeology association, or the local panchayat could just lend a hand.
C13-JAGTA-EN-004   ≈ p. 104 (Hindi original)
There, the cleaning of the mounds is necessary. The excavation of the ruins is necessary.
C13-JAGTA-EN-005   ≈ p. 123 (Hindi original); also at ≈ p. 123 (Hindi original)
There, the translations of all those inscriptions, in Hindi and English, ought to have been mounted in letters so large and clear that even a sightless person could all but make them out from afar — [showing] what our ancestors have written and left for us in them. Better still, they ought to have been carved, in Hindi and English, on that same stone dug from Udaipur's quarries out of which these temples were built.
C13-JAGTA-EN-006   ≈ p. 130 (Hindi original)
This much is certain: that if beautiful, clean translations of these inscriptions were available in the temple precinct itself, they would not only help improve the understanding of the village people, but would also prove useful to the tourists coming here, in terms of the knowledge of local history. It is strange that, when these inscriptions have [already] been read, they should be kept locked away in government vaults.
C13-JAGTA-EN-007   ≈ p. 144 (Hindi original)
Mishra, in March 2021 at his Bhopal office, the suggestion of us both was this: that in Udaipur's vast temple precinct, the Hindi–English translation of selected inscriptions ought to be displayed — ones that would give new information to tourists. As it is, people come; they take *darshan*; they wander about a little; and, without any information, they simply drift off into the void.
C13-JAGTA-EN-008   ≈ p. 149 (Hindi original)   also Ch 11
On the basis of the Udaipur experience, he says that if the state government were to have an extensive record-making (documentation) of the panchayats done, it would be a big initiative. "We are doing this work in 10–12 districts. If the government has this work done in all the panchayats of the state, then it is certain that, for the very first time, many priceless things will come into our knowledge all at once.
C13-JAGTA-EN-009   ≈ p. 152 (Hindi original)
We have also suggested to the government that heritage walks ought to be made compulsory in schools and colleges at the state and district level. College and university students should go and see the great heritage present throughout the country. Students of the higher school classes should be taken to such places of their own state, and to the heritage at the district or block level students of the lower classes should be taken at least twice a year. For this, the Tourism Department can develop easy tourist circuits, in which a route of visiting two or three monuments in a single day is laid out.
C13-JAGTA-EN-010   ≈ p. 158 (Hindi original)
Udaipur is but a waiting — a long, endless sort of wait. Udaipur's lanes await the footfalls of those school-and-college students who show the courage to peer into the tunnels of India's past.
C13-JAGTA-EN-011   ≈ p. 123 (Hindi original)
For this, top-class publications — both brief and detailed — should be produced on local history and archaeology, to be kept in the schools and colleges themselves. Short films should be made and circulated in the digital world. In writing and speech competitions among children, our heritage should be made compulsory as a central theme. Through this, they will grow eager to read their history, and to go and see it.
C13-JAGTA-EN-012   ≈ p. 159 (Hindi original)
Tiwari gives the happy news that, in memory of Dr. Suresh Mishra — who departed to the abode of the gods in the recent corona period — a plan is under consideration to do some grassroots work for cherishing the heritage around us and for awakening a consciousness of history.
C13-JAGTA-EN-013   ≈ p. 161 (Hindi original)
When the experiences of the Udaipur heritage walk were narrated, Upadhyay-ji arrived at such an emotional state that [he wished] wings would sprout right now and he would go and land on the very roof of Udaipur's palace. He got ready to hold, in Bhopal the very next Sunday, our PowerPoint presentation centred on the story of Udaipur. This was a completely fresh offer for Udaipur from INTACH.
C13-JAGTA-EN-014   ≈ p. 75 (Hindi original)
Madanmohan Upadhyay gave a small introduction, on behalf of INTACH, about the interest awakened toward Udaipur.
C13-JAGTA-EN-015   ≈ p. 77 (Hindi original)
Besides this, one's most skilled employees ought to be posted at such places — those who have their own interest in enhancing the lustre of such sites. They should not be like the unskilled labourers of MGNREGA.
C13-JAGTA-EN-016   ≈ p. 149 (Hindi original)
On the basis of this intensive ground work, INTACH's future plan is that, to raise Udaipur into prominence, the Archaeological Survey of India, the State Archaeology Department, the district administration, the panchayat, private institutions, corporate companies, and the aware local society be brought together.
C13-JAGTA-EN-017   ≈ p. 151 (Hindi original)
Upadhyay draws attention to the fact that in Madhya Pradesh there is a famine of experts who can read inscriptions. In government departments and educational institutions too there are no people who can read new inscriptions.
C13-JAGTA-EN-018   ≈ p. 152 (Hindi original)
The redemption of Udaipur is not merely the superficial polishing of an ancient temple or settlement and, by this show, the attracting of others. The spread of tourism is a material initiative, which ought to happen without delay. But if Udaipur's call could not touch our soul, then these efforts too would remain superficial — and such calls are, even now, writhing to come out from the ground in India's villages, towns and far-flung areas.
C13-JAGTA-EN-019   ≈ p. 122 (Hindi original)
- The state government, if it wishes, can do something new in the field of tourism. If, for the conservation of the 'Paramara-era temples,' a tourism circuit were made by combining the northern, central, eastern, western and southern regions, then, for large-scale religious tourism, domestic tourists would surely come, and the number of the occasional, study-minded, curious tourists too would increase. Not only that: the foreign wanderers who come to Khajuraho could also be brought here.
- The time has now come to begin the Udaipur chapter of the Paramara-era tourism circuit.
C13-JAGTA-EN-020   ≈ p. 166 (Hindi original)
- The time has now come to begin the Udaipur chapter of the Paramara-era tourism circuit. Bhojpur has already been established. The concept of Public–Private Partnership is now no longer new; it is known as the PPP model.
- The government, if it wishes, can begin this with local cooperation too.
C13-JAGTA-EN-021   ≈ p. 166 (Hindi original)
Dr. Suresh Mishra had said that day, returning from Udaipur, that the whole town of Udaipur can be developed like a heritage town. Our ancestors could not save the Vijay Mandir; let us at least save Udaipur.
C13-JAGTA-EN-022   ≈ p. 35 (Hindi original)
Suresh Mishra had said that day, while returning from Udaipur, that the whole town of Udaipur itself could be developed like a heritage town. Our ancestors could not save the Vijay Mandir; let us at least save Udaipur. So now is the time to remove the illegal kiosks (*gumatis*)…!"
C13-JAGTA-EN-023   ≈ p. 165 (Hindi original)
## Geo-Heritage of Udaypur, Vidisha District, Madhya Pradesh: The Third Eye (GEOHER, 5)
A survey of the entire fort to produce an engineering-geological map that identifies priority areas of risk.
Priority areas of risk.
The installation of a monitoring system to gauge the movement of stone and soil.
C13-GEOHER-001   p. 103
Indian National Trust for Arts and Cultural Heritage (INTACH), Bhopal chapter INTACH's role:
INTACH can be a fascinating partner by way of developing guides from the villages and properly educating them, giving them literature, and defining their roles.
Cultural activities in the village can also help develop plans. How the villagers can help conservation and sustenance of the geo heritage by not disturbing it further.
C13-GEOHER-002   p. 142
The District Collector can be the overall team leader to coordinate with all agencies for the development of this unique geo-heritage park.
Bhopal chapter will pilot the entire project from preparation to conceptualization and to implementation. The operational part of the geo-conservation plan is a more elaborate and extensive document that defines the role and responsibilities of all stakeholders.
C13-GEOHER-003   p. 143
Located inside a protected forest, there are many stakeholders, like:
• Forest Department,
• Archaeology department,
• Tourism department,
• Panchayat and rural development,
• Geological Survey of India and
C13-GEOHER-004   p. 153
Development of a space for parking: We also need a small space for parking, which can be developed between the Natraj sculpture and the 1400-meter-long fortification wall. Since full development of the site is going to take some time and we do not expect it to happen immediately. The tourists will not start coming in from day one. The site will be developed over a period. There are plenty of government open lands available by the side of the fortification wall. We will fix proper signage, apart from an interpretation board at the entrance, highlighting the relevance of this site.
C13-GEOHER-005   p. 150
